# ClawCode memory system audit (2026-10-25)

Scope: every memory subsystem in `src/memory`, `src/documents`, `src/document-ingest` and the memory-related parts of `src/manager`, plus four end-to-end traces, a test run and a research sweep of agent memory systems. Produced by 23 reader, trace, test and research agents with two completeness critics; every issue cites file and line evidence. Companion plan: `plan-2026-10-25.md`.

## Headline findings

I checked every candidate against the code at /Users/jasjagpal/Development/projects/clawcode-platform without editing anything; I also did not write to the vault in this step. The audits missed several memory paths: the client fact vault (src/manager/client-vault.ts), delegated-research consolidation (src/discord/subagent-consolidation.ts), derived sessions opening a second store on the parent's DB, workflow run.json memory, and inline turn-vision captions that are never persisted. No auditor looked at live production data or ran a recall eval with production-shaped inputs, and the SessionManager lifecycle tests did not run. I resolved 13 contradictions by reading the code. The resolutions that change the picture: the dedup knob is inert but dedup is always on at 0.85; system-prompt directives are never injected; createdAt never reaches the footer or the model; context-summary.md is effectively never written; the effective weeklyThreshold is 7 for this clawcode.yaml; the scanner never backfills and is removed by any per-agent restart; the brief gap skip fires in the common unclean-restart and crash cases; mid-session rows are read and crowd explicit search; subagent output is persisted, but only into the delegate's store. The three biggest threats to recall are: the Discord envelope used as the retrieval query and stored with every turn; path and document weights that swamp RRF; and the scanner going stale after restarts. Next come restart continuity, the mid-session flood, envelope-starved summaries, KNN-then-window, 200-word embeddings, lost disk flushes, destructive dedup, Discord-only capture, upload ingest that is off by default and lossy when on, weak explicit tools, and consolidation that never runs for this config. Overbroad audit claims were narrowed or dropped as noted in the contradictions.

### Top issues (critic-verified, ranked by effect on recall)

1. **The Discord envelope is both the retrieval query and the stored user turn, so keyword recall never sees the question** (retrieval (fact-lifecycle and document-lifecycle traces)). Verified. formatDiscordMessage wraps every message in <channel source chat_id message_id user ts> (bridge.ts:8848-8867). turnText goes to dispatchStream (bridge.ts:5475), and augmentWithMemoryContext uses message.trim() as the query (turn-dispatcher.ts:1244); getMemoryRetrieverForAgent strips nothing. extractFtsTerms keeps the first 12 non-stopword tokens (fts-query.ts:21, 38-51), and all 12 are envelope tokens, so neither keyword ranker ever sees the user's words. The vector query and the topic-shift cosine are diluted the same way. Captured user turns (bridge.ts:6242 userContent: formattedMessage) carry the envelope into daily-log chunks, summaries and flushes. This hits every Discord turn on every agent.
2. **Vault (+0.2), procedures (+0.1) and document (+0.2, then priority-multiplied) weights are about 6x the maximum RRF score** (retrieval (+ documents)). Verified. rrfFuse gives at most 1/61 per ranker (about 0.033 for both); memory-retrieval.ts adds meta.score_weight to chunk scores, and memories get 0. Weights come from memory-chunks.ts:150-154 and cross-ingest.ts:224. Any vault or document chunk anywhere in the KNN top 20 outranks every saved memory and decides which 20 candidates enter the reranker window. When the 500ms reranker falls back, these weights decide the final order outright. The comment at memory-chunks.ts:147-148 claims they only nudge.
3. **Memory scanner never backfills and is permanently removed by any per-agent restart** (capture (fact-lifecycle trace; contradicts capture audit)). Verified. chokidar runs with ignoreInitial:true (memory-scanner.ts:107) and production never calls backfill (only the CLI does). The lazy store proxy turns events that arrive before initMemory into no-ops (daemon.ts ~5473-5485). stopAgentCore deletes the scanner (session-manager.ts ~2485), and only daemon boot creates one (daemon.ts:5516). After restartAgent, the daily logs, flush files, cue files and digests written afterwards are never indexed until a daemon restart, and files written while the scanner or store was down stay invisible. The whole file-chunk side of recall silently goes stale.
4. **After the most common restarts the agent has neither its transcript nor the Recent Sessions brief** (conversation + restart-and-compaction trace). Verified. closeStaleActiveSessions sets ended_at=now just before buildSessionConfig (session-manager.ts:1303, 1320). listRecentTerminatedSessions counts only sessions that still have turns, and conversation-brief.ts:150-170 skips the entire brief when that session ended less than 4 hours ago. Two common cases leave turns in place: unfinished stops (stopAll runs serially, each stopAgent awaits a Haiku summary of up to 120s, inside a 10s step and 20s watchdog; shutdown-watchdog.ts:25-28, session-manager.ts:2980-2986) and crash restarts (the summary is fire-and-forget and the restart follows in about 2s). In both, the gap is about 0 and no brief is built. Stop and crash also null the registry sessionId (session-manager.ts:2499-2504, session-recovery.ts:201), so the transcript is not resumed. Session summaries are also excluded from per-turn retrieval by default.
5. **Mid-session flush writes a whole-session summary row every 15 minutes, crowding both automatic and explicit recall** (storage / capture / conversation). Verified. The timer fires for any active session with at least one turn (session-manager.ts:3986-4002). flushSessionMidway re-reads all turns with no delta check (session-summarizer.ts:524) and inserts with skipDedup (:620), and nothing ever prunes these rows. They are dropped by excludeTags only after the memories KNN top 20 (memory-retrieval.ts:419-426, 540-551), so they take candidate slots that memory_save and compaction rows need. clawcode_memory_search has no default excludeTags (daemon.ts:18600), so they crowd explicit recall too. That is up to about 96 Haiku calls and 96 rows per agent per day, even when the agent is idle.
6. **Session summaries and flushes contain almost no user words in long or very short sessions** (capture (fact-lifecycle trace)). Verified. Once the transcript exceeds 30K chars, buildSessionSummarizationPrompt cuts each turn to floor(30000/N) chars (session-summarizer.ts:66-84). A ConversationStore session spans the agent's whole uptime, and every stored user turn starts with a roughly 130-char envelope, so past about 200 turns each user turn contributes only envelope. Sessions under 3 turns (a single exchange) get buildShortSessionSummary, whose 80-char cut keeps only the envelope (session-summarizer.ts:147-157). The brief, the mid-session flushes and memory_lookup's summary path all depend on these summaries.
7. **14-day window is applied after a fixed top-20 fetch, so recall of recent notes gets worse over time** (retrieval). Verified. searchMemoryChunksVec and searchMemoryChunksFts fetch k=20 over all chunks with no mtime predicate (store.ts:2551-2599), and applyTimeWindowFilter runs only afterwards in retrieveMemoryChunks. Every daily-log turn becomes its own chunk, so out-of-window chunks fill the 20 slots and relevant in-window chunks at rank 21 and below are never fetched.
8. **Embeddings cover only the first 200 words of chunks that hold up to about 750 (memory) or 375 (document) words** (retrieval (+ documents)). Verified. MAX_WORDS=200 in embedder.ts:51 and embedder-bge-small.ts:55. Memory chunks run up to 800 tokens soft and 1000 hard (memory-chunks.ts:21-24); document chunks are 500 tokens x 0.75, about 375 words (chunker.ts). Facts in the back half of a section, or an invoice total late in a chunk, are invisible to vector search, and the reranker also truncates.
9. **Disk flush (MEM-04) silently skips windows and is not started on the resume path** (capture). Verified. MemoryFlushTimer advances lastFlushAt on both skip and empty summary (memory-flush.ts ~218-256). summarizeWithHaiku turns any failure, including the 10s abort (memory-flush.ts ~239), into an empty string (summarize-with-haiku.ts:72-87), although that summarizer path is documented at 30-64s. reconcileRegistry starts only the DB flush timer (session-manager.ts:3138); the disk timer starts only in startAgentCore (:1877). The retrievable flush files for those windows never exist.
10. **Dedup at a fixed 0.85 replaces older content and unions tags across all rows** (storage / dream-output trace). Verified. Dedup is always on (session-memory.ts:71 passes no config). checkForDuplicate runs a k=1 match against all of vec_memories (dedup.ts:42-68); mergeMemory then UPDATEs the content, unions tags, bumps access_count and deletes the v2 vector. This applies to memory_save, compaction facts, consolidation digests (consolidation.ts:384, no skipDedup) and delegated-research records. A fact that merges into a summary row inherits the session-summary tag and disappears from pre-turn retrieval, and the previous text is lost with no history. The dream-output trace reproduced this.
11. **Only Discord exchanges are ever recorded as conversation turns** (capture / conversation). Verified. captureDiscordExchange is the only caller of ConversationStore.recordTurn (capture.ts:87, 99; bridge.ts:2817, 6239, 8223). Scheduled, OpenAI-endpoint, heartbeat, cross-agent and workflow turns pass through TurnDispatcher but are never recorded, and recorded assistant content is final text only, with no tool results. That work produces no daily log, flush, summary or consolidation input, so the agent cannot recall it.
12. **Uploaded files are silently not indexed unless autoIngestAttachments is set** (documents (document-lifecycle trace)). Verified. The zod default is false (config/schema.ts:3476). The handler returns skipped with only an info log (auto-ingest-handler.ts:226-250), and there is no Discord receipt or note to the agent. In this repo's clawcode.yaml only servicedesk enables it (line 311), so three of four agents never index uploads. Afterwards the only trace is the file path in the raw daily-log turn.
13. **When uploads are ingested, content is partly lost or stored as garbage** (documents (document-lifecycle trace)). Verified. detect.ts:39-41 types any PDF with any text layer as text-pdf, and handleTextPdf never OCRs image-only pages. When OCR and vision both yield nothing, auto-ingest-handler.ts:432-437 falls back to chunkText(fileBuffer.toString('utf-8')), so raw PNG bytes are embedded and reported as success. tesseract-cli reports 0.75 confidence for any non-empty text, so Claude-vision OCR never replaces garbage OCR output. The upload looks ingested but its content is missing or wrong.
14. **Explicit recall paths are weak and point the agent at the wrong tool** (tools / conversation / injection). Verified. memory_lookup is the only memory tool pinned with alwaysLoad (server.ts:1564), yet it never searches memory_chunks: it uses GraphSearch over memories or searchByScope. Its conversation FTS phrase-quotes the whole query (conversation-store.ts:46-51), and its memory and summary paths do whole-string substring matching over the 200 newest rows (conversation-search.ts:108-125, 312-315). The hybrid clawcode_memory_search is deferred behind ToolSearch (toolSearch: auto on all four agents) and has no default excludeTags. The operator directive telling agents to search before saying 'I don't know' is never injected. Everything older than the 14-day window depends on these paths.
15. **Consolidation threshold is effectively 7 here, so dated conversation leaves automatic recall after 14 days** (dreaming/consolidation (fact-lifecycle trace; resolves auditor contradiction)). Verified. clawcode.yaml has a defaults.memory block without consolidation, so zod applies the memory/schema.ts default weeklyThreshold of 7. Files are grouped per ISO week (at most 7), so an agent that does not log every day of the week never produces a weekly digest or archives its logs. Digests are the only window-exempt record of dated conversation, so once a daily log passes 14 days its content is reachable only through the weak explicit tools.

### Test run

Memory test suites are nearly green: 1138 tests passed, 3 failed, and 1 suite did not run. Two failures in src/memory/__tests__/conversation-brief.test.ts are a stale fixture (listRecentTerminatedSessions now requires turn rows). One failure in src/manager/__tests__/session-config.test.ts is a stale assertion (identity over-budget now head-tail truncates and reports strategy 'drop-lowest-importance'). The session-manager.test.ts suite did not run because its embedding model warmup hook timed out at 60s, which looks environmental. No type errors were found in memory-related paths, and no files were modified. No command hit the 10 minute cap. I did not run the session-manager suite separately to confirm the model cache cause.

| Suite | Passed | Failed | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `npx vitest run src/memory --reporter=dot` | 842 | 2 | 70 files, 69 pass; only conversation-brief.test.ts fails |
| `npx vitest run src/documents src/document-ingest --reporter=dot` | 63 | 0 | 8 files all pass |
| `npx vitest run src/manager/__tests__ -t "memory|dream|consolidat|diges` | 233 | 1 | 348 files; 1 failed test plus 1 failed suite (session-manager.test.ts beforeAll hook timeout, so its tests did not run). Note: macOS has no `timeout` binary, so the 10-minute cap was applied via the B |
| `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json | grep memory/documents/document-ing` | 0 | 0 | No type errors reported in memory-related paths |

- `src/memory/__tests__/conversation-brief.test.ts` :: assembleConversationBrief > skips when gap under threshold (stale-test): Test seeds an ended session 2h ago via seedEndedSession with no conversation_turns rows. conversation-store.ts listRecentTerminatedSessions now has an EXISTS(conversation_turns) filter (Phase 99 sub-scope D hotfix, 2026-04-25), so the empty session is ignored, no gap check fires, and result.skipped is false. Fixture needs to insert at least one turn per seeded ended session. Production code is behaving as designed.
- `src/memory/__tests__/conversation-brief.test.ts` :: assembleConversationBrief > regression: agents-forget-across-sessions (production ordering) > still gap-skips when the prior terminated session is within threshold (stale-test): Same root cause: seeded terminated session has no turn rows and is filtered out by the new EXISTS clause in listRecentTerminatedSessions. Fix is in the seed helper (add a turn), not in conversation-brief.ts. The other 'injects when gap over threshold' tests pass only because they expect no gap skip.
- `src/manager/__tests__/session-config.test.ts` :: resume summary budget (Phase 53) > Test 10: memoryAssemblyBudgets threaded through to assembler + warn fires for over-budget (stale-test): Test expects warn strategy 'warn-and-keep' for over-budget identity. context-assembler.ts (~line 1238-1262, Phase 115 D-03) deliberately replaced warn-and-keep with head-tail truncation and now emits strategy 'drop-lowest-importance'; the code comment says the contract change is BREAKING and tests were to be updated atomically. This test was missed. Minor code smell: the emitted strategy label 'drop-lowest-importance' is arguably misleading for head-tail truncation, but warn still fires with section 'identity'.
- `src/manager/__tests__/session-manager.test.ts` :: SessionManager suite (file-level beforeAll) (environment): beforeAll at line 650 calls new EmbeddingService().warmup() with a 60s timeout and timed out ('Hook timed out in 60000ms'). The ONNX embedding model (@huggingface/transformers) is likely not cached locally or needs a network download, so the file's tests did not run. Not confirmed as a code bug; rerun with network or a cached model, or check whether the model cache dir is populated.

## End-to-end traces

### fact-lifecycle

**Verdict.** The Monday sentence is stored, but almost never in a form that is reliably retrievable on Friday. What is certain: the heartbeat session-log-flush writes the verbatim envelope-wrapped turn into memory/2026-10-26.md within a minute, the scanner turns it into two memory_chunks rows (no importance, tier or decay), and Gap-3 plus the session summary add LLM paraphrases that may or may not mention it. The cue path does not fire. Whether anything is saved explicitly is up to the model, and the only instruction it has points at the shared vault MEMORY.md, not memory_save. Friday: the fact can reach the prompt only as a raw daily-log chunk in the per-turn <memory-context> block on the user message (not the system prompt), and only if (1) this is a refresh turn (first after restart, every 3rd, or topic shift; compaction does not reset the gate), (2) the chunk survives KNN-20 and reranking with a query that is mostly Discord envelope (the FTS half is entirely envelope tokens), (3) no larger or vault-weighted chunk ahead of it trips the break-on-overflow budget, and it then renders as '### 17:14:22 [user]' with no date. Session-summary, mid-session and raw-fallback rows are excluded from that block by design, the Recent Sessions brief is skipped on any restart under 4 hours and is not re-sent after native /compact, and stop-path summaries/final flushes are cut by a 10s/20s shutdown budget. For the web agent only, the SOUL fingerprint also carries a truncated version of the rule (executed). Six weeks out: the daily log is out of the 14-day pre-turn window, never consolidated (weeklyThreshold 7 needs all 7 days), memory rows are still warm (cold needs about 112 to 119 idle days) but can never become hot, dream is off, so only an agent-appended line in the vault MEMORY.md or an explicit lazy search would still surface it. Overall: the capture side works, the retrieval side has multiple independent breaks, the most consequential being the envelope contaminating the query and every stored turn, KNN-then-filter with the 15-minute mid-session flood, the 4-hour brief gap rule, and the shutdown budget. Not verified because it needs a live daemon or the embedding model: real cosine values, Haiku's actual summaries, real shutdown timing. No files were edited, so the vault note clawcode-memory-architecture-and-gaps was not updated; it should gain these findings (it currently describes consolidation as '7+ daily logs' without the per-ISO-week limitation and does not mention the envelope/FTS issue).

**Breaks.**

- [high] Retrieval query construction: bridge.ts:8848-8876, bridge.ts:5475, turn-dispatcher.ts:1238-1299, fts-query.ts:21-51: The pre-turn query is the whole Discord <channel ...> envelope plus the question. The FTS keyword ranker keeps only the first 12 terms, all of which are envelope tokens, so the real question words never reach BM25. The vector query and every stored user-turn chunk are also envelope-dominated, so similarity is diluted and BM25 ranks user-turn chunks by envelope overlap rather than meaning.
  Evidence: Executed extractFtsTerms on a realistic Friday message: [channel, source, discord, chat_id, 000000000000000001, message_id, 1433000000000000000, user, jas, ts, 2026, 10]. Without the envelope: [safe, run, migration, staging]. Fake-store run confirmed searchMemoryChunksFts received the full envelope string. Embedding-level dilution is inference (no model offline).
- [medium] Per-turn retrieval cadence: memory-context-augmenter.ts:96-126, turn-dispatcher.ts:1238-1262: Retrieval runs only on the first turn after process start, every 3rd turn, or on topic shift (cosine<0.75 between envelope-wrapped queries). The gate is in-memory per agent and has no reset on compaction, so a turn right after compaction can get no block even though the earlier block was summarized away. Topic-shift detection is probably muted because all Discord queries share a long identical envelope (inference).
  Evidence: RetrievalCadenceGate has no reset method; grep shows it is only used via shouldRefresh. Restart resets it (new TurnDispatcher), compaction does not.
- [high] Memory-side KNN then tag filter: store.ts:2636-2680, memory-retrieval.ts:540-551, session-manager.ts:3987-4010 (Gap-3 timer), session-summarizer.ts:491-661: Rows tagged session-summary/mid-session/raw-fallback are excluded only after the top-20 vector KNN. The Gap-3 flush inserts a NEW whole-session summary row every 15 minutes per active session with no idle or delta check and skipDedup:true, so Monday to Friday can leave hundreds of near-identical topical rows that fill the 20 KNN slots, then get dropped, pushing memory_save and compaction rows out of the candidate set. Also a Haiku call per agent per 15 minutes forever.
  Evidence: Timer callback only checks activeConversationSessionId and draining; flushSessionMidway only skips on not-active or zero turns; insert uses skipDedup:true. searchMemoriesVec uses k=limit with no tag predicate. Effect size depends on session length (mechanism confirmed by reading; not measured).
- [medium] Result rendering: memory-context-augmenter.ts:129-140, memory-retrieval.ts:682-691, session-log.ts extractTime: The block shows heading instead of path and carries no date: a Monday daily-log chunk renders as '### 17:14:22 [user]' (UTC time, while the envelope inside says PDT), flush chunks as '### Decisions'. The agent cannot tell a 4-day-old or 6-week-old statement from a fresh one. createdAt is computed for memory rows but dropped when results are built, so the recall footer date range is always empty.
  Evidence: Executed renderMemoryContext on daily-log chunks and a fake-store retrieval: createdAt undefined, recallDateRange returned an empty string.
- [medium] Ranking and budget: memory-chunks.ts:150-155, memory-retrieval.ts:678-694: Path weights (+0.2 vault, +0.1 procedures, -0.2 archive) are 6x the maximum RRF score (0.0328), so any vault chunk beats any relevant dated chunk and archived logs are effectively unreachable. The 6000-char budget loop BREAKS at the first overflowing chunk instead of skipping it, so one large higher-ranked chunk evicts smaller relevant ones behind it.
  Evidence: Executed: irrelevant vault chunk 0.216 > Monday chunk 0.033; a 5900-char top-ranked chunk returned alone and the Monday chunk was dropped. The comment at memory-chunks.ts:147-148 claims the weights only nudge.
- [high] Session-resume brief: conversation-brief.ts:150-170, session-config.ts:1109-1190, persistent-session-handle.ts:914-934 and :3283, daemon-compact-session-ipc.ts:28-31: On any restart where the previous session ended less than 4 hours earlier (i.e. every normal stop-then-start) the Recent Sessions brief is skipped, so the Monday summary never enters the prompt. When it is injected it is prepended to the first user message only and is re-armed only by handle.swap(), which native /compact no longer uses, so the compaction removes it for good.
  Evidence: Executed assembleConversationBrief with fakes: ended 40s ago -> skipped(gap); 3h59m -> skipped; 4h01m -> injected. continuityConsumed reset appears only at persistent-session-handle.ts:3283 (swap path).
- [high] Shutdown path: daemon.ts:14013 and :14323, shutdown-watchdog.ts:25 and :28, session-manager.ts:2418-2446, session-summarizer.ts:36-47, bridge-spawn-governor.ts:40-41: stopAgent does a final disk flush (10s cap) and then an awaited Haiku session summary (child ceiling 60s, measured 30-64s) for each of 4 agents serially, but the daemon gives manager.stopAll a 10s step after a 15s drain and force-exits at 20s total. Stop-path summaries and final flushes are routinely cut off, leaving sessions ended/crashed with no summary until the next boot's summarize-pending tick; later agents in the loop may never reach endSession and stay 'running' in the registry, which flips them onto the SDK-resume branch.
  Evidence: Constants and call order read directly. Practical latency is inferred from the code comment at session-summarizer.ts:36-46 and the 60s summarizer child ceiling; not observed.
- [high] Extraction prompts: session-summarizer.ts:86-104, memory-flush.ts:44-45, compact-extractors/tier2-prompt.ts:22-35 and :49-52: Every LLM extractor asks for categories an environment-topology fact does not fit: summary = preferences/decisions/open threads/commitments; MEM-04 = decisions/tasks/standing rules; compaction Tier 2 = activeClients/decisions/standingRulesChanged/inFlightTasks/drivePathsTouched/criticalNumbers. Tier 2 also only sees the last 6000 chars, so a Monday turn is outside its window after four days. The statement is likely to be dropped or reworded; it survives only verbatim paths.
  Evidence: Prompts read verbatim. Outcome is model-dependent, so likelihood is inferred.
- [medium] Cue detection: memory-cue.ts:44-45 and :74-87, turn-dispatcher.ts:1411-1424: The sentence has no trigger word, so the explicit 'remember this' path never fires. Even when it does, the written 'Standing note' body is the entire <channel> envelope (no sentence boundary found) with no date in the rendered heading.
  Evidence: Executed detectCue on the raw and wrapped sentence -> match false; with 'remember:' prefix the extracted context was the full envelope.
- [medium] Short sessions: session-summarizer.ts:147-157 and :246: Sessions with fewer than 3 turns skip Haiku and get a deterministic summary that truncates the last user message to 80 characters. Because the stored user turn starts with a ~150 char envelope, the recorded text contains no user words at all.
  Evidence: Executed buildShortSessionSummary: Last user: "<\channel source=\"discord\" chat_id=\"000000000000000001\" message_id=\"1432198765432...\" ... (sentence absent).
- [high] Agent-explicit save path: capability-manifest.ts:281-285, memory-roots.ts:57-76, clawcode-memory-edit.ts:139-152, session-config.ts:708-763, context-assembler.ts:846-955, memory-scanner.ts:49-51: The only memory instruction given to agents is to record facts with clawcode_memory_edit on MEMORY.md; with memoryAutoLoadPath that is the shared vault index. Writes are read-modify-write with no atomic rename, lock, size cap or dedup across 4 agents (lost updates), the whole file is returned to the model on every append, the file is not scanned or embedded (invisible to clawcode_memory_search), and it only reaches the prompt at the next session start. When it exceeds the identity budget MEMORY.md is the first thing halved from the middle, and the truncation marker promises a dream pass that is disabled.
  Evidence: Code read; defaults.dream not set in clawcode.yaml so dream.enabled is false. Existing vault note clawcode-memory-architecture-and-gaps already flags the shared-index hazard.
- [medium] memory_save and dedup: mcp/server.ts:1675, daemon.ts:18529-18567, store.ts:290-291 and :349-351, dedup.ts:105-131: memory_save defaults to importance 0.7 and no tags; an explicit importance of exactly 0.5 is silently replaced by the heuristic. Dedup (cosine>=0.85 against ANY row, including summaries and digests) merges by REPLACING the existing row's content with the new text, so a new fact can overwrite an unrelated nearby row, and envelope-dominated Tier-4 compaction facts are especially likely to collide (inference).
  Evidence: mergeMemory UPDATE sets content=input.content; checkForDuplicate runs k=1 over all of vec_memories. Collision likelihood is inferred.
- [medium] MEM-04 flush wiring: session-manager.ts:1877 vs :3138, session-manager.ts:1953-1975, memory-flush.ts:239 and :249-256: (1) startMemoryFileFlushTimer is only called from startAgentCore; the reconcileRegistry resume path starts only the Gap-3 timer, so resumed agents get no disk flush and no final flush. (2) The flush has a 10s abort while the shared Haiku bridge pool allows 3 concurrent children and 4 agents x 2 flush timers tend to fire together, so aborts are plausible; summarizeWithHaiku turns an abort into '' and the timer then advances lastFlushAt, permanently skipping that window with only a debug log.
  Evidence: grep shows a single call site for startMemoryFileFlushTimer. Executed MemoryFlushTimer with a summarizer returning '': first call returned null and the next getTurnsSince cursor advanced from 1000000 to 1900000. Concurrency pressure is inferred from BRIDGE_SPAWN_DEFAULTS.maxConcurrent=3.
- [medium] Indexing of memory files: memory-scanner.ts:107, :203-206; daemon.ts:5489-5541; cli/commands/memory-backfill.ts:129; session-manager.ts:2485-2492: The daemon starts each scanner with ignoreInitial:true and never calls backfill(), despite the comment at daemon.ts:5493 saying it does; only the CLI does. Files written before the store exists, while a scanner is stopped, or whose change event arrives during an in-flight index (handleUpsert silently returns 0 if the path is already indexing) stay unindexed until another change. stopAgent deletes the scanner and nothing recreates it for an in-daemon restart.
  Evidence: grep for .backfill( finds only the CLI. The lazy store proxy returns '' for insertMemoryChunk when no store exists. Race and final-flush timing are plausible, not observed.
- [medium] Pre-turn time window: memory-chunks.ts:170-204, session-manager.ts:938: Chunks older than 14 days by file mtime vanish from per-turn retrieval: daily logs and MEM-04 flush files expire at two weeks; only vault, procedures, digests, documents and cue files are exempt. After that the fact is reachable only if the agent runs clawcode_memory_search or memory_lookup.
  Evidence: Executed applyTimeWindowFilter: Monday flush file kept on Friday, dropped at +6 weeks; cue file kept; daily log dropped.
- [medium] Consolidation: consolidation.ts:94-167 and :273, schema.ts:23, daemon.ts:4676-4695, consolidation.ts:384: weeklyThreshold defaults to 7 but files are grouped per ISO week (max 7), so a week is consolidated only if the agent logged on all 7 days. A weekday-only agent never produces a digest or archives a daily log; flush and cue files never match the YYYY-MM-DD.md pattern. If it did run, each day is head-sliced to 30000/N chars before Haiku and the digest row is inserted with dedup ON (content-replacing merge risk). Monthly needs 4 weekly digests.
  Evidence: Executed detectUnconsolidatedWeeks: 5 weekday logs, threshold 7 -> 0 groups; 7 logs -> 1 group. daemon.ts comment says prod overrides weeklyThreshold to 1; clawcode.yaml does not.
- [low] Tiering and decay: tiers.ts:36-43 and :66-95 and :129-142, store.ts:1302-1315, memory-retrieval.ts:701-713: Pre-turn retrieval touches accessed_at but not access_count, so a retrieved fact can never reach hot tier (needs 3 accesses in 7 days or 5 backlinks); the stable-prefix hot slot (top 3) is out of reach. Decay itself is mild at 6 weeks, so no archival loss happens in the window; the loss is rank drift versus fresh rows and the 14-day chunk window.
  Evidence: Executed relevance at 42 days: 0.7 -> 0.265, 0.78 -> 0.296, 0.65 -> 0.246, 0.30 -> 0.114; cold after about 115, 119, 112 and 78 idle days. Cold rows remain searchable.
- [low] Compaction Layer 1: compaction.ts:124 and :148-155, compact-extractors/index.ts:133-155, daemon.ts compact call sites: flushConversation appends every active-session turn to the daily log again although the heartbeat flusher already wrote them (cursor only protects the heartbeat), creating duplicate chunks that compete for top-5 slots. Extracted facts are inserted with no tags/importance and a 50-fact cap ordered preserved, Tier 2, then oldest-first Tier 4. Tier 3 prose is not wired. SDK-native auto compaction has no hook, so it writes nothing.
  Evidence: Code read; grep finds no PreCompact hook and no tier3Summarize at the daemon call sites.
- [low] Static SOUL carrier for the web agent: session-config.ts:593, memory/fingerprint.ts:19 and :44: The web agent's SOUL states the exact fact, but only a fingerprint is injected: the first line of the first 3 bullets under a heading containing 'rule', cut mid clause. The model sees 'recology-api staging and prod share one database: treat staging.' without 'writes as production writes', and the agent name is extracted as 'SOUL.md'. Other agents' souls do not contain it.
  Evidence: Executed extractFingerprint on the real clawcode.yaml souls: web carries it truncated; power-platform, servicedesk, opswatch do not.
- [low] Importance and embedding inputs: importance.ts:21-36, embedder.ts:51 and :139: The heuristic counts digits and capitalized words in the Discord envelope, inflating a wrapped turn to 0.66 versus 0.278 for the bare sentence; embeddings only see the first 200 words, so tail sections of long summaries (Commitments) are not reflected in the vector.
  Evidence: Executed calculateImportance on wrapped vs bare text. Truncation read in truncateText.
- [low] Haiku routing: haiku-direct.ts:85 and :118-133: Default route is the claude binary bridge, which works on macOS. If bridge.haikuDirectRouting is ever flipped to direct, loadOAuthToken needs ~/.claude/.credentials.json, which does not exist on this Mac (tokens live in the Keychain), so every summarizer, flush and consolidation call would silently return ''.
  Evidence: File absent (checked). Latent only; no current impact.

### document-lifecycle

**Verdict.** The ingest pipeline is real and fairly mature, but the three-week-later question is only partly answerable, and for the 'blue logo' part not reliably. What works for an agent with the flag on (only `servicedesk` in the local clawcode.yaml): the PDF text and the PNG OCR plus Haiku summary are chunked, stored in document_chunks and cross-ingested as memory_chunks `document:<slug>`, which are exempt from the 14-day window, so per-turn retrieval and clawcode_memory_search can still surface the invoice text at 21 days. DocumentStore itself is searched only by the search_documents tool; context-assembler.ts does no retrieval. What breaks the chain: (1) the retriever query is the whole Discord envelope, so BM25 never sees the user's words; (2) the logo color is not captured for the PDF at all and only incidentally for the PNG (the vision prompt never asks, there is no image embedding, and nothing links the PNG to the PDF); (3) agents without autoIngestAttachments silently ingest nothing; (4) mixed text/scanned PDFs lose their scanned pages, and a textless PNG with failed vision is stored as raw PNG bytes; (5) chunks have no vendor or filename context and per-turn retrieval admits only 2 document chunks, so a wrong invoice's total is a real risk. The agent's realistic path is clawcode_memory_search then clawcode_memory_recall to read the total, and search_documents (not advertised in the manifest) followed by Read on the `Image:` archive path or the original inbox PDF to check the logo. The agent would have to guess that sequence. Nothing was run against a live daemon; OCR tier behavior on this Mac is inferred from the missing tesseract binary and code reading.

**Breaks.**

- [high] Pre-turn retrieval query: src/discord/bridge.ts:5318-5319 and 8863-8867; src/manager/memory-context-augmenter.ts:170; src/manager/turn-dispatcher.ts:1244; src/memory/fts-query.ts:21,38-50: The retriever is given the whole Discord envelope (<\channel source=... chat_id=... message_id=... user=... ts=...>question</channel>), not the user's words. extractFtsTerms keeps the first 12 non-stopword tokens, which are all envelope tokens, so the keyword half of hybrid retrieval never sees 'invoice', 'total', 'vendor', 'blue', 'logo'. The vector half embeds envelope noise plus the question. This affects every Discord turn, and is fatal for exact-token recall of invoice facts.
  Evidence: Probe: terms for the enveloped question = [channel, source, discord, chat_id, <snowflake>, message_id, <snowflake>, user, jjagpal, ts, 2026, 10]; bare question = [total, invoice, vendor, blue, logo]. Probe on an in-memory MemoryStore with a 21-day-old document: invoice chunk plus 25 noise chunks: bare query puts the invoice chunk in the top 5; the enveloped query returns 20 noise FTS hits and no invoice chunk in the top 5 (vector side was uniform in the probe, so illustrative of the FTS half only). No envelope stripping exists in session-manager.ts:840-945 or retrieveMemoryChunks.
- [high] Visual content capture: src/manager/auto-ingest-handler.ts:323-336; src/document-ingest/handlers/text-pdf.ts; src/vision/prompts.ts:16-31; src/vision/types.ts:71-78: 'The vendor with the blue logo' cannot be resolved from the index. PDFs never get vision (gated on image/* mime) and pdf-parse ignores embedded images, so a PDF logo is recorded nowhere. For the PNG, the only visual record is Haiku's 1-2 sentence summary, and the prompt never asks for colors, logos, layout or brand appearance, so the attribute is only present if the model volunteers it. There is no image embedding. The PDF and PNG are separate documents with no stored relationship (the shared message_id sits in the documents table but is in no chunk and is used by no search tool), so the PNG logo cannot be joined to the PDF vendor. If vision returns null (refusal, transport failure, health failover to ocr-only) the PNG is OCR text only.

No Discord receipt (progress message is gated on the flag), no note to the agent, nothing indexed. Three weeks later the question has no indexed answer on those agents. Pages that are scans (a stamped remittance page, scanned backup, signature page with the amount) get no text and no imageBuffer, so they are skipped with no OCR and no warning, and telemetry understates the page count.

The same fallback hits any file whose name is not lowercase .pdf (endsWith('.pdf') is case sensitive). The garbage chunk then competes in per-turn retrieval with the +0.2 document nudge.

A PDF+PNG upload (2 files, so a progress message is seeded) ends with '✅ Ingested 2/2 files' even when an item failed, was skipped as an empty doc, or was a no-chunk result. A second-hop 'get the total chunk of this invoice' call can come back empty even though the chunks exist.

Because ingest is fire-and-forget and takes tens of seconds, a search issued right after the upload is cached as empty and the same query keeps returning empty for 30 minutes. The tool schema has no bypass_cache parameter, so the agent cannot opt out.

- [medium] PDF content class: src/documents/auto-ingest-classifier.ts:267-280: A PDF's priority is decided only by byte size (>= 100,000 high, else low). A text-only invoice PDF smaller than 100KB is classed LOW (0.5x) with 'invoice' content never inspected, versus 1.5x for a large PDF. Combined with the agent weight and no recency boost after 2 days, a small invoice ranks well below other documents.
  Evidence: Probe: classifyAttachment size 60000 -> low ('PDF size 60000 < 100000'); the generated mixed.pdf was 11,865 bytes.
- [medium] Chunk content and ranking: src/documents/chunker.ts:34-48; src/document-ingest/index.ts:181; src/document-ingest/cross-ingest.ts:215-228; src/memory/store.ts:2441; src/memory/memory-retrieval.ts:510,658-669; src/config/schema.ts:4810-4812; src/document-ingest/schemas/index.ts: Chunks carry no filename, vendor, invoice number, page number, ingest date or message provenance (filename and slug are not in FTS or embeddings), and chunkText flattens tables into one run of words. The vendor name is usually only in chunk 0 while 'Total' is in a later chunk, so a total chunk cannot be tied to a vendor. Per-turn retrieval admits at most 2 document chunks, and the +0.2 document scoreWeight is about 6x the maximum RRF score (0.033), so document chunks always outrank conversational chunks but the 2 admitted ones can belong to a different invoice, producing a confident wrong total. Auto-ingest has no structured extraction for invoices (only a taxReturn schema exists).

The rescan skips anything that already has a documents row, so the document is never retried into memory_chunks. It then exists only in DocumentStore, which pre-turn retrieval and clawcode_memory_search never read; only search_documents finds it.

On this Mac the CLI is absent, so tier 2 (tesseract.js 7.0.0) runs and needs language data on first use (network need unverified), then Claude vision if its real confidence is below 0.7.

### restart-and-compaction

**Verdict.** Neither a restart nor an SDK compaction preserves what the agent knew a turn earlier. After the usual restart (a stop that completed, or any crash) the agent gets a fresh SDK session with a new stable prefix plus a one-shot first-message block. The prefix is rebuilt from SOUL fingerprint, IDENTITY.md, capability manifest, MEMORY.md and USER.md (squeezed into a 4,000-token identity budget, so the advertised 16K and 4K char caps are not real), tools, discord bindings and 3 hot memories. The first-message block holds a resume summary that is effectively never written plus up to 3 Haiku summaries of Discord-only text. A crash within 4h can drop even those. Tool results, partial work, the in-flight user message, background tasks and the extracted in-flight-task facts are all lost. The only transcript-preserving path is the accidental resume of agents whose clean stop did not finish inside the 20s shutdown watchdog, which gives nondeterministic per-agent behaviour. After compaction, ClawCode's own pipeline (daily-log flush, fact extraction) runs only on manual IPC. Its automatic trigger is dead because the daemon-side fill meter is never fed, SDK-native auto-compaction has no ClawCode hook, nothing mirrors the SDK summary, the continuity block is never re-armed, and the system prompt stays frozen at session start. No compaction summary is ever persisted to the file that boot reads back. The five most urgent fixes: (1) feed or replace the fill provider with SDK getContextUsage so the zone snapshot and autoCompactAt trigger can fire; (2) persist a real summary (SDK compact output or the Tier-2 facts) to the resume summary file with a timestamp, and register PreCompact/PostCompact hooks; (3) inject active-state.yaml (or the Tier-2 facts) into the first-message block and add a current-task category to the session summarizer; (4) preserve sessionId across crash and stop and resume the transcript consistently, and size shutdown (drain/stopAll/summarizer timeouts) so stop-path summaries finish; (5) reconcile the identity budget with the Tier-1 caps and wire sources.systemPromptDirectives.

**Breaks.**

- [critical] src/memory/context-summary.ts + src/manager/daemon.ts:5589-5598 + src/memory/compaction.ts:183-205 + src/heartbeat/checks/context-fill.ts:46: The resume summary read at boot (context-summary.md) is effectively dead. Compaction never writes it, and the only writer fires from a path that cannot trigger for SDK agents. Boot injects either nothing or the content-free string 'Auto-snapshot at N% context fill [zone zone]'.
  Evidence: saveContextSummary has exactly one caller: the heartbeat snapshotCallback, which writes `Auto-snapshot at ${pct}% context fill [${zone} zone]` (daemon.ts:5591-5593). It fires only on an upward zone transition, but CharacterCountFillProvider.addTurn has zero production callers (grep: definition only, compaction.ts:192), so fill is always 0 and the zone is always green. The repo admits it in heartbeat/checks/session-log-flush.ts:7-9 ('SDK-session agents report ~0% daemon-side fill, so compaction never fires'). The same dead fill value means autoCompactAt never triggers (context-fill.ts:101-103). CompactionResult.summary is returned to handleCompactSession and used only as `summary.length > 0` (daemon-compact-session-ipc.ts:414). session-config.ts:1091 reads the file via loadLatestSummary.
- [high] src/manager/session-adapter.ts:151-187 (hooks), src/manager/daemon-compact-session-ipc.ts:395-409: SDK-native auto-compaction (the only compaction that actually happens in production) is invisible to ClawCode. Layer 1 (daily-log flush, fact extraction into memories.db) runs only on an operator-issued compact-session IPC. The SDK summary exists only in the transcript and is never mirrored to disk, memory, or the prompt, so it dies on the next fresh-session restart.

Hooks registered are PreToolUse only (session-adapter.ts:187). The heartbeat auto-trigger is dead (break 1). Layer 1 is invoked solely from handleCompactSession (daemon.ts:5771 and 17407 paths). Inferred: the SDK still auto-compacts by default since no code disables it.

The Session Continuity block (resume summary plus Recent Sessions) exists only inside the first user message, so a native /compact summarizer can drop or blur it. ### dream-output-reaches-prompt

**Verdict.** Partly. Consolidation output reaches agent prompts reliably; dream output reaches them only through MEMORY.md promotion, and most of what dreaming produces is orphaned.

REACHES A PROMPT (verified in code):
- Consolidation digests (weekly and monthly `memories` rows, plus the digests/*.md file chunks): per-turn <memory-context> via hybrid RRF (cadence-gated, top 5, 1500 token budget), on-demand via clawcode_memory_search / recall / memory_lookup, and the hot tier if a row gets promoted.
- Dream MEMORY.md promotions: only for D-10 eligible candidates (score >= 80 and non-mutating, or a priority pass), after the 30-minute veto window, the 1-minute tick, and a cap check. They are injected only at the next session build, not into a running session.
- Dream is off by default (`enabled:false`); consolidation is on by default.

ORPHANED (never reach a prompt): dream logs (`<tier1Dir>/dreams`, including themedReflection), graph-edges.json, `suggestedConsolidations`, all operator-required promotions, and the ops logs (dream-runs, dream-veto-pending, consolidation-runs). The dream-log slot in the prompt (<dream_log_recent>) is an empty sentinel. Dream wikilinks never reach `memory_links`. Exception, shape-dependent: dream logs are indexed when memoryPath equals <workspace>/memory.

DELETE / SUPERSEDE: nothing is deleted or superseded. Consolidation moves daily logs and weekly digests to archive/ (still indexed at -0.2, time-windowed) and removes only the `session_logs` bookkeeping row. It never reads or changes `memories` rows, and weekly and monthly digest rows coexist. Dream promotion copies a chunk into MEMORY.md and leaves the source.

CONTRADICTIONS: not reconciled anywhere. No prompt, schema field, column or code path handles them. The only accidental mechanism is dedup-on-insert, which silently overwrites any memory at cosine >= 0.85 (break 1, reproduced). The dream's edit/merge intent is downgraded to append and the consolidation proposals are write-only (break 2).

Most important breaks, in order: (1) consolidation digest inserts can overwrite an existing memory (reproduced); (2) dream 'operator-required' output has no apply path; (3) the priority-pass loop cannot reduce MEMORY.md; (4) promotions need an agent restart to take effect; (5) the dream re-fires on every idle tick with identical input. Not verified: live prod data or dedup hit rates; no tests were run beyond the scratch repro; no repo files were modified.

**Breaks.**

- [high] src/memory/consolidation.ts:384-391 and :439-446 (insert with no skipDedup) -> src/memory/store.ts:290-296 -> src/memory/dedup.ts:74,107: A weekly or monthly digest insert can silently MERGE into an unrelated existing memory instead of creating a new row. The existing row's content is replaced by the digest text, tags are unioned, and `source` stays as it was. If the target is a `session-summary` row, the merged row now carries the excluded tag and disappears from per-turn retrieval. If it is a manual/owner memory, the owner's fact is overwritten by LLM digest text. writeWeeklyDigest ignores insert()'s return value, so the run still counts the digest as created and then archives the daily logs. session-summarizer.ts and episode-store.ts pass skipDedup:true for exactly this reason; consolidation does not.
  Evidence: DEFAULT_DEDUP_CONFIG enabled:true, threshold 0.85 (store.ts:163), and session-memory.ts:71 constructs `new MemoryStore(dbPath)` with no override. EXECUTED REPRO (scratchpad merge-repro.mts, real MemoryStore, sqlite-vec cosine): inserted a source=manual memory, then called writeWeeklyDigest with a digest embedding at cosine 0.989. Result: rows after digest insert = 1, same id as the manual memory, source still 'manual', tags ['preference','weekly-digest','2026-W05'], content replaced by the digest text. My demo used cos 0.989, which is above the 0.85 threshold; how often real digests hit >=0.85 is not measured. No test covers dedup with consolidation (consolidation.test.ts has no dedup or merge cases).
- [high] src/manager/dream-auto-apply.ts:461-474 (operatorOnly never persisted); src/manager/dream-discord-summary.ts footer and '[veto-required]' rows; src/manager/daemon.ts approve-dream-run (~10790-10810): Everything the dream marks 'operator-required' is write-only. That covers all suggestedConsolidations, all mutating (edit/merge/overwrite) candidates in a non-priority pass, and all additive candidates scoring below 80. These items go only to the dream log and a Discord message labelled '[veto-required]'. They are never stored in the veto store and no code path applies them. Approving a run only closes the window for the `eligible` rows. The only consolidation-like action the dream can take is 'append a chunk to MEMORY.md'. The dream therefore cannot merge, rewrite or prune.
  Evidence: operatorOnly is built at dream-auto-apply.ts:466,474 and used only for toSummaryRow and the count; scheduleAutoApply (:501) receives only `eligible`. grep -rn 'suggestedConsolidations|operatorRequired' src outside dream-pass/auto-apply/discord-summary/log-writer returns only type declarations (slash-commands.ts:489, cli/dream.ts:44). dream-auto-apply.ts header comment says the mutating side 'calls the agent-curated archive primitive', but grep for clawcodeMemoryArchive shows no dream caller.
- [medium] src/manager/dream-promote.ts:118-126 vs src/manager/session-config.ts:716-723 and src/manager/dream-cron.ts priority pass: The priority-pass loop cannot relieve MEMORY.md truncation. A tier1 over-cap truncation event triggers a priority dream pass. promoteCandidates then refuses every append that would push MEMORY.md past capChars (reason 'memory-at-cap') and nothing in the dream prunes, merges or archives. A pass that promotes nothing and skips a cap or missing-chunk candidate is marked 'expired' with applyError. The truncation marker '[TRUNCATED ... dream-pass priority requested]' promises a compaction the dream cannot deliver.
  Evidence: dream-promote.ts:118 `if (memory.length + section.length > deps.capChars)` -> skip 'memory-at-cap'; :147-152 ok=false when nothing promoted and any skip is non-benign. Truncation marker at session-config.ts:723. No removal code path exists in dream-*.ts (only clawcodeMemoryEdit mode=append at daemon.ts:3962).
- [medium] src/manager/daemon.ts:3940-3969 (applyDreamVetoRow) and src/manager/session-config.ts:710-763: A dream-promoted MEMORY.md section reaches the prompt only at the next session build (agent restart or resume). The running session keeps the stable prefix built at start. There is no refresh after promotion and no restart, so the 'Dream pass promoted N notes into MEMORY.md' Discord line over-states the effect for long-lived sessions.
  Evidence: buildSessionConfig call sites: session-manager.ts:1320 (startAgent), :3027 (reconcile resume), :3354 (voice only). `refreshStablePrefix` appears only in a comment (session-manager.ts:306). applyDreamVetoRow has no restart or rebuild call.
- [medium] src/manager/idle-window-detector.ts:75-76 and src/manager/dream-cron.ts:225-266: A dream pass fires on every cron tick for as long as the agent stays idle, with identical inputs, and nothing records that this idle stretch was already dreamed. Beyond 6h `idle-ceiling-bypass` returns idle:true forever, so an agent silent over a weekend gets roughly 96 Haiku calls. Each appends another section to the same day's dream log and can schedule duplicate veto rows (the already-present substring check only rescues the MEMORY.md append). The header comment says 'still fire ONE final consolidation pass'.
  Evidence: isAgentIdle returns {idle:true,reason:'idle-ceiling-bypass'} whenever elapsed > 6h. onTick (dream-cron.ts:225) has no last-run state; it calls deps.runDreamPass(deps.agentName) at :255 whenever idle.idle. Cron step is min(idleMinutes,59) minutes (:211).
- [medium] src/memory/consolidation.ts:266-281 then :654-661: Lossy weekly digest followed by archival. When combined daily logs exceed 30,000 chars, each day is cut to its first floor(30000/n) chars (head only, so the end of each day, usually the newest conversation, is dropped). The logs are then archived. The dropped detail survives only as archive-path chunks, which carry a -0.2 weight and are time-windowed in the pre-turn path, so it is effectively absent from per-turn recall.
  Evidence: maxPerDay = Math.floor(MAX_PROMPT_CHARS / dailyLogs.length); content.slice(0, maxPerDay) at :273-275. archiveDailyLogs is called after writeWeeklyDigest regardless of truncation (:661).
- [medium] src/manager/memory-context-augmenter.ts:135 and src/memory/memory-chunks.ts (chunkMarkdownByH2 drops pre-H2 frontmatter): Digest provenance and period are lost at injection time. A consolidation `memories` row renders as `### memory:<id>` with no date or period; a digest file chunk renders as `### Key Facts` with the frontmatter 'period:' discarded. Stale facts from an old digest and newer facts are injected side by side with no timestamps, and nothing reconciles them (see contradictions). Only the user-visible recall footer uses createdAt.
  Evidence: `const heading = c.heading ?? c.path` -> memory rows have heading null and path `memory:<id>` (memory-retrieval.ts hydrate block, ~:555). The digest markdown puts period/sources in frontmatter before the first `## ` (consolidation.ts:365-375); chunkMarkdownByH2 discards everything before the first H2.
- [medium] src/manager/memory-digest.ts:195 (store.delete) vs consolidation.ts dual-write (W1/W2 + W3): Owner 'forget' on a digest item is not a real forget. A number reaction deletes only the `memories` row. The same text remains in digests/*.md, in its memory_chunks rows (standing path, no time window) and in the archived daily logs, so it stays retrievable. Consolidation digest rows are also eligible digest items because only four tags are excluded, so an owner can be shown first lines of LLM digests as 'things I learned about you'. The memory-digest.ts header claims dreams save memories, but the dream writes nothing to `memories`.
  Evidence: applyDigestFeedback -> `store.delete(id)` (memory-digest.ts:195) removes memories, vec and v2 rows only. DIGEST_EXCLUDE_TAGS = session-summary, mid-session, raw-fallback, confirmed (:25). consolidation.ts writes both the file and the row.

They never reach `memory_links` (memory-id edges used by GraphSearch and tier centrality), and the only reader is the next dream prompt, so they do not influence retrieval, hot-tier centrality or the prompt. (b) The agent-facing capability manifest says dream logs persist to `memory/dreams/YYYY-MM-DD.md`, but they are written to `<tier1Dir>/dreams/`. In the default layout that path does not exist and is not under the scanned `memory/` tree. (c) The dream log is not indexed by the scanner in the default layout, so the themed reflection is unreachable by the agent. Shape-dependent exception, inferred from memory-roots.test.ts fixtures rather than a live deployment: when memoryPath = <workspace>/memory (the client-a-personal shape), tier1Dir/dreams falls under the watched root and is indexed.

Scanner roots (memory-scanner.ts:83) are `<workspace>/memory` plus extra roots; dreams/ is not a child of memoryDir.
- [low] src/memory/consolidation.ts:354-391 + :161-171 (existsSync(digestFile) skip) and :235-241 (monthly): Partial-failure and coverage gaps. (a) writeWeeklyDigest writes the file, then embeds and inserts. If embedding or insert throws, the file exists, the week is skipped forever by detectUnconsolidatedWeeks, the `memories` row is never created, and the daily logs are never archived. (b) A monthly digest can be created once 4 weekly digests exist even if a fifth week of that month is still to come. When the fifth week's digest appears later, monthly-YYYY-MM.md already exists, so it is never rolled up or archived. (c) Months with fewer than monthlyThreshold weekly digests never roll up.
  Evidence: consolidation.ts:170 `if (existsSync(digestFile)) continue;` evaluated before any check on the SQLite row; write order :380 then :383-391 then archive at :661; :241 `if (existsSync(monthlyFile)) continue;`.

The promoted text then lands in a file the prompt never loads. The same mismatch affects clawcode_memory_edit. Nothing validates the basename, and all memory-roots tests use MEMORY.md.
  Evidence: memoryMdPath = resolve(autoLoad) (any filename); tier1Dir = dirname(autoLoad); appendMemory passes {path:'MEMORY.md'} with memoryRoot: roots.tier1Dir. Not observed in a live config; derived from code.

## Subsystem audits

### Memory storage, schema, tiers, decay, importance and migrations (src/memory/store.ts, schema.ts, types.ts, tiers.ts, tier-manager.ts, decay.ts, importance.ts, episode-store.ts, episode-archival.ts, migrations/embedding-v2*.ts, embedding-version.ts, embedding-v2-dual-write.ts, errors.ts, error-guard.ts, index.ts)

**How it works.** MemoryStore (store.ts:166) opens one better-sqlite3 connection per agent at <memoryPath>/memory/memories.db (session-memory.ts:70-71), sets WAL, busy_timeout, synchronous=NORMAL and foreign_keys=ON (store.ts:204-207), loads sqlite-vec and runs 16 idempotent migrations in fixed order (store.ts:211-238): base tables memories / session_logs / vec_memories float[384] cosine (1666-1693); CHECK-constraint table rebuilds for the 'consolidation' and 'episode' source values (1700-1804); tier column (1747-1758); memory_links graph with ON DELETE CASCADE (1810-1824); conversation tables plus external-content FTS (1833-1977); session_archive (2009-2059); api_key_sessions v1/v2 (2079-2120); origin_id with a partial UNIQUE index (2131-2144); metadata (2155-2162); memory_chunks + vec_memory_chunks + memory_chunks_fts with a porter rebuild (2186-2246); memories_fts keyword index kept in sync by triggers that are re-asserted every boot (2257-2284); vec_memories_v2 / vec_memory_chunks_v2 int8[384] plus the per-agent `migrations` state table (2314-2335); auto_linked_at and the tier/accessed_at indexes (2350-2377). Statements are prepared once (2768-2879).

Insert (store.ts:270-466): unless origin_id, skipDedup, or dedup disabled, checkForDuplicate runs a k=1 KNN against v1 vec_memories (dedup.ts:42-68). Similarity >= 0.85 calls mergeMemory, which replaces content, keeps max importance, unions tags, bumps access_count, swaps the v1 vector and drops the v2 vector (dedup.ts:105-131). Otherwise a nanoid row is written at tier 'warm' (2774-2777) with its v1 vector, optional v2 vector and wikilink edges in one transaction (360-409). Importance is caller-supplied unless absent or exactly 0.5, in which case calculateImportance applies length + code/number/proper-noun counts + a constant 0.2 (importance.ts:21-36; store.ts:349-351). After commit, a fire-and-forget hook embeds with bge-small and writes vec_memories_v2 when the agent's migration phase is in V2_WRITE_PHASES (416-418; embedding-v2-dual-write.ts:87-110; embedding-version.ts:57-63), and autoLinkMemory adds auto:similar edges (437-441; similarity.ts:199-225).

Reads: getById bumps access_count and accessed_at (472-493). searchMemoriesVec / searchMemoriesFts / getMemoryForRetrieval feed the pre-turn retriever (2607-2734); the v1 or v2 table is chosen per call from the migrations.phase row (849-859) and v2 distances are affine-mapped onto the v1 scale (embedding-version.ts:88-107). touchAccessedAt (1302-1310) refreshes accessed_at only (no count) for memories that actually reached the prompt (memory-retrieval.ts:701-713). SemanticSearch (search.ts:93-146) serves explicit memory_lookup and bumps access for its top-K.

Tiers: every row starts warm. TierManager.runMaintenance (tier-manager.ts:560-627) runs on the 6h heartbeat (heartbeat/checks/tier-maintenance.ts:23,52) and refreshHotTier at session start (session-manager.ts:1314). Step 0 re-warms cold rows touched inside hotAccessWindowDays (store.ts:1471-1489); step 1 demotes hot rows idle >= hotDemotionDays (tiers.ts:108-115); step 2 cools the 1000 least-recently-used warm rows whose relevance importance * 0.5^(days/30) is below coldRelevanceThreshold unless they carry >= centralityPromoteThreshold backlinks (tiers.ts:129-142; tier-manager.ts:587-601; store.ts:1434-1462); step 3 promotes warm rows with access_count >= hotAccessThreshold inside the window or >= centralityPromoteThreshold backlinks (tiers.ts:66-95), ranked by scoreAndRank (relevance.ts:54-85) into the free hotBudget slots (tier-manager.ts:477-512). Cold rows keep their vectors and stay searchable (tier-manager.ts:7-17). Hot memories reach the prompt as getHotMemories().slice(0,3) (session-config.ts:831).

Embedding v2: EmbeddingV2Migrator (migrations/embedding-v2.ts:143-463) holds a phase row and legal transitions (91-123); runReEmbedBatch (embedding-v2-runner.ts:77-280) is called every 30s by migration-cron (manager/migration-cron.ts:181-258), backfills memories by id cursor (with a reset when nanoid ordering hides rows, 156-201), then chunks (290-336), and auto-advances to re-embed-complete. EpisodeStore (episode-store.ts:57-125) wraps insert with source='episode'; error-guard.ts:48-56 rejects empty or API-error summaries before consolidation persists them.

**Data flow.** Entry points into memories: MemoryStore.insert (store.ts:270) from the memory_save tool, session-summarizer final summaries and mid-session flushes (session-summarizer.ts:323, 613, both skipDedup), storeSoulMemory (session-memory.ts:312), compaction, cue writer, OpenClaw/skills imports via origin_id, the iOS gateway (openai-memory-gateway.ts:81-123 via origin_id accessors), TierManager.insertFromArchive for legacy cold files (tier-manager.ts:253-296). Chunks enter via insertMemoryChunk from the markdown scanner (store.ts:2389-2451); session_logs via recordSessionLog from compaction.ts:132. Tables written: memories, vec_memories, vec_memories_v2 (hook or backfill), memories_fts (triggers), memory_links (insert + autoLinkMemory), memory_chunks/vec_memory_chunks/vec_memory_chunks_v2/memory_chunks_fts/memory_files, session_logs, migrations; conversation_* and session_archive are written by ConversationStore over the same connection (session-memory.ts:168). Reads into prompts: pre-turn retriever searchMemoriesVec/searchMemoriesFts (store.ts:2607-2683) + getMemoryForRetrieval (2695) then touchAccessedAt (1302) into <memory-context>; hot tier via TierManager.getHotMemories (tier-manager.ts:521) into session-config.ts:831 (top 3) and bridge.ts:2781 (top 5); explicit memory_lookup via SemanticSearch.search (daemon.ts:15938); graph walk via getStoredVectorFloat + bumpAccess (graph-search.ts:91,125); conversation brief and search via findByTag("session-summary"), listRecent, listInRange (conversation-brief.ts:176, conversation-search.ts:116-149); digest via listInRange (memory-digest.ts:77); report card via countInRange. Exits: delete (store.ts:510) only from summary-dedupe.ts:258, cross-agent-coordinator.ts:310 and the iOS forget seam; tiering never deletes. Captured but never retrieved: `metadata` column (only round-tripped by the iOS gateway, 2155-2162); session_logs dates (getSessionLogDates has no caller, 672); episodes (recordEpisode has no production caller, so the episodes list IPC at daemon.ts:16541-16550 always reads an empty set); `consolidation.summaryModel`, `compactionThreshold`, `searchTopK`, `conversation.enabled`, `conversation.turnRetentionDays`, `episodes.archivalAgeDays` and `deduplication.*` are parsed (schema.ts:37-40, 74-76, 80-81, 104-105) and resolved (loader.ts:1348) but no runtime code reads them.

**Config knobs parsed but not wired at runtime.**

- `memory.compactionThreshold`: None. Only schema.ts:104, shared/types.ts:564 and config defaults mention it; no runtime reader.
- `memory.searchTopK`: None. SemanticSearch topK is passed per call by the lookup handler; no reader of this field.
- `memory.consolidation.summaryModel`: None. Only schema.ts:25 and shared/types.ts:572; the consolidation summarizer never reads it.
- `memory.deduplication.enabled / similarityThreshold`: None at runtime: session-memory.ts:71 calls new MemoryStore(dbPath) with no second argument, so store.ts:200 always uses DEFAULT_DEDUP_CONFIG.
- `memory.episodes.archivalAgeDays`: None. archiveOldEpisodes (episode-archival.ts:34) has no caller; recordEpisode has no caller either.
- `memory.conversation.enabled`: None. No code reads it; turns are always recorded.
- `memory.conversation.turnRetentionDays`: None. Turns are deleted only by deleteTurnsForSession at summarization (conversation-store.ts:600, 1324); session-retention prunes only empty terminated sessions after a hardcoded 30 days (session-retention.ts:15, 29).
- `defaults.embeddingMigration.cpuBudgetPct`: None. Resolved at migration-cron.ts:166 and carried on RunnerConfig but never read; embedding-v2-runner.ts:74-75 states the implementation rate-limits by heartbeat cadence instead.

**Issues.**

- [high/design-gap] **No supersession or contradiction handling; decay clock is reset by retrieval itself, so stale facts never age out once they start being recalled**
  Evidence: decay.ts:35-41 computes `daysSinceAccess = (nowTime - accessedTime)` from `accessedAt` only. store.ts:1302-1310 `touchAccessedAt` sets `accessed_at = ?` for every memory the retriever injects, called from memory-retrieval.ts:709 `args.store.touchAccessedAt(usedMemoryIds, ...)`. The memories table (store.ts:1668-1678) has no superseded_by, valid_until or version column. The only conflict resolution is dedup.ts:59 `if (similarity >= config.similarityThreshold)` at 0.85, which replaces content; two memories at similarity 0.6-0.84 coexist indefinitely. relevance.ts:71-73 ranks by `semantic * 0.7 + relevance * 0.3` with no created_at term.
  Impact: When a fact changes ("deadline is March 3" then "deadline moved to March 10") and the two texts fall under 0.85 cosine, both survive forever; whichever is retrieved gets its accessed_at refreshed and keeps competing on equal footing, so the agent can keep asserting the obsolete value. Nothing in the store lets a caller mark a memory as replaced.
  Fix: Add a nullable `superseded_by TEXT` column in migrateMaintenanceColumns, filter `m.superseded_by IS NULL` in searchMemoriesVec / searchMemoriesFts / SemanticSearch / listWarmCandidatesForPromotion, and expose `supersede(oldId, newId)` for memory_save and the edit tool. Optionally blend created_at into calculateRelevanceScore (e.g. decay on max(accessed_at, created_at + grace)) so retrieval alone cannot keep a very old row at full relevance.
- [high/design-gap] **Nothing ever forgets: memories and their vectors accumulate without bound and all remain in the brute-force KNN candidate set**
  Evidence: tier-manager.ts:14-17: cold rows "keep their vector and remain searchable"; archiveToCold (194-202) only calls `updateTier`. store.ts:1434-1462 and 1471-1489 move rows between warm and cold; no store method deletes by age or tier, and `delete()` (510) is called only from summary-dedupe.ts:258, cross-agent-coordinator.ts:310 and the iOS seam. session-summarizer.ts:597-621 inserts one `mid-session` row per flush with `skipDedup: true` every flushIntervalMinutes (schema.ts:99, default 15). search.ts:93-146 (explicit memory_lookup) applies no tag or tier filter, so cold mid-session blobs compete at the 0.7 semantic weight; sqlite-vec vec0 KNN is a full scan of vec_memories (store.ts:2669-2676).
  Impact: An agent with sessions running all day accrues roughly 96 mid-session memories per day forever; retrieval latency grows linearly with the corpus, dedup KNN (dedup.ts:47-51) and the auto-linker scan over every row, and explicit lookups return superseded checkpoints alongside real facts. The former cold-file design at least bounded the live table; the 999.119 fix removed deletion without adding any bounded retention.
  Fix: Smallest step: in runMaintenance, after the cold pass, delete cold rows tagged `mid-session` or `raw-fallback` whose `session:<id>` already has a final `session-summary` memory (findByTag) and whose accessed_at is older than hotAccessWindowDays. A second step is a hard cap on cold rows per agent (lowest relevance first), exposed as a `tiers.coldMaxRows` knob.
- [medium/inert-config] **memory.deduplication config is parsed but never passed to MemoryStore**
  Evidence: schema.ts:37-40 defines `dedupConfigSchema` with `enabled` and `similarityThreshold`. session-memory.ts:71 `const store = new MemoryStore(dbPath);` passes no second argument, and store.ts:200 `this.dedupConfig = dedupConfig ?? DEFAULT_DEDUP_CONFIG;` locks 0.85. grep over src for `.deduplication` finds only config/schema.ts defaults and shared/types.ts. The only tests that exercise a non-default value construct the store directly (store.test.ts:320, 332).
  Impact: Operators cannot disable dedup for an agent or raise the threshold after seeing distinct facts merged; the YAML knob silently does nothing while the merge path (issue below) continues to overwrite content.
  Fix: `new MemoryStore(dbPath, config.memory?.deduplication)` at session-memory.ts:71, plus one test asserting a resolved config with `enabled: false` yields two rows for a duplicate embedding.
- [medium/data-loss] **Dedup merge overwrites the older memory's content and discards the new memory's provenance, and a merge counts as an access**
  Evidence: dedup.ts:106-115 `UPDATE memories SET content = ?, importance = ?, tags = ?, access_count = access_count + 1, updated_at = ?, accessed_at = ? WHERE id = ?`. store.ts:296-301 passes only `content, importance, tags, embedding`; `input.source`, `input.sourceTurnIds` and `input.origin_id` are never written for the surviving row (compare the normal path at 361-372). Tags are unioned (dedup.ts:101) so a memory merged into a `session-summary` or `mid-session` row inherits the tag that memory-retrieval.ts:540-551 uses to drop it from the prompt. The only record of the merge is dedup.ts:133-138 `console.debug("Memory merged:", { ... similarity: "N/A" ...})`.
  Impact: "Meeting with Bob Monday about the budget" followed by "Meeting with Bob Monday about hiring" (plausibly >= 0.85 under MiniLM) leaves one row with only the second sentence and no trace of the first; a conversation-derived fact that lands on a system or summary row keeps the wrong source and lineage; restating a fact three times in a week satisfies tiers.ts:75 `accessCount >= config.hotAccessThreshold` without any retrieval.
  Fix: In mergeMemory: never add tags that appear in the retrieval exclude list; do not bump access_count; keep the prior text in a `content_history` JSON column (or append when the new text is not a superset); merge source_turn_ids; log at info with the real similarity (store.ts:295 has `dedupResult.similarity`).
- [medium/design-gap] **Agent-level memory block replaces the whole fleet memory block, dropping operator defaults for every sub-block except consolidation**
  Evidence: loader.ts:1348 `memory: agent.memory ?? defaults.memory,`. memoryConfigSchema (schema.ts:103-133) gives tiers/decay/deduplication/episodes zod defaults and leaves conversation optional, so an agent block containing only `conversation:` parses with schema defaults for the rest. daemon.ts:4659-4664 acknowledges it: "Agents override `memory` WHOLESALE (loader `??`), so a per-agent memory block without an explicit consolidation value loses the operator's `defaults.memory.consolidation`" and compensates only for consolidation (4665-4679).
  Impact: A fleet-wide `defaults.memory.tiers.hotBudget: 40` or `decay.halfLifeDays: 60` silently reverts to 20 / 30 for any agent that sets `memory.conversation.flushIntervalMinutes`; the operator sees no warning.
  Fix: In the loader, merge per sub-block: `memory: { ...defaults.memory, ...agent.memory, tiers: { ...defaults.memory.tiers, ...agent.memory?.tiers }, ... }`, or make the sub-blocks `.optional()` on agentSchema and cascade per field like daemon.ts:4665-4679 does.
- [medium/inert-config] **conversation.enabled and conversation.turnRetentionDays have no runtime reader**
  Evidence: schema.ts:80-81 `enabled: z.boolean().default(true), turnRetentionDays: z.number().int().min(7).default(90)`. grep for `turnRetentionDays` outside schema finds only shared/types.ts:604; grep for `conversation.enabled`/`conversation?.enabled` finds nothing. Turns are removed only by conversation-store.ts:600 `deleteTurnsForSession` (prepared at 1324, called from summarizeSession); session-retention.ts:15,29 deletes only "terminated rows with no turns" after `EMPTY_SESSION_RETENTION_DAYS = 30`.
  Impact: Setting `enabled: false` to keep transcripts out of SQLite does nothing; sessions that crash or whose summarization fails keep raw turns and their FTS rows indefinitely, so the privacy knob and the retention knob both lie.
  Fix: Gate ConversationStore.recordTurn (and the flush timer) on `config.memory.conversation?.enabled !== false`; in session-retention add `DELETE FROM conversation_turns WHERE created_at < ? AND session_id IN (SELECT id FROM conversation_sessions WHERE status != 'active')` driven by turnRetentionDays.
- [medium/inert-config] **memory.decay only reaches hot-tier promotion ranking; search, pre-turn retrieval and cold archival each keep their own hardcoded copy**
  Evidence: session-memory.ts:149-153 threads decay into TierManager.scoringConfig only. search.ts:28-32 `DEFAULT_SCORING_CONFIG = { semanticWeight: 0.7, decayWeight: 0.3, halfLifeDays: 30 }` and both constructors pass nothing (graph-search.ts:63 `new SemanticSearch(this.store.getDatabase())`, daemon.ts:15938 same). memory-retrieval.ts:367-371 `DEFAULT_MEMORY_SCORING` and session-manager.ts:846-861 builds retriever args from topK/tokenBudget/excludeTags only. tiers.ts:135-140 `calculateRelevanceScore(importance, accessedAt, now, { halfLifeDays: 30 })` ignores the configured half-life even though TierManager holds `scoringConfig.halfLifeDays` (tier-manager.ts:166, 592).
  Impact: Changing `decay.halfLifeDays` to 90 does not slow cooling and does not change what memory_lookup or <memory-context> rank; only the tie-break among already-qualifying promotion candidates moves. Three copies of the same constants can drift (conversation search already uses 14 at conversation-search.ts:89-91).
  Fix: Pass `config.memory.decay` into `new SemanticSearch(db, scoring)` at both call sites, into `retrieveMemoryChunks({ scoring })` from getMemoryRetrieverForAgent, and add a `halfLifeDays` parameter to shouldArchiveToCold fed from `this.scoringConfig.halfLifeDays`.
- [medium/design-gap] **Content edits and re-imports leave stale vectors and links**
  Evidence: store.ts:1570-1572 `Deliberately does NOT re-embed ... the stale vector is acceptable, documented debt` on updateContentByOriginId (1574-1591 updates only content/updated_at). store.ts:385-389 and 420-434: an origin_id collision takes the INSERT OR IGNORE branch, discards the passed `embedding` and content, and returns the existing row; types.ts:50-51 states "content changes don't create duplicates" but nothing updates them either. The memories_fts AFTER UPDATE OF content trigger (2273-2276) keeps keyword search current, so vector and keyword results disagree.
  Impact: An iOS edit from "lives in Austin" to "lives in Denver" is findable by keyword but its vector still means Austin, so semantic recall for Denver misses it and dedup may merge a later Denver fact into some other row; a re-run OpenClaw import after the source file changed keeps the old text forever.
  Fix: In updateContentByOriginId delete the row's vec_memories / vec_memories_v2 rows inside the same transaction so the existing doctor path (`countMemoriesMissingEmbedding`, store.ts:911; daemon.ts:9028) and the v2 backfill re-embed it, and re-extract wikilinks. For origin_id collisions, compare content and run the same update when it differs.
- [low/dead-path] **Episodes are never recorded and never archived; archivalAgeDays is inert and the archival code contradicts the 999.119 cold model**
  Evidence: episode-store.ts:73 `recordEpisode` has no caller outside tests (grep); episode-archival.ts:34 `archiveOldEpisodes` has no caller; schema.ts:74-76 `episodeConfigSchema` is read by nothing. session-memory.ts:160 constructs the store and daemon.ts:16541-16550 only lists. episode-archival.ts:56 `db.prepare("DELETE FROM vec_memories WHERE memory_id = ?")` removes the vector, while tier-manager.ts:14-17 defines cold as "keep their vector and remain searchable". store.ts:1057-1062 still documents the cold-archive shape as the reason for the orphan-cleanup direction.
  Impact: The episodes IPC always returns zero rows; a 187-line test file pins behavior that cannot execute; if someone wires archival as written, archived episodes vanish from semantic search and show up as `memoriesWithoutEmbedding` in checkIntegrity.
  Fix: Either delete episode-store.ts, episode-archival.ts, the knob and their tests, or wire recordEpisode to a real event and change archiveOldEpisodes to call only `store.updateTier(row.id, "cold")`.
- [low/bug] **Explicit importance 0.5 is silently replaced by the heuristic, and the heuristic's recency boost is a constant**
  Evidence: store.ts:349-351 `const importance = input.importance != null && input.importance !== 0.5 ? input.importance : calculateImportance(input.content);`. importance.ts:33-35 `const recencyBoost = 0.2; return clamp(lengthScore + entityScore + recencyBoost)`; nothing ever removes the boost, so every heuristic score has a 0.2 floor. The dedup path uses a different default: store.ts:298 `importance: input.importance ?? 0.5`.
  Impact: A caller that deliberately sets 0.5 (tier-manager.test.ts:605 does) gets an arbitrary 0.2-1.0 value; two code paths disagree on what "no importance given" means; the comment and tests describe a recency effect that does not exist.
  Fix: Treat only `undefined` as "compute" (drop the `!== 0.5` clause), use calculateImportance in the merge path too, and rename recencyBoost to baseScore or make it decay in calculateRelevanceScore.
- [low/inert-config] **compactionThreshold, searchTopK, consolidation.summaryModel and embeddingMigration.cpuBudgetPct are parsed but never read**
  Evidence: grep over src for `compactionThreshold`, `searchTopK`, `summaryModel` finds only schema.ts:104-105, schema.ts:25, shared/types.ts:564-572 and config defaults. embedding-v2-runner.ts:74-75: "The CPU budget knob is documented but the actual implementation rate-limits via heartbeat cadence"; migration-cron.ts:166 resolves `cpuBudgetPct` into RunnerConfig and the runner never dereferences it.
  Impact: Operators tune values that change nothing; the schema and `shared/types.ts` advertise four capabilities the runtime lacks.
  Fix: Remove the four fields (and their type entries) or wire them: searchTopK into the memory_lookup handler's SemanticSearch call, summaryModel into the consolidation summarizer, cpuBudgetPct into migration-cron's tick spacing, compactionThreshold into CompactionManager.
