- [low/dead-path] **Dead store and tier-manager surface, with one misleading contract**
  Evidence: No callers outside tests for store.ts:672 `getSessionLogDates`, 790 `insertWithDualWrite`, tier-manager.ts:539 `getHotMemoriesStableToken` (context-assembler.ts:667,1397 computes its own `computeHotStableToken(hotInput.rendered)`), tier-manager.ts:211 `exportColdArchive` and 307 `rewarmFromCold` (plus embeddingToBase64 / generateColdSlug), and migrations/embedding-v2.ts:410 `currentReadVersion` / 424 `currentWriteVersions`. `currentWriteVersions` returns `["v2"]` for cutover (434-436) while store.ts:391 `this.stmts.insertVec.run(id, embedding)` always writes v1 and embedding-v2-dual-write.ts:52-54 states "v1 is ALWAYS written".
  Impact: Maintenance cost and a false contract: a future caller trusting currentWriteVersions would stop writing v1 and break dedup (dedup.ts:49 reads vec_memories only) and the rollback path (embedding-v2.ts:244-254).
  Fix: Delete the unused methods (keep importColdArchives and the index.ts exports it needs) and either delete currentWriteVersions or make it return the truth (`["v1","v2"]` through v1-dropped).
- [low/design-gap] **getById mutates access state and is used as a read-only hydrator, inflating the hot-promotion signal**
  Evidence: store.ts:472-484 `getById` runs `this.stmts.updateAccess.run(now, id)` on every call; the doc at 2690-2693 for getMemoryForRetrieval explains why the retriever avoids it. clawcode-memory-search.ts:182 calls `deps.store.getById(h.memoryId)` per hit just to read tags, after the search already bumped the same rows; tiers.ts:75 promotes on `accessCount >= 3`.
  Impact: A single tag-filtered search can add two accesses per hit; three searches in a week push unrelated rows into the 20-slot hot tier (session-config.ts:831 injects the top 3 of it by importance) and the 1022-1038 contract "keep the bump exactly one-per-search-call" is violated by the store's own consumers.
  Fix: Add `peekById(id)` (same SELECT, no UPDATE) and use it in the search tool, dream-machinery and greeting hydration; keep getById for the recall tool.
- [low/race] **Fire-and-forget v2 write can land after the memory is deleted, leaving an orphan v2 row until the next repair**
  Evidence: store.ts:416-418 fires the hook after the insert transaction; embedding-v2-dual-write.ts:99-103 awaits `embedV2` then calls `insertEmbeddingV2`, which is `INSERT OR REPLACE` (store.ts:2808-2810) with no existence check and no FK on the vec table. delete() (510-540) runs in between on the summary-dedupe or iOS path.
  Impact: An orphan vec_memories_v2 row; harmless for v1 reads, but under v2 reads searchMemoriesVec's INNER JOIN hides it (2662-2663) while checkIntegrity reports orphanVecV2 until boot or the auto-linker repair runs.
  Fix: In insertEmbeddingV2, run `INSERT OR REPLACE ... SELECT ?, vec_int8(?) WHERE EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM memories WHERE id = ?)` or check `checkMemoryExists` inside a transaction.
- [low/design-gap] **Memories that lack a v1 vector are invisible to the v2 backfill**
  Evidence: store.ts:813-816 and 950-955 both require `m.id IN (SELECT memory_id FROM vec_memories)` before `NOT IN (SELECT memory_id FROM vec_memories_v2)`. store.ts:911-919 `countMemoriesMissingEmbedding` documents 256 such rows on fin-client and only the operator doctor (daemon.ts:9028-9031) re-embeds them.
  Impact: Under v2 reads those rows are unreachable by vector search until an operator runs the doctor and then waits for the backfill; the migration dashboard reports 100% while they are missing.
  Fix: Drop the `IN vec_memories` predicate from the two v2-missing queries (the runner only needs content), or have the backfill call insertEmbeddingV1 for rows missing both.
- [low/performance] **memories_fts delete/update triggers scan the whole index**
  Evidence: store.ts:2262-2266 declares `memory_id UNINDEXED` and the triggers at 2270-2276 run `DELETE FROM memories_fts WHERE memory_id = old.id`; FTS5 cannot use an UNINDEXED column as a key, so each memory delete or content update is O(rows in memories_fts). deleteMemoryChunksByPath (2476-2478) has the same shape over memory_chunks_fts.
  Impact: Cheap today; grows linearly with the never-pruned memories table (see the forgetting issue), and runs inside the delete transaction that tier maintenance and summary-dedupe hold.
  Fix: Key the FTS table by rowid of memories (external-content mode, as conversation_turns_fts does at 1939-1944) now that the table-rebuild migrations no longer fire, or keep a plain `memories_fts_map(memory_id PRIMARY KEY, rowid)` table.
- [low/test-gap] **Tests do not cover the config-to-runtime seams, so inert knobs went unnoticed**
  Evidence: store.test.ts:320, 332 construct `new MemoryStore(":memory:", { enabled: true, similarityThreshold: 0.85 })` directly; decay.test.ts:48-96 only parses the schema; no test exercises session-memory.ts:59-157 with a non-default `config.memory`; no test covers loader.ts:1348 cascade; episode-archival.test.ts:4 imports a function with no production caller; there is no dedup test across different `source` values or into a tagged row, and none for `importance: 0.5` on store.ts:349.
  Impact: Every inert knob in this audit (deduplication, decay for search/retrieval, episodes, conversation.enabled, turnRetentionDays, searchTopK, compactionThreshold, summaryModel, cpuBudgetPct) passes the suite.
  Fix: Add one AgentMemoryManager test that passes a resolved config with distinct dedup/decay/tier values and asserts the MemoryStore, TierManager and SemanticSearch received them; add a loader test for a partial agent memory block; delete or quarantine the episode tests.

**Strengths.**

- Insert and delete are transactional across memories, vec_memories, vec_memories_v2, memory_links and the session-summary FK detach (store.ts:360-409, 510-540), with regression tests pinning both directions (embedding-v2-cascade-delete.test.ts:106-130, 375-426).
- Boot-time and pre-linker integrity repair (store.ts:1217-1270; session-memory.ts:80-93; auto-linker.ts:55) removes the orphan classes that wedged maintenance for months, and checkIntegrity (1140-1202) gives operators a census.
- Every schema migration is idempotent with PRAGMA table_info or sqlite_master guards, FTS backfills are gated on first creation, and the memories_fts triggers are re-asserted each boot (store.ts:1747-1758, 1930-1977, 2257-2284).
- Cold is a tier inside SQLite: rows keep vectors, stay searchable, and are lazily re-warmed on use (tier-manager.ts:7-17; store.ts:1471-1489), so the agent no longer silently forgets after four months.
- Tier maintenance isolates per-row failures, reads least-recently-used candidates first with backlink counts from a single JOIN, and protects graph hubs from cooling (tier-manager.ts:560-627; store.ts:1395-1462).
- The v2 migration is a guarded state machine with a resumable cursor, a nanoid cursor-reset fix, chunk backfill before auto-advance, a rollback guard that checks v1 rows exist, and distance normalization at the store boundary so no consumer re-tunes thresholds (embedding-v2.ts:91-123, 244-254; embedding-v2-runner.ts:156-201; embedding-version.ts:88-107).
- Use-after-close is handled everywhere a background writer can race shutdown (store.ts:643-645; embedding-v2-dual-write.ts:98-102; tier-manager.ts:442, 563).
- Retrieval-side touchAccessedAt resets the decay clock without inflating the promotion counter, and getMemoryForRetrieval is deliberately side-effect free (store.ts:1291-1310, 2685-2694).
- error-guard.ts:48-56 treats empty or short summaries as unusable, closing the data-loss path where consolidation archived live logs for an empty digest.
- The test suite encodes production incidents by date and agent (store-integrity.test.ts, store-orphan-cleanup.test.ts, embedding-v2-migration.test.ts:494), which makes regressions legible.

### Retrieval, ranking, embeddings, hybrid search, reranking (src/memory/memory-retrieval.ts and its ranker, embedder, graph, chunker, eval and embed-worker dependencies, plus the SessionManager/TurnDispatcher/config wiring that drives them)

**How it works.** Pre-turn retrieval starts in TurnDispatcher.augmentWithMemoryContext (src/manager/turn-dispatcher.ts:1238). RetrievalCadenceGate (src/manager/memory-context-augmenter.ts:84-127) decides whether to refresh: turn 1, every 3 turns, or when the MiniLM cosine between this query and the last refreshed query drops below 0.75. On refresh it calls the closure built by SessionManager.getMemoryRetrieverForAgent (src/manager/session-manager.ts:839-948), which resolves topK (memoryRetrievalTopK, default 5), tokenBudget (1500), excludeTags, the reranker block, document priority (ingestionPriority weight 1.5/1.0/0.7, 2-day recency window) and documentChunkCap (2), then calls retrieveMemoryChunks (src/memory/memory-retrieval.ts:391) with timeWindowDays hard-coded to 14 (:939).

retrieveMemoryChunks embeds the query once in the agent's read space (:406; store.embedQuery picks v1 MiniLM float32 or v2 bge-small int8 from the migrations row, src/memory/store.ts:849-866). It then runs four top-20 searches (:419-426): vec_memory_chunks cosine, memory_chunks_fts BM25, vec_memories cosine (JOIN memories, carrying importance/accessed_at/tier), and memories_fts BM25. FTS input is rewritten by buildFtsMatchQuery (src/memory/fts-query.ts:57) into quoted OR-terms after lowercasing, stopword removal and a 12-term cap, against porter-tokenized indexes (store.ts:2211-2215, 2262-2265). Chunk candidates are fused with position-based RRF, k=60 (:215-238). Memory candidates are first re-sorted by 0.7 * (1 - distance) + 0.3 * importance * 0.5^(days since accessed_at / 30) (:443-459) and that order is RRF-fused with the keyword list (:476-480). Hydration (:500-563) adds path/heading/body, adds the path weight to the chunk score (:510; vault +0.2, procedures +0.1, archive -0.2 from src/memory/memory-chunks.ts:150-154), synthesizes path memory:<id> and file_mtime_ms = now for memories (:554-557), and drops memories whose tags intersect excludeTags (:545-551; default session-summary, mid-session, raw-fallback). applyTimeWindowFilter (memory-chunks.ts:170-182) removes dated chunks older than 14 days unless the path is standing (vault, procedures, digests, document:, cue files; :197-204). The list is sorted by fusedScore (:586); document: candidates are multiplied by agentWeight * content_priority_weight * 1.3 recency (:121-202). If the reranker is enabled (schema default true) the top 20 are rescored by Xenova/bge-reranker-base (src/memory/reranker.ts:184-243) under a 500 ms Promise.race with fallback to input order, and the top finalTopK (5) are kept (:632-642). The document cap then limits document: chunks (:657-669), the list is sliced to topK and accumulated until body chars exceed tokenBudget * 4, always emitting the first (:676-694). Emitted memories get accessed_at touched (:701-713). SessionManager then appends up to 3 owner facts from SharedFactsStore with cosine >= 0.5 (session-manager.ts:685-703). renderMemoryContext wraps everything as a memory-context block with one "### heading-or-path" section per hit and prepends it to the user message (memory-context-augmenter.ts:130-140); summarizeRecall records a reply footer (src/manager/recall-footer.ts:62).

Document chunks (document:<slug> rows in memory_chunks) ride the same pipeline. Conversation history (conversation_turns_fts, session_archive_fts) does not: it is reachable only through memory_lookup (src/manager/memory-lookup-handler.ts:202-234). The lazy clawcode_memory_search tool calls the same function with no window and a 4000-token budget, but without reranker, document priority or cap (src/memory/tools/clawcode-memory-search.ts:120-143). memory_lookup scope=memories uses GraphSearch (src/memory/graph-search.ts:54): SemanticSearch KNN with 2x over-fetch, decay blend and an importance multiplier (src/memory/search.ts:93-146), plus 1-hop wikilink and auto:similar neighbors above cosine 0.3, capped at 5 neighbors and 15 total. Auto-links are produced by src/memory/similarity.ts (KNN 6, threshold 0.6, auto_linked_at cursor). The embed worker (src/manager/embed-worker.ts, embed-worker-client.ts) serves auto-ingest only; query embeddings run inline on the main thread. If the embedder throws, the whole retrieval throws and the dispatcher fails open to no context (turn-dispatcher.ts:1297-1303).

**Data flow.** Writes. memory/**/*.md files enter through MemoryScanner.indexFile (src/memory/memory-scanner.ts:205-258): chunkMarkdownByH2 splits on H2 (memory-chunks.ts:42), scoreWeightForPath assigns the path weight (:150), EmbeddingService.embed embeds heading + body (embedder.ts:122, truncated to 200 words at :51/:223), and MemoryStore.insertMemoryChunk writes memory_files, memory_chunks, vec_memory_chunks and memory_chunks_fts in one transaction (store.ts:2400-2445) with a best-effort v2 write to vec_memory_chunks_v2 (:2447-2449). Cross-ingested documents land in the same tables with path document:<slug> (src/document-ingest/cross-ingest.ts). Agent memories (memory_save, flushes, summaries) go through MemoryStore.insert into memories and vec_memories (+ vec_memories_v2 during dual-write); memories_fts is kept in sync by AFTER INSERT/DELETE/UPDATE OF content triggers (store.ts:2262-2276) and backfilled once (:2278-2283). Auto-ingest embeddings are computed off-thread by EmbedWorkerClient.embedBothVersions (embed-worker-client.ts:150-179) and written by the main thread.

Reads. Pre-turn: TurnDispatcher.augmentWithMemoryContext -> RetrievalCadenceGate.shouldRefresh -> SessionManager.getMemoryRetrieverForAgent closure -> retrieveMemoryChunks, which calls store.embedQuery, store.searchMemoryChunksVec (store.ts:2551), store.searchMemoryChunksFts (:2581), store.searchMemoriesVec (:2636), store.searchMemoriesFts (:2607), store.getMemoryChunk (:2740), store.getMemoryForRetrieval (:2695), rerankTop, and store.touchAccessedAt (:1302). SessionManager.appendOwnerFacts adds SharedFactsStore.search hits. The result leaves as text: renderMemoryContext prepends the block to the user message (mutable suffix, not the cached prefix); summarizeRecall stores a RecallRecord in TurnDispatcher.pendingRecall that the Discord bridge renders as a footer; the memory_retrieval and context_assemble trace spans carry counts and token size only. Lazy: MCP clawcode_memory_search (src/mcp/server.ts:1739-1749) -> clawcodeMemorySearch -> retrieveMemoryChunks (no window, budget 4000) -> 500-char snippets with ids for clawcode_memory_recall. Explicit: memory_lookup IPC -> invokeMemoryLookup -> GraphSearch.search (SemanticSearch over vec_memories + memory_links via getForwardLinks/getBacklinks, graph.ts:123-144; bumps access_count on seeds and neighbors) or searchByScope (src/memory/conversation-search.ts: substring scan of the 200 newest memories + session-summary tag lookup + conversation_turns_fts + session_archive_fts). memory-search IPC (daemon.ts:15924-15956) -> SemanticSearch.search. Nothing in this subsystem writes back except accessed_at/access_count bookkeeping and auto:similar edges from similarity.ts.

**Config knobs parsed but not wired at runtime.**

- `memory.decay.halfLifeDays / semanticWeight / decayWeight`: Parsed (src/memory/schema.ts:112-116) and read only by TierManager (session-memory.ts:149-153). No retrieval ranker reads it: retrieveMemoryChunks uses DEFAULT_MEMORY_SCORING (memory-retrieval.ts:367-371), GraphSearch and the memory-search IPC construct SemanticSearch without a scoringConfig (graph-search.ts:63, daemon.ts:15938). RetrieveArgs.scoring is never passed by any caller.
- `memory.searchTopK`: Parsed at src/memory/schema.ts:105; no runtime reader anywhere (only test fixtures and the type).

**Issues.**

- [high/bug] **Path score_weight (+0.2/+0.1/-0.2) is 6x the maximum RRF score, so it dominates ranking instead of nudging**
  Evidence: memory-retrieval.ts:224 `(scoreMap.get(r.chunk_id) ?? 0) + 1 / (k + i + 1)` with k=60 gives at most 1/61 = 0.0164 per ranker and 0.0328 for a chunk found by both. memory-retrieval.ts:510 `fusedScore: f.score + meta.score_weight` adds memory-chunks.ts:151-153 `return 0.2` (vault), `0.1` (procedures), `-0.2` (archive). Memories get `scoreWeight: 0` (:559). The code's own comments claim the opposite: memory-retrieval.ts:65-66 'small additive weights (scoreWeight <= 0.2) act as tiebreakers rather than dominators' and memory-chunks.ts:147-148 'Weights are small (+-0.2) so they nudge, not dominate. RRF k=60 already compresses the score range to roughly [0, 0.033] per ranker'. The sort at :586 and the reranker slice `prioritized.slice(0, args.reranker.topNToRerank)` at :633 both operate on this inflated score. The only test (memory-retrieval.test.ts:170-202, MEM-03-R6) checks vault beats archive at equal distance, never that relevance can beat the weight.
  Impact: Because KNN always returns the 20 nearest chunks regardless of absolute similarity, any vault chunk in the vec or FTS top-20 (even at rank 20, score 0.0125 + 0.2) outranks every memory and every non-vault chunk; procedures chunks outrank all memories; archive chunks can never surface while any other candidate exists. With the reranker on, only the top 20 by this sort are rescored, so an agent with 20 or more vault/procedure chunks in play never gets its saved memories into the rerank window. The agent is shown standing rules it already has in MEMORY.md instead of the relevant memory.
  Fix: Make the weight proportional to the RRF scale: either `scoreWeight * 0.02` (about one rank step) or multiplicative `f.score * (1 + scoreWeight)`. Add a test where a rank-1 dated chunk must outrank a rank-20 vault chunk and a rank-1 memory must outrank a rank-10 vault chunk.
- [high/design-gap] **14-day time window is applied after a fixed top-20 fetch, so recall degrades as the dated-note corpus grows**
  Evidence: memory-retrieval.ts:420-421 `args.store.searchMemoryChunksVec(qEmb, 20)` and `searchMemoryChunksFts(args.query, 20)` run over all chunks; store.ts:2566-2571 `SELECT chunk_id, distance FROM vec_memory_chunks WHERE embedding MATCH ? AND k = ? ORDER BY distance` has no mtime predicate; the filter runs afterwards at memory-retrieval.ts:585 `applyTimeWindowFilter(hydrated, windowDays, now)`. Every session flush writes a new dated file (memory-flush.ts:262-277, `${date}-${hhmm}.md`), and the scanner indexes all of them (memory-scanner.ts:163-172).
  Impact: Older flush notes are semantically close to newer ones about the same topics, so the 20 nearest chunks fill up with out-of-window rows; after filtering, the in-window candidate set shrinks toward zero even when relevant in-window chunks exist at rank 21+. Recall of recent dated notes gets worse the longer the agent lives, which is the opposite of persistence.
  Fix: When windowDays > 0, over-fetch (k = 100) and filter before fusion, or push the predicate into SQL by joining memory_chunks and adding `(file_mtime_ms >= ? OR path LIKE '%/memory/vault/%' OR ...)` to both the vec and FTS queries. Keep the post-fetch filter as a safety net.
- [high/design-gap] **Embedding covers only the first 200 words of a chunk that can hold 800-1000 tokens**
  Evidence: embedder.ts:51 `const MAX_WORDS = 200;` and :139 `const truncated = truncateText(text);` (same in embedder-bge-small.ts:55, :109). memory-chunks.ts:21 `export const DEFAULT_MAX_TOKENS = 800;` and :24 `HARD_CAP_RATIO = 1.25` (1000 tokens, about 4000 chars, roughly 600 words). memory-scanner.ts:236-237 `const textToEmbed = (c.heading ? `${c.heading}\n\n` : "") + c.body; const embedding = await this.deps.embed(textToEmbed);`. The reranker also truncates: node_modules/@huggingface/transformers/src/pipelines/text-classification.js:90-92 `this.tokenizer(texts, { padding: true, truncation: true`.
  Impact: Facts in the back two thirds of a long H2 section are invisible to the vector ranker and to the cross-encoder; only an exact-ish keyword hit through FTS can surface them, and then the reranker scores them on text that does not contain the fact. Long sections (the common shape for MEMORY-style notes and flush summaries) are systematically under-recalled.
  Fix: Lower the chunker soft cap to about 200 tokens (DEFAULT_MAX_TOKENS = 220, hard cap 1.25x) so a chunk fits the embedding window, or embed several 200-word windows per chunk and store the max-similarity row. Re-index via the sha256 path (bump the chunker version into the hash input).
- [medium/inert-config] **memoryRetrievalTopK is silently capped by reranker.finalTopK, and documentChunkCap loses eviction-with-replacement when the reranker is on**
  Evidence: memory-retrieval.ts:633-641: `const candidates = prioritized.slice(0, args.reranker.topNToRerank); const reranked = await rerankTop<Hydrated>(args.query, candidates, { topK: Math.min(args.reranker.finalTopK, candidates.length), ... }); postRerank = [...reranked];` then :657-669 filters document chunks out of that 5-element list and :678 `const limited = capped.slice(0, topK);`. Schema defaults: schema.ts:4780 `enabled: z.boolean().default(true)`, :4782 `finalTopK: ...default(5)`. The comment at :649-656 promises 'eviction-with-replacement, not bare trimming' but memory-retrieval-document-cap.test.ts:87-94 runs without a reranker, so the production path is untested.
  Impact: An operator who raises memoryRetrievalTopK to 10 still gets 5. With three or more document: chunks among the five reranked hits and cap=2, the emitted set shrinks to four or fewer with nothing replacing the evicted rows, so the cap that was meant to free slots for conversational memory instead just removes context.
  Fix: Rerank `topNToRerank` candidates but keep `max(topK, finalTopK) + documentChunkCap` of them, or run the cap pass on `prioritized` before slicing to the reranker; add a test that enables the reranker with 5 document chunks, cap 2 and topK 5 and asserts 5 results.
- [medium/never-injected] **createdAt is hydrated but dropped from the output object, so the recall footer never shows dates**
  Evidence: memory-retrieval.ts:561 `createdAt: meta.created_at,` goes into Hydrated, but the emitted object at :682-691 is `Object.freeze({ chunkId: h.chunkId, path: h.path, heading: h.heading, body: h.body, fusedScore: h.fusedScore, scoreWeight: h.scoreWeight, source: h.source, })` with no createdAt. The type at :55-59 documents the field 'for the recall footer's date range'. recall-footer.ts:74 `...(c.createdAt ? { createdAt: c.createdAt } : {})` and :106 `.map((m) => (m.createdAt ? new Date(m.createdAt).getTime() : NaN))`. recall-footer.test.ts:16-24 builds chunks by hand with createdAt and never runs summarizeRecall on real retrieval output.
  Impact: The Phase 999.124 footer always renders as '*recalled N memories*' with no '(Jun 12 to Aug 30)' range and the verbose block has no per-memory dates, so the operator cannot see how stale a recalled memory was, which was the feature's stated purpose. It also removes the only date the model-facing block could have used.
  Fix: Add `...(h.createdAt ? { createdAt: h.createdAt } : {})` to the emitted object at memory-retrieval.ts:683 and add a test that feeds retrieveMemoryChunks output into summarizeRecall and asserts a date range.
- [medium/silent-failure] **Reranker timeouts and errors are never logged in production; whether reranking runs at all is unobservable**
  Evidence: session-manager.ts:963-969 declares the resolver type as `{ enabled: boolean; topNToRerank: number; finalTopK: number; timeoutMs: number; }` and :942 passes `reranker: rerankerCfg,` with no `logger`. reranker.ts:232 `opts.logger?.warn?.(` and :218 `opts.logger?.info?.(` are therefore no-ops. The budget is schema.ts:4782 `timeoutMs: ...default(500)` for up to 20 pairs whose passages are full chunk bodies (`getText: (h) => h.body`, memory-retrieval.ts:639) through a q8 bge-reranker-base cross-encoder on CPU. On timeout the inference is not cancelled (reranker.ts:199-208 Promise.race only). The only production log is the boot warmup failure (daemon.ts:6057-6061).
  Impact: If the 500 ms budget is routinely missed, every refresh turn pays the full inference cost and then uses the RRF order anyway, and nobody can tell; the operator believes a cross-encoder is correcting the (issue 1 distorted) RRF ranking when it is not.
  Fix: Pass `logger: this.log.child({ agent: agentName })` in the retriever closure (session-manager.ts:942) and add a counter for applied vs fallback; measure p50 rerank latency on the production box and set timeoutMs/topNToRerank from the measurement.
- [medium/design-gap] **An embedder failure throws before the FTS searches run, so keyword recall is lost along with vector recall**
  Evidence: memory-retrieval.ts:406 `const qEmb = await args.embed(args.query);` is unguarded and precedes the Promise.all at :419. embedder.ts:146-150 wraps any pipeline error as `throw new EmbeddingError(...)`. turn-dispatcher.ts:1297-1303 catches it and returns the message unchanged ('memory retrieval failed - continuing without context (fail-open)'); clawcode-memory-search.ts:120 has no catch, so the lazy tool surfaces the error to the agent.
  Impact: During a model download failure, an ONNX crash, or the warmup window after a daemon restart, the agent gets no memory at all even though memory_chunks_fts and memories_fts need no embedding and could still answer exact-term questions.
  Fix: Wrap the embed call; on failure log once and set vecTop and memoriesTop to [] while still running both FTS searches, then continue the pipeline (RRF handles single-ranker input).
- [medium/design-gap] **The injected block carries no provenance or freshness, and dated chunks have no recency signal beyond the binary window**
  Evidence: memory-context-augmenter.ts:133-139 renders `### ${heading}\n${c.body}` where heading falls back to path; memories render as `memory:<id>` (memory-retrieval.ts:554-555). Chunk score is RRF + path weight only (:510); memories always pass the window because :557 sets `file_mtime_ms: now`; the memories decay term uses `accessed_at` (:453), not created_at. No date, source, importance or tier reaches the prompt.
  Impact: The model cannot tell a two-year-old memory from yesterday's, nor a flush note from an operator document, so it cannot resolve conflicting recollections by recency or weight an old low-importance memory lower; a 13-day-old note and today's note tie exactly on the chunk side.
  Fix: Render `### memory:<id> (saved <date>)` and `### <basename> > <heading> (modified <date>)` using createdAt/file_mtime_ms (needs issue 5 fixed and file_mtime_ms carried into the result), and multiply dated-path chunk scores by a mild continuous decay (0.5^(ageDays/14)).
- [medium/design-gap] **No contradiction or supersession handling and no cross-surface dedup at retrieval time**
  Evidence: memory-retrieval.ts:498-563 hydrates chunk-side and memory-side candidates independently and :676-694 emits them in order; nothing compares bodies. The same content legitimately exists twice: a cue file (`## Standing note\n\n${args.context}`, memory-cue.ts:124-128) and a memory_save of the same text, or a digest chunk and the memories it summarizes. The only dedup is insertion-time `INSERT OR IGNORE` on origin_id (store.ts:2838-2842) and the store's similarity dedup on insert, neither of which marks an older memory as superseded.
  Impact: Two contradictory memories (an old allocation and a later correction) are both injected with equal standing and no ordering cue; duplicate text consumes the 6000-char budget that typically fits only two or three chunks.
  Fix: At emission, skip a candidate whose normalized body prefix matches an already-emitted one or whose stored vector cosine to an emitted candidate is >= 0.95; longer term add a `superseded_by` column that memory edits set and that getMemoryForRetrieval filters on.
- [medium/inert-config] **memory.decay and memory.searchTopK are parsed but do not reach any retrieval ranker; RetrieveArgs.scoring is dead**
  Evidence: src/memory/schema.ts:105 `searchTopK: z.number().int().min(1).default(10),` and :112-116 `decay: decayConfigSchema.default(...)`. The only reader of decay is session-memory.ts:149-153 (TierManager). retrieveMemoryChunks uses `const scoring = args.scoring ?? DEFAULT_MEMORY_SCORING;` (memory-retrieval.ts:443) and no caller passes `scoring` (grep over src: none). graph-search.ts:63 `new SemanticSearch(this.store.getDatabase())` and daemon.ts:15938 `new SemanticSearch(store.getDatabase())` omit the scoringConfig argument. searchTopK has no runtime reader at all. memory_lookup's scoped path uses a third constant, conversation.retrievalHalfLifeDays = 14 (memory-lookup-handler.ts:229).
  Impact: An operator tuning memory.decay changes which memories go cold but not which ones the agent is shown; the three retrieval paths silently run three different decay settings (30 / 30 / 14 days).
  Fix: Thread `config.memory.decay` into getMemoryRetrieverForAgent as `scoring` and into both `new SemanticSearch(db, scoring)` sites; remove searchTopK from the schema or wire it to the lazy tool's default k.
- [medium/design-gap] **Three retrieval paths use three different rankers; memory_lookup cannot do keyword recall and its scope=all memory path is a substring scan of the 200 newest rows**
  Evidence: memory-lookup-handler.ts:177-178 `const graphSearch = new GraphSearch(memoryStore); const results = graphSearch.search(queryEmbedding, limit);` (vector + links only, no memories_fts). conversation-search.ts:17-19 'MVP matching strategy is a case-insensitive substring match over memory content' and :112-117 `deps.memoryStore.listRecent(MEMORY_CANDIDATE_POOL)` with `MEMORY_CANDIDATE_POOL = 200` then `contentMatches(m.content, normalizedQuery)`. graph-search.ts:155-157 gives neighbors `relevanceScore: info.similarity, combinedScore: info.similarity` (raw cosine) while KNN rows carry the 0.7/0.3 blend times `(0.7 + 0.3 * importance)` (search.ts:122), and :167 concatenates them unsorted.
  Impact: The ticker-style exact token that the pre-turn and lazy paths find (memory-retrieval-999187-keyword.test.ts:49-55) is unreachable through memory_lookup; in scope=all, any memory older than the 200 newest is never considered; relevance_score values returned to the agent are not comparable between rows.
  Fix: Route the legacy scope=memories branch through retrieveMemoryChunks (timeWindowDays 0) or at least fuse searchMemoriesFts into GraphSearch; replace the substring scan with searchMemoriesVec + searchMemoriesFts over the whole table; sort neighbors into the KNN list on one scale.
- [medium/data-loss] **Chunker discards all content before the first H2, including the lead paragraph of flush summaries**
  Evidence: memory-chunks.ts:46-47 'all content before the first H2 is discarded' and :82-83 `if (!seenFirstH2) return;`. Flush files are `# Session Flush ${date} ...\n\n${summary}` (memory-flush.ts:274-276) where the summarizer is asked for 'markdown sections' (memory-flush.ts:45) and the summarizer templates use H2 headings (session-summarizer.ts:89-98). memory-chunks.test.ts:10-18 only asserts the two H2 chunks exist, not that pre-H2 prose is kept.
  Impact: Whenever a summary (or an operator-authored note) opens with a gist paragraph before its first '## ' heading, that paragraph is never indexed by either ranker, so the most condensed statement of the session is the part that cannot be recalled.
  Fix: In chunkMarkdownByH2, emit the pre-H2 region as a chunk with heading = the H1 text (or null) when its trimmed body is non-empty, and add a test for it.
- [low/silent-failure] **FTS and memories-vec store searches swallow every error and return []**
  Evidence: store.ts:2597-2599 `} catch { return []; }` (searchMemoryChunksFts), :2620-2622 same (searchMemoriesFts), :2680-2686 `catch { // Empty table or no matches ... return []; }` (searchMemoriesVec). searchMemoryChunksVec (:2551-2574) has no catch, so the two vec paths behave differently.
  Impact: A missing memories_fts table, a malformed MATCH, or a v1/v2 dimension mismatch on vec_memories during a migration mishap looks exactly like 'nothing relevant'; the memories side of pre-turn retrieval can be dead for an agent with no log line.
  Fix: Keep the fail-open return but log once per process per error message (a small Set of seen messages) at warn level.
- [low/performance] **The query is embedded up to three times per refresh turn, all inline on the main thread**
  Evidence: Cadence gate embeds with v1: daemon.ts:4050 `queryEmbedder: (q: string) => manager.getEmbedder().embed(q)` used at memory-context-augmenter.ts:109 and :120. The retriever embeds again: session-manager.ts:940 `embed: (text: string) => store.embedQuery(embedder, text)`. appendOwnerFacts embeds a third time with v1: session-manager.ts:695 `const embedding = await this.memory.embedder.embed(query);`. EmbedWorkerClient (embed-worker-client.ts:150) is used only by auto-ingest.
  Impact: Two or three ONNX inferences on the event loop per refresh turn (the blocking mode the worker was built to avoid, embed-worker.ts:4-9), two of them computing the identical v1 vector.
  Fix: Have the gate return its embedding in the verdict and pass it through to the retriever closure (which can reuse it for the v1 case and for owner facts); optionally route query embeds through EmbedWorkerClient.
- [low/design-gap] **accessed_at is touched for every injected memory regardless of use, and owner facts bypass the token budget**
  Evidence: memory-retrieval.ts:701-713 touches accessed_at for every emitted memory; :451-457 then rewards recent accessed_at with weight 0.3. session-manager.ts:694-696 `const facts = ownerFactsAsChunks(store, owner, embedding); return facts.length > 0 ? [...chunks, ...facts] : chunks;` runs after retrieveMemoryChunks already applied the budget at :676-694; SHARED_FACTS_RETRIEVAL_K = 3 (shared-facts-store.ts:26).
  Impact: The same handful of memories keeps re-qualifying because being shown counts as being used; up to three extra facts can push the block past memoryRetrievalTokenBudget.
  Fix: Touch only on 'first' and 'topic-shift' refreshes (or when the model later cites the id), and append owner facts before the budget loop by passing them into retrieveMemoryChunks as extra candidates.
- [low/performance] **GraphSearch re-prepares statements per call and expands every link of hub nodes before capping**
  Evidence: graph-search.ts:63 `const semanticSearch = new SemanticSearch(this.store.getDatabase());` prepares three statements on every search (search.ts:60-80); :146-151 `db.prepare("SELECT id, content, tags, created_at FROM memories WHERE id = ?")` inside the neighbor map; :74-75 `getForwardLinks` / `getBacklinks` return every edge (graph.ts:123-144) and :91 fetches and dequantizes a vector for each unseen neighbor before `.slice(0, this.config.maxNeighbors)` at :112.
  Impact: A memory with hundreds of auto:similar edges makes each memory_lookup do hundreds of vector reads to keep five; prepare churn adds latency on every explicit lookup.
  Fix: Cache the SemanticSearch instance per GraphSearch, prepare the neighbor row statement once, and add LIMIT to the link queries (or stop after maxNeighbors * 4 candidates).
- [low/test-gap] **Key ranking interactions have no test coverage**
  Evidence: No test asserts path weight magnitude relative to RRF (memory-retrieval.test.ts:170-202 only checks vault beats archive at equal distance); memory-retrieval-document-cap.test.ts:87-94 never enables the reranker; recall-footer.test.ts:16-24 hand-builds chunks with createdAt; no test exercises embed failure inside retrieveMemoryChunks; embedder.test.ts:56-79 asserts 200-word truncation but nothing relates it to DEFAULT_MAX_TOKENS; the recall eval fixture is 12 files, 4 memories, 15 queries with a hash embedder (recall-eval-999187.test.ts:30-32, fixtures/recall-eval.json).
  Impact: Issues 1, 4, 5 and 7 above all passed CI; the eval cannot detect the embedding-window or weight-dominance regressions because every fixture document is short and no fixture has a vault file competing with a memory.
  Fix: Add the four targeted tests named in issues 1, 4, 5 and 7, and extend recall-eval.json with one long multi-paragraph file, one vault file, and a query whose answer is a saved memory competing against that vault chunk.

**Strengths.**

- RRF fusion is implemented correctly as position-based with the canonical k=60, and both surfaces (file chunks and saved memories) now get a vector and a keyword ranker each (memory-retrieval.ts:215-238, 419-426, 476-480).
- fts-query.ts fixes the implicit-AND dead-keyword problem with stopword removal, a term cap and quoted terms that neutralize FTS5 syntax; the store rebuilds pre-porter indexes once and re-asserts the memories_fts triggers on every boot with a one-time backfill (store.ts:2222-2283).
- Embedding-space handling is careful: QueryVector tags prevent cross-space queries, v2 distances are mapped onto the v1 scale at the store boundary so every threshold keeps its meaning, and the read version is looked up per query so a migration cutover takes effect immediately (embedding-version.ts, store.ts:849-866).
- The pre-turn path is fail-open end to end, always emits at least one hit under the budget, returns frozen results, and the cadence gate bounds transcript growth while still refreshing on a topic shift (memory-context-augmenter.ts:84-127).
- The reranker is defensively built: hardcoded model id, no query or passage text in logs, Promise.race timeout with order-preserving fallback, env kill switch, non-blocking warmup at boot, and a DI hook so integration tests never load the model (reranker.ts).
- Standing-memory paths (vault, procedures, digests, document:, cue files) are an explicit, tested allow-list, and the lazy clawcode_memory_search tool deliberately runs without a window so older context stays reachable (memory-chunks.ts:197-204, clawcode-memory-search.ts:132-136).
- EmbedWorkerClient has a clear failure policy (boot fail-open to inline, runtime respawn without inline, per-request timeout to free the governor permit) with an identity guard against respawn cascades, and the test file covers each branch (embed-worker-client.ts, embed-worker-client.test.ts).
- Int8 quantization uses a shared fixed range, which is the correct pattern for sqlite-vec native int8 distance, with clamping and a recallLossEstimate helper for pre-cutover sampling (embedder-quantize.ts).
- The auto-linker was hardened well: cursor-based candidates, per-candidate transactions, and a JOIN that removes ghost vectors before the FK insert (similarity.ts:8-27, 145-187, 259-281).
- A deterministic recall eval with asserted floors exists in CI, which is the first quality signal the retrieval path has had (recall-eval.ts, recall-eval-999187.test.ts).
- getMemoryForRetrieval deliberately avoids bumping access_count, and touchAccessedAt is kept separate from bumpAccess, so pre-turn injection does not pollute hot-tier promotion (store.ts:2688-2693, 1295-1309).

### Context assembly and injection (src/manager/context-assembler.ts, session-config.ts, memory-context-augmenter.ts, recall-footer.ts, session-memory.ts, memory-lookup-handler.ts, memory-graph-handler.ts, mcp-prompt-block.ts, persona-files.ts, topic-profiles.ts, topic-prompt.ts)

**How it works.** BOOT. `buildSessionConfig` (session-config.ts:494) runs once per session start (session-manager.ts:1320), on registry reconcile (:3027), and for voice builds (:3354). It is NOT per turn. It collects: SOUL via `readSoulContent` (persona-files.ts:45) and keeps only the fingerprint (fingerprint.ts:62, <=1200 chars) at :594-599; IDENTITY.md in full at :605; an agent-name line plus `buildCapabilityManifest` (capability-manifest.ts:64, memory-protocol prose at :305-310) at :613-626; MEMORY.md from `resolveMemoryRoots` (memory-roots.ts:57) with legacy-path fallback, capped at `injectedMemoryMaxChars` (16,000 chars) by 70/20 head-tail truncation at :663-762; USER.md appended INTO the same string under "## About the user" with a 4,000-char cap at :772-800; the top 3 hot-tier memories by importance from `TierManager.getHotMemories` (tier-manager.ts:521) at :831; a skills header of one bullet per skill (fullContent is the same bullet, :862-870); tool text = subagent-thread guidance, the capability-probe-filtered MCP table via `renderMcpPromptBlock` (mcp-prompt-block.ts:127), three built-in helper lines (:980), the admin agent table and subagent-model line (:896-1012); the Discord steer block (:1015-1075); the resume summary from `<memoryPath>/memory/context-summary.md` via `loadLatestSummary` + `enforceSummaryBudget` (context-summary.ts:161/210) at :1084-1104; the "## Recent Sessions" brief from the last 3 session-summary memories when the gap >= 4h (conversation-brief.ts:133) at :1120-1165; and the fs-capability block at :1187.

ASSEMBLY. `assembleContextInternal` (context-assembler.ts:1182) enforces per-section budgets (DEFAULT_PHASE53_BUDGETS :560: identity 4000, skills 1500, hot 1000 tokens). Identity uses `enforceDropLowestImportance` (:846): truncate MEMORY.md(+USER.md) first, then the capability manifest, then IDENTITY.md; the SOUL fingerprint is never cut. Hot memories are importance-sorted (:1048). In the default "static-first" placement the stable prefix is: [directives (never populated), identity, "## Available Tools" (skills + tools, re-truncated by the legacy 2000-token chars/4 budget :1418), `<tool_status>`/fs block/`<dream_log_recent>` triplet (:1451), delegates, Discord bindings (:1480)] + `<!-- phase115-cache-breakpoint -->` + ["## Key Memories" (:1406), "## Related Context" (never populated)] (:1612-1650), then an 8K-token emergency head-tail cap (:983). The mutable suffix is [Key Memories only when the hot stable-token mismatched (:1404)] + "## Session Continuity" = resume summary + brief (:1523).

DELIVERY. `systemPrompt` becomes `{type:"preset", preset:"claude_code", append, excludeDynamicSections}` (session-adapter.ts:1536/1690), so the whole stable prefix sits in the SDK-cached system prompt. `mutableSuffix` is prepended to the FIRST user message only (persistent-session-handle.ts:927-933) and re-armed once after a compaction `swap()` (:3283); voice sessions drop it entirely (voice-session-base-options.ts:167).

PER TURN. `TurnDispatcher.augmentWithMemoryContext` (turn-dispatcher.ts:1238) asks `RetrievalCadenceGate.shouldRefresh` (memory-context-augmenter.ts:84: turn 1, every 3 turns, or query cosine < 0.75 vs the last retrieval), then calls `getMemoryRetrieverForAgent` (session-manager.ts:840) -> `retrieveMemoryChunks` (memory-retrieval.ts:391: vec + FTS over `memory_chunks` from the memory/*.md scanner, which excludes MEMORY.md itself (memory-scanner.ts:48), fused with the `memories` table, RRF k=60, excludeTags session-summary/mid-session/raw-fallback, 14-day window for chunks, doc-chunk cap 2, topK 5, 1500-token budget) plus owner shared facts (session-manager.ts:688). `renderMemoryContext` (:130) wraps hits as `<memory-context source="hybrid-rrf">` and prepends it to the USER MESSAGE, never the system prompt; the hit list also feeds the Discord recall footer (recall-footer.ts, consumed at bridge.ts:5638-5649). v3 agents get the same augmenter (daemon.ts:11761) but their system prompt is only the channel protocol (claude-code-interactive.ts:958-960, :1056).

NOT INJECTED: system-prompt directives (never rendered), documents (only via cross-ingested `document:` chunks in retrieval), conversation summaries beyond the 3-session brief (excluded from retrieval by tag; `memory_lookup` scope "all" only), dream logs/digests (`<dream_log_recent>` is an empty sentinel; the weekly digest goes to Discord), graph context, episodes, the session archive, hot memories beyond the top 3, and for v3 agents everything in Tier 1. memory-lookup-handler.ts and memory-graph-handler.ts are pull-only IPC handlers; topic-profiles/topic-prompt gate a UI trail, not injection.

**Data flow.** ENTERS: files read at session build only: SOUL.md/IDENTITY.md (persona-files.ts:22 readFirstPresent), MEMORY.md/USER.md (session-config.ts:678-700 readTier1File at memory-roots canonical then legacy path), context-summary.md (context-summary.ts:161 loadLatestSummary; written only by session-memory.ts:284 saveContextSummary, whose sole production caller is the heartbeat zone snapshot at daemon.ts:5593 with the placeholder text "Auto-snapshot at NN% context fill"); SQLite memories.db: `memories` rows with tier='hot' (tier-manager.ts:521 listByTier), session-summary-tagged memories (conversation-brief.ts:176 findByTag), conversation_sessions (conversation-store.ts:452 listRecentTerminatedSessions, which requires EXISTS conversation_turns), memory_chunks/vec/fts from the memory/*.md scanner (memory-scanner.ts) and from document cross-ingest (`document:<slug>` paths); shared-facts.db (session-manager.ts:688 appendOwnerFacts via ownerFactsAsChunks); live MCP readiness and fs snapshots via deps providers (session-config.ts:925, :1188). LANDS: `AgentSessionConfig.systemPrompt`/`mutableSuffix`/`hotStableToken` (session-config.ts:1423-1425); SessionManager caches `latestStablePrefixByAgent` and `lastHotStableTokenByAgent` (session-manager.ts:1329-1332); traces.db `context_assemble` span `section_tokens` + `tier1_inject_chars` (context-assembler.ts:1760-1790) and the per-turn `memory_context` span (turn-dispatcher.ts:1197-1210); `touchAccessedAt` on memories that reached the prompt (memory-retrieval.ts:690-700). READS: session-adapter.ts:1690/1927 buildSystemPromptOption -> SDK `systemPrompt.append`; persistent-session-handle.ts:927 promptWithMutable (first turn + post-swap); turn-dispatcher.ts:996/1103 prepend `<memory-context>` to every refresh-turn user message; bridge.ts:5638 recall footer. LEAVES: the Claude Code CLI subprocess request (system prompt + user messages), Discord reply footer, dashboards reading traces.db. Never read back into any prompt: CompactionResult.summary (compaction.ts:157-171, not persisted), EpisodeStore, DocumentStore rows (only their cross-ingested chunks), memory_links graph (memory-graph-handler.ts is dashboard-only), dream files except as scanner chunks, systemPromptDirectives.

**Config knobs parsed but not wired at runtime.**

- `defaults/agents.*.systemPromptDirectives (no-em-dashes, mutate-verify, parallel-tool-calls, discord-format, background-work-status, file-sharing, verify-file-writes, ...)`: Intended to prepend operator directives to the stable prefix; parsed, defaulted, resolved by loader.ts:1828/1872 and classified RELOADABLE at config/types.ts:155-160, but no runtime caller renders or threads them into ContextSources
- `perf.memoryAssemblyBudgets.soul`: Read by mergeBudgets but session-config always passes soul:"" (session-config.ts:1228); no effect
- `perf.memoryAssemblyBudgets.recent_history`: Measured-only section; recentHistory is always "" (session-config.ts:1246); no effect
- `perf.memoryAssemblyBudgets.per_turn_summary`: perTurnSummary is always "" (session-config.ts:1241); no effect
- `perf.memoryAssemblyBudgets.resume_summary`: Assembler treats resume_summary as passthrough; the real cap is perf.resumeSummaryBudget (session-config.ts:1090); no effect
- `agents.*.contextBudgets.identity`: Never read by the assembler (only budgets.toolDefinitions and budgets.graphContext are used, context-assembler.ts:1418/1489)
- `agents.*.contextBudgets.hotMemories`: Never read; hot tier uses memoryAssemblyBudgets.hot_tier
- `agents.*.contextBudgets.graphContext`: Read, but graphContext is always "" (session-config.ts:1234); no effect
- `MEMORY_AUTOLOAD_MAX_BYTES (schema.ts:117)`: Legacy 50KB constant, exported only for a schema test; the assembly path uses INJECTED_MEMORY_MAX_CHARS

**Issues.**

- [high/never-injected] **System-prompt directives are parsed, defaulted and documented as reloadable but never injected into any prompt**
  Evidence: context-assembler.ts:55-70 documents the contract: "Caller (session-config.ts) renders this via renderSystemPromptDirectiveBlock(resolveSystemPromptDirectives(...)) and passes the resulting string." The `sources` literal in session-config.ts:1208-1262 has no `systemPromptDirectives` key, and a repo-wide grep for `renderSystemPromptDirectiveBlock|resolveSystemPromptDirectives` finds only their definitions (config/loader.ts:1828, :1872) and tests. config/types.ts:155-160 nonetheless lists `agents.*.systemPromptDirectives` as RELOADABLE: "a YAML edit takes effect on the NEXT prompt assembly (per-turn boundary - assembler reads via the loader resolver each turn)". schema.ts:189 DEFAULT_SYSTEM_PROMPT_DIRECTIVES ships `no-em-dashes`, `mutate-verify`, `parallel-tool-calls`, `discord-format`, `background-work-status` etc. with `enabled: true`.
  Impact: Every behavioral directive the operator believes is active (mutation read-back, no em dashes, parallel tool calls, background-work ETA) is silently absent from the agent's context; per-agent overrides in clawcode.yaml do nothing. The assembler tests (context-assembler-directives.test.ts) pass because they feed the string directly, so the wiring gap is invisible to CI.
  Fix: In buildSessionConfig compute `systemPromptDirectives: renderSystemPromptDirectiveBlock(resolveSystemPromptDirectives(config.systemPromptDirectives, deps.defaultDirectives ?? DEFAULT_SYSTEM_PROMPT_DIRECTIVES))` and add it to `sources`; thread the fleet defaults through SessionConfigDeps the same way advisorDefaults is. Add one buildSessionConfig test asserting a default directive phrase appears in systemPrompt.
- [high/data-loss] **USER.md is folded into the MEMORY.md sub-source, so identity budget enforcement head-tail truncates the user profile first and drops the agent-actionable truncation marker**
  Evidence: session-config.ts:796-800: `identityMemoryAutoload = (identityMemoryAutoload ? identityMemoryAutoload + "\n\n" : "") + "## About the user (USER.md)\n\n" + userBody;`. context-assembler.ts:900-915 step A: `while (total > budgetTokens && countTokens(memoryAuto) > 100 ...) { const targetChars = Math.max(400, Math.floor(memoryAuto.length * 0.7)); memoryAuto = headTailTruncate(memoryAuto, targetChars); }` and headTailTruncate :810-818 keeps head 70% + tail 20%, dropping the middle. With a 16,000-char MEMORY.md plus a 4,000-char USER.md the USER.md heading sits at offset ~16,000, inside the dropped middle on the first pass; the 16K MEMORY.md alone is ~4,000 tokens, equal to the whole identity budget (DEFAULT_PHASE53_BUDGETS.identity = 4000, :560), before the fingerprint, IDENTITY.md and capability manifest are counted, so step A fires for any agent whose MEMORY.md is near the cap. The read-site marker `[TRUNCATED - N chars dropped, dream-pass priority requested]` (:720) is placed at the 70% point and is itself inside the region the second truncation removes.
  Impact: Agents with a full MEMORY.md lose their curated user profile (or get it headless, mid-sentence) and never see the dream-pass request marker, while the prompt still asserts "Your curated memory is in MEMORY.md and USER.md, always shown above" (capability-manifest.ts:306). Operators cannot see this except via a budget warn log.
  Fix: Carry USER.md as its own ContextSources field (e.g. identityUserAutoload) ranked above MEMORY.md in enforceDropLowestImportance, and make the identity budget default at least cover injectedMemoryMaxChars + injectedUserMaxChars (or lower the char caps so 16K+4K chars fit 4000 tokens). Truncate MEMORY.md by dropping whole H2 sections from the tail instead of the middle so the marker survives.
- [medium/design-gap] **Phase 52 hot-tier stable-token logic is dead on the main path and strips Key Memories entirely on the voice/rebuild path**
  Evidence: context-assembler.ts:1399-1407: `const hotInMutable = priorToken !== undefined && priorToken !== currentHotToken; if (hotInMutable) { mutableParts.push(hotBlock); } else { dynamicParts.push(hotBlock); }`. The token only exists after a prior build for a running agent: session-manager.ts:1330-1332 stores it at startAgent, :2470 deletes it on stop, :3503-3511 threads it back into every later buildSessionConfig. buildSessionConfig is called again for a running agent only by buildVoiceSessionBaseOptions (:3354) and reconcile (:3027). The mutable suffix is prepended on the first turn only (persistent-session-handle.ts:927-933) and voice drops it: voice-session-base-options.ts:167 "Deliberately OMITS advisorModel / mutableSuffix". `refreshHotTier` runs on the heartbeat (tier-manager.ts:436), so the hot composition routinely drifts between start and a later voice build.
  Impact: For the main SDK session the mismatch never occurs (no prior token at start), so the design adds nothing. For a voice query built after hot-tier drift, Key Memories vanish from the prompt altogether (not "for this turn only" as :1395 claims), with no log.
  Fix: Remove the hot-in-mutable branch and always place hot memories in dynamicParts after the cache marker (the marker already isolates churn), or at minimum ignore priorHotStableToken for voice builds and in reconcile.
- [medium/silent-failure] **Emergency 8K outer cap head-tail truncates the whole joined prefix, cutting through tool tables and steers, and is reachable with default budgets**
  Evidence: context-assembler.ts:983-1010: `const targetChars = maxTokens * 4; const truncated = headTailTruncate(joined, targetChars);` applied to the joined stable prefix with a generic `[TRUNCATED - N chars dropped]` marker. Per-section budgets already sum past the cap: identity 4000 + skills_header 1500 + hot_tier 1000 (:560) + the tools block's separate 2000-token budget (:1418) = 8500 tokens before the unbudgeted fs block, delegates and the ~1,900-char Discord steer (session-config.ts:1015-1075). countTokens uses @anthropic-ai/tokenizer v0.0.4 (performance/token-count.ts:20), not the serving model's tokenizer.
  Impact: A fully-populated agent silently loses a random middle slice of its system prompt (typically the MCP status table, built-in helper lines or the Discord delivery steer) on every session; only a log.error line records it, and the cache-breakpoint marker can be among the dropped bytes.
  Fix: Make the fallback shrink the largest budgeted section (MEMORY.md autoload, then hot tier) rather than the joined string, and set the default per-section budgets so their sum plus the fixed blocks stays under STABLE_PREFIX_MAX_TOKENS.
- [medium/never-retrieved] **context-summary.md only ever contains the heartbeat placeholder, yet it is injected at every boot as the previous session's summary and never expires**
  Evidence: The only production writer is daemon.ts:5590-5593: `const summaryMessage = \`Auto-snapshot at ${pct}% context fill [${zone} zone]\`; await manager.saveContextSummary(agentName, summaryMessage);`. compaction.ts:157-171 builds a real `summary` ("Key context from previous session: ...") on CompactionResult, but session-manager.ts:3274 `compactForAgent` returns it and daemon-compact-session-ipc.ts:414 only reads `.summary.length`; grep shows no other saveSummary/saveContextSummary caller. session-config.ts:1084-1104 loads whatever is on disk and injects `## Context Summary (from previous session)` into the Session Continuity block with no age check or clear-after-use.
  Impact: The agent's first message every session carries a stale one-line "Auto-snapshot at 85% context fill [red zone]" presented as prior-session context, while the real compaction summary (extracted facts) is thrown away. The topic-prompt classifier even has to special-case this block (topic-prompt.ts:52-55).
  Fix: Persist `result.summary` in compactForAgent when non-empty (session-manager.ts:3274) and stop writing the zone placeholder (or write it to a different file); delete or timestamp-gate context-summary.md after it is injected.
- [medium/design-gap] **Stable prefix is frozen for the session lifetime: MEMORY.md/USER.md edits, skill re-links and config reloads never reach a running agent's prompt**
  Evidence: session-manager.ts:304-307 says "Skills hot-reload updates this via `refreshStablePrefix` which triggers a fresh buildSessionConfig run", but `refreshStablePrefix` is not defined anywhere (grep returns only that comment). config-reloader.ts:134-157 re-links skill symlinks and logs "skills re-linked" without rebuilding the prompt. persistent-session-handle.ts:3220-3240 `swap()` calls `buildEpoch(newSessionId)` with the handle's original baseOptions, so even a compaction fork keeps the boot-time MEMORY.md. Meanwhile capability-manifest.ts:306 tells the model "Your curated memory is in MEMORY.md and USER.md, always shown above."
  Impact: After an agent appends a fact with clawcode_memory_edit, its own system prompt still shows the old file until restart; the model is told the file is always shown, so it reasons from a stale view and may re-record or contradict facts. Schema comments calling injectedMemoryMaxChars and memoryAutoLoad "reloadable" are only true at the next restart.
  Fix: Re-run buildSessionConfig inside `swap()` (compaction is already a fresh SDK session) and pass the new systemPrompt into buildEpoch; add a cheap mtime check at swap/turn boundaries to decide whether a rebuild is needed.
- [medium/bug] **Brief-cache fingerprint and SESS-03 gap check use a terminated-sessions query that excludes summarized sessions once their turns are pruned**
  Evidence: conversation-store.ts:1198-1210: `WHERE agent_name = ? AND status IN ('ended','crashed','summarized') AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM conversation_turns ct WHERE ct.session_id = conversation_sessions.id LIMIT 1)`. session-summarizer.ts:386 documents that `deleteTurnsForSession` prunes turns after summarization. session-config.ts:1131-1134 fingerprints `convStore.listRecentTerminatedSessions(config.name, sessionCount).map((s) => s.id)` and conversation-brief.ts:150 uses the same list for the gap check. Running the suite shows the symptom: "cache entry with stale fingerprint" fails with received `e3b0c44298fc1c14` (sha256 of an empty list) vs expected `d153a3a4756989f0`.
  Impact: Once all prior sessions are summarized and pruned, the fingerprint is the constant sha256("") so the cached brief never invalidates for a running agent (voice/reconcile rebuilds serve a frozen Recent Sessions block), and the 4-hour gap skip measures against nothing. The two Phase 73 tests fail on a clean checkout.
  Fix: Add a second prepared statement without the EXISTS clause for the brief/fingerprint callers (or fingerprint over the session-summary memory ids returned by findByTag), keep the EXISTS variant for the summary drain.
- [medium/dead-path] **Lazy-skill compression is inert: full and compressed renderings are the same bullet, mention re-inflation sources are always empty, and the usage window is read only at boot**
  Evidence: session-config.ts:862-870: "Full content falls back to the description bullet when a real SKILL.md body is not wired in yet" and `fullContent: bullet`. session-config.ts:1254-1255 passes `currentUserMessage: ""`, `lastAssistantMessage: ""` ("A future per-turn assembler re-call will populate these"). The usage window is read once at :1197 `deps.skillUsageTracker?.getWindow(config.name)`, and session-manager.ts:2473 resets it on stop, so `usage.turns < cfg.usageThresholdTurns` (default 20) holds at every session build and renderSkillsHeader (context-assembler.ts:1140-1144) takes the warm-up path. A second truncation of the combined block at :1416-1419 `truncateToBudget(toolsCombined, budgets.toolDefinitions)` can drop the trailing built-in helper lines, admin table and MCP verbatim-error rule once the block exceeds 8,000 chars.
  Impact: The three lazySkills knobs, the SkillUsageTracker and extractSkillMentions machinery cost CPU and complexity with zero effect on what the agent sees; the skills header never carries SKILL.md guidance either way. The legacy tools cap can silently remove tool guidance for admin agents with many servers.
  Fix: Either read SKILL.md bodies via `entry.path` into fullContent so compression has something to save, or delete the lazy path and knobs; remove the second chars/4 truncation of toolsCombined (skills_header already has a budget) or make it warn.
- [medium/design-gap] **Hot memories and retrieved chunks carry no provenance or dates, and nothing resolves contradictions between injected memories**
  Evidence: session-config.ts:833-836 renders hot entries as `- ${mem.content}`; context-assembler.ts:1073-1085 keeps the same shape. memory-retrieval.ts:528-536 captures `createdAt` for memory-sourced hits but memory-context-augmenter.ts:133-139 renders only `### ${heading ?? path}\n${body}` (path is `memory:<id>`), so the date reaches the Discord footer (recall-footer.ts:92-113) but never the model. memory-retrieval.ts:681 `if (out.length > 0 && acc + len > tokenBudget * 4) break;` always emits the first chunk regardless of size, and nothing excludes `source: "system"` rows such as the SOUL.md memory stored by session-memory.ts:301-327 with importance 1.0.
  Impact: The model cannot tell a two-year-old fact from yesterday's or a dreamed inference from a user statement, so stale or conflicting memories are presented with equal authority; one oversized memory (plausibly the stored SOUL.md once memory_lookup bumps its access_count past hotAccessThreshold=3, search.ts:79 / tiers.ts:37) can consume the entire hot or retrieval budget and would re-embed the full SOUL text the fingerprint design deliberately keeps out (session-config.ts:591-593).
  Fix: Render `(saved YYYY-MM-DD, <source>)` on each bullet/heading from fields already in MemoryEntry/MemoryRetrievalResult; exclude tags soul/identity and source system from hot-tier injection and from the retriever's memories side; cap per-entry chars in the hot block.
- [medium/test-gap] **Eight session-config tests fail on a clean checkout, so the subsystem's regression guards are not enforced**
  Evidence: `npx vitest run src/manager/__tests__/session-config.test.ts` -> "Tests 8 failed | 55 passed": MEM-01-C2 still asserts `"…(truncated at 50KB cap)"` and a 51,200-char body (test :1353-1383) although session-config.ts:710-722 now applies the 16,000-char head-tail cap; Test 10 (:608) expects strategy "warn-and-keep" while context-assembler.ts:1261 emits "drop-lowest-importance"; the v1.5 size pin (:420) expects <= 5200 but the prompt is 5449 chars; the two Phase 73 brief-cache tests fail for the EXISTS-turns reason above; three advisor-pairing tests pin `claude-opus-5` / `claude-fable-5` ids that the resolver no longer returns.
  Impact: Changes to MEMORY.md capping, identity truncation and brief caching cannot be caught by CI for this file; the suite's red state hides new regressions among known ones.
  Fix: Update the stale pins to the Phase 115 behavior (16K char cap, drop-lowest-importance, current size), fix the brief-cache seed to insert a turn or fix the query, and refresh the advisor model ids; then make the file a required CI check.
- [low/inert-config] **Several zod knobs are parsed but have no runtime effect**
  Evidence: contextBudgets.identity and .hotMemories: the assembler only reads `budgets.toolDefinitions` (context-assembler.ts:1418) and `budgets.graphContext` (:1489); graphContext is always `""` (session-config.ts:1234, "today most call sites pass empty string" :1483). memoryAssemblyBudgets.soul/.recent_history/.per_turn_summary are fed only empty strings (session-config.ts:1228, :1241, :1246); .resume_summary is passthrough (:1301 "pass-through (enforced in src/memory/context-summary.ts ...)") while the real cap is `config.perf?.resumeSummaryBudget` (:1090). `identityMemoryAutoloadSource` (context-assembler.ts:141) has no producer. `AgentSessionConfig.contextSummary` (:1427, types.ts:132) echoes the always-undefined parameter rather than the loaded summary.
  Impact: Operators tuning these fields in clawcode.yaml see no change and get no validation error; dashboards reading section_tokens for soul/recent_history/per_turn_summary show permanent zeros.
  Fix: Delete the unread knobs from the schema (or mark them deprecated in the zod description), drop the graphContext/perTurnSummary/recentHistory plumbing, and remove the dead `contextSummary` return field.
- [low/bug] **Markdown-table steer is appended inside the conversation-brief block, so it depends on memory stores and leaks into channel-less agents**
  Evidence: session-config.ts:1120 `if (convStore && memStore) {` ... :1168-1175 `discordBindingsStr += " Markdown tables are welcome for comparisons and are rendered for Discord automatically" ...` with no `channels.length` guard, while the Discord block itself is built under `if (channels.length > 0)` at :1017.
  Impact: An agent whose memory init failed (session-memory.ts:213 "failed to initialize memory (non-fatal)") silently loses the table-rendering contract; an agent with no Discord channel gets a dangling sentence with no heading in its stable prefix.
  Fix: Move the append into the `channels.length > 0` block at :1017-1075.
- [low/design-gap] **v3 (claude --channels) agents receive none of the Tier 1 prompt: no MEMORY.md, USER.md, hot memories, capability manifest or session continuity**
  Evidence: claude-code-interactive.ts:958-960: "`--append-system-prompt` carries the channel-protocol contract (parity / caveat #1); identity/soul comes from the workspace CLAUDE.md." and :1056-1062 passes only `buildChannelProtocolPrompt(...)`. daemon.ts:11761-11771 wires MemoryContextAugmenter for the per-turn block only.
  Impact: A v3 agent only remembers via the cadence-gated `<memory-context>` block and explicit tool calls; the memory-protocol prose that promises MEMORY.md is "always shown above" is not even present, and the two backends drift in what the agent knows at boot.
  Fix: Append `buildSessionConfig(config, deps).systemPrompt` to the v3 `--append-system-prompt` value (the stable prefix is already a plain string).
- [low/dead-path] **Prompt tells the agent to use `memory_lookup` with an agent parameter while the manifest teaches `clawcode_memory_*`, and the SOUL memory it points at is never stored for soulFile agents**
  Evidence: session-config.ts:614: "When using memory_lookup, pass '${config.name}' as the agent parameter."; fingerprint.ts:24 `DEFAULT_INSTRUCTION = "Use memory_lookup for deeper identity context when needed"`; capability-manifest.ts:193-195 and :305-310 teach `clawcode_memory_search` / `clawcode_memory_recall` / `clawcode_memory_edit`. session-memory.ts:306 `const soulPath = join(config.workspace, "SOUL.md")` ignores the persona-files.ts:45 precedence (soulFile, inline soul).
  Impact: Two tool vocabularies in one prompt; for agents configured with soulFile or inline soul the "deeper identity context" memory does not exist, so the instruction sends the model to an empty lookup.
  Fix: Use one tool name set in both strings and route storeSoulMemory through readSoulContent.

**Strengths.**

- Two-block design is sound: everything long-lived rides the SDK-cached `preset+append` system prompt (session-adapter.ts:1536) and everything per-turn rides user messages, with the static-first cache marker isolating hot-tier churn (context-assembler.ts:1612-1650) and spread-conditional field emission keeping untouched agents byte-identical.
- MEMORY.md/USER.md now have a single canonical root shared by the prompt loader and the edit/archive tools (memory-roots.ts:57), with a warned legacy fallback (session-config.ts:678-700) instead of the old split-brain.
- The assembler is pure and DI-driven, never logs prompt bodies (BudgetWarningEvent carries counts only), and emits per-section token telemetry plus tier1_inject_chars onto traces (context-assembler.ts:1760-1790).
- Per-turn retrieval fails open everywhere (turn-dispatcher.ts:1296-1303, memory-context-augmenter.ts:181-187), is cadence- and topic-shift-gated to bound transcript growth (memory-context-augmenter.ts:84-127), filters pollution tags, caps document chunks, fuses vector + FTS on both chunk and memory sides, touches accessed_at so decay reflects real use, and folds owner-scoped shared facts in with a similarity floor.
- Operators can see what the model was shown: the recall footer (recall-footer.ts) and the weekly digest give provenance to humans even though the model itself does not get it.
- Security hygiene in the tool block: renderMcpPromptBlock reads only name/optional/lastError (mcp-prompt-block.ts:27-32), the capability-probe filter has a single call site, and lastError is capped at 500 chars to protect the cache.
- Conversation brief handles the raw-fallback case with a one-line placeholder instead of a dump (conversation-brief.ts:117-121) and uses terminated sessions for the gap check so daemon restarts do not suppress it.
- Budget enforcement protects the SOUL fingerprint unconditionally and prefers dropping lowest-value content (MEMORY.md tail) over persona text (context-assembler.ts:846-975).

### Capture: flush, cues, scanner, session summaries, compaction, transcripts, dedup (src/memory/{memory-flush,memory-cue,memory-scanner,session-log,session-summarizer,summary-dedupe,context-summary,compaction,subagent-capture,sdk-transcript-reader,tool-output-prune,fingerprint,dedup}.ts, src/manager/{summarize-with-haiku,daily-summary-cron,session-log-flusher,compaction-event-log}.ts)

**How it works.** Everything automatic in this subsystem is downstream of one table: ConversationStore turns. Those are written only by captureDiscordExchange (src/discord/capture.ts:66-105), called from three places in src/discord/bridge.ts (2817, 6239, 8223) with the user text and the final assistant reply text. No other origin (scheduler, OpenAI endpoint, heartbeat, cross-agent, workflows) records turns; tool calls never appear in turn content.

Per agent, startAgent starts two timers (src/manager/session-manager.ts:1869, 1877). (1) DB mid-session flush: startFlushTimer (3969-4020) every memory.conversation.flushIntervalMinutes (default 15, src/memory/schema.ts:99) calls flushSessionMidway (src/memory/session-summarizer.ts:491-661), which loads ALL turns of the active session (524), runs Haiku through summarizeWithHaiku (src/manager/summarize-with-haiku.ts:42-88, 120s timeout at session-summarizer.ts:47), embeds, and inserts a MemoryEntry tagged mid-session/session:<id>/flush:N with skipDedup (601-623). (2) Disk flush: startMemoryFileFlushTimer (1899-1931) builds a MemoryFlushTimer (src/memory/memory-flush.ts:166-291) every memoryFlushIntervalMs (900_000 default, src/config/schema.ts:3424); it takes turns since lastFlushAt, applies meaningfulTurnsSince (101-118), summarizes with a 10s abort (239), and atomically writes <memoryDir>/YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM.md (260-278).

Session boundary: stopAgent (session-manager.ts:2410-2446) stops the DB timer, awaits the final disk flush with a 10s race (1953-1972), ends the ConversationStore session, then runs summarizeSession (session-summarizer.ts:188-463): Haiku 4-section prompt (66-113, 30k-char proportional truncation), raw-turn fallback on timeout/error (123-133), deterministic short-session summary under 3 turns (147-157), embed, insert tagged session-summary with skipDedup (323-335), markSummarized, archiveSession, then deleteTurnsForSession (381-438). Crash path fires the same summarizer detached (3895-3915). On daemon restart a fresh session starts and closeStaleActiveSessions marks the orphan crashed (session-manager.ts:3067-3071; src/memory/conversation-store.ts:223-233); the summarize-pending heartbeat check drains crashed/ended sessions 5 per tick (session-manager.ts:4097-4160).

Daily log: the session-log-flush heartbeat check (src/heartbeat/checks/session-log-flush.ts:28-66, every heartbeat tick, intervalSeconds default 60 at schema.ts:717) calls flushNewTurnsToSessionLog (src/manager/session-log-flusher.ts:90-127), cursor-gated by .session-log-cursor, appending new active-session turns through SessionLogger.appendEntry (src/memory/session-log.ts:69-85) into <memoryDir>/YYYY-MM-DD.md. That file is the sole consolidation input (src/memory/consolidation.ts:136).

Cues: TurnDispatcher.maybeFireCueHook (src/manager/turn-dispatcher.ts:1413-1461) runs detectCue (src/memory/memory-cue.ts:59-65) on each user message and writes <memoryDir>/YYYY-MM-DD-remember-<id>.md (102-135); such files are standing and never expire (src/memory/memory-chunks.ts:197-207). Subagent returns: captureSubagentReturn (src/memory/subagent-capture.ts:75-137) is reachable only via TurnDispatcher.handleTaskToolReturn (turn-dispatcher.ts:1478-1503), which nothing in production calls.

Scanner: MemoryScanner (src/memory/memory-scanner.ts) watches <workspace>/memory plus the agent memoryDir when it differs (src/manager/daemon.ts:5467-5527); on add/change it sha256-gates, deletes all chunks for the path, re-chunks on H2 and re-embeds every chunk (203-259) into memory_chunks/vec/fts/memory_files (src/memory/store.ts:2389-2452). Retrieval fuses chunks (14-day window, session-manager.ts:938, except standing paths) with memories; the memories side drops session-summary, mid-session and raw-fallback by default (session-manager.ts:862-863; src/memory/memory-retrieval.ts:540-548).

Compaction: handleCompactSession (src/manager/daemon-compact-session-ipc.ts:338-420) runs Layer 1 compactForAgent -> CompactionManager.compact (src/memory/compaction.ts:117-173): flush turns to the daily log, recordSessionLog, extractMemories via the tiered extractor fed from the SDK JSONL transcript (loadCompactionTurns, daemon.ts:2401-2427; src/memory/sdk-transcript-reader.ts:239-310), insert each fact with dedup on, build a summary string; then Layer 2 native /compact. The summary string is reduced to a boolean (daemon-compact-session-ipc.ts:115). CompactionEventLog is in-memory only. Insert-time dedup is a k=1 cosine check at 0.85 with merge (src/memory/dedup.ts:42-139; store.ts:291-300); summary-dedupe.ts is a manual doctor action (daemon.ts:9000-9009). fingerprint.ts compresses SOUL.md for the prompt (src/manager/session-config.ts:598-599). tool-output-prune.ts and compactToolOutputs are unwired primitives (compaction.ts:80-88). daily-summary-cron.ts is a Discord cost embed, not memory.

**Data flow.** Entry: Discord message -> bridge.ts:2817/6239/8223 captureDiscordExchange -> ConversationStore.recordTurn (conversation_turns, conversation_sessions). Nothing else writes turns.

Automatic writers from turns: (a) flushSessionMidway -> memories (+vec_memories, fts) rows tagged mid-session, every 15 min, all turns each time; (b) MemoryFlushTimer -> <memoryDir>/YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM.md (delta since last flush) -> MemoryScanner -> memory_chunks/vec_memory_chunks/memory_chunks_fts/memory_files; (c) flushNewTurnsToSessionLog -> <memoryDir>/YYYY-MM-DD.md (append, cursor file) -> scanner chunks (each "## HH:MM:SS [role]" is a chunk) and -> consolidation weekly digests (reads YYYY-MM-DD.md only, archives them to memory/archive/YYYY/); (d) summarizeSession on stop/crash/drain -> memories row tagged session-summary -> conversation_sessions.summary_memory_id, session_archive (raw copy), then conversation_turns deleted; (e) cue hook -> <memoryDir>/YYYY-MM-DD-remember-<id>.md -> scanner chunks (standing).

Explicit-only writers: compact-session IPC -> CompactionManager.compact -> daily log + session_logs row + memories rows (source conversation, dedup on) + tier2 facts -> active-state.yaml; summary dedupe via doctor --run; clawcode_memory_* tools (other subsystem).

Readers: memory-retrieval.retrieve (pre-turn): chunks within 14 days + memories minus excluded tags; conversation-brief: findByTag("session-summary") last N into the resume brief; session-config.loadLatestSummary: <memoryPath>/memory/context-summary.md into "Context Summary (from previous session)"; memory-digest: memories minus excluded tags; summarize-pending drain: listPendingSummarySessions.

Never read: mid-session memories (excluded by retrieval default, brief, digest, dedupe), events.jsonl (session-log.ts:45-63, writer only), session_logs table (only a DELETE and a DISTINCT-date helper in store.ts:654/675), compaction result.summary (boolean only), context-summary.md (never written in production: saveContextSummary's single caller is the zone snapshot at daemon.ts:5590-5594, gated on a fill provider nobody feeds), subagent capture files (writer never invoked), CompactionEventLog token snapshots after restart.

**Config knobs parsed but not wired at runtime.**

- `defaults.memoryCueEmoji / agents.*.memoryCueEmoji`: Intended reaction emoji on a captured cue; resolved into ResolvedAgentConfig.memoryCueEmoji (loader.ts:1310) and listed as hot-reloadable (daemon.ts:9703) but turn-dispatcher.ts:1443 hardcodes "✅"
- `SummarizerConfig.timeoutMs / minTurns / importance (code-level, not zod)`: session-manager never passes config to summarizeSession or flushSessionMidway (4053-4062, 4175-4184), so operators cannot tune them

**Issues.**

- [high/dead-path] **Subagent return capture (MEM-06) is never invoked in production**
  Evidence: src/manager/turn-dispatcher.ts:1478 `async handleTaskToolReturn(agentName, event)` is the only entry to `captureSubagentReturn`; a repo-wide grep for `handleTaskToolReturn` finds only its definition and src/manager/__tests__/turn-dispatcher.test.ts:576/602/619. daemon.ts:4060-4065 wires `subagentCapture` into the dispatcher, but no session adapter, stream demux, or hook ever calls the method. The module header (src/memory/subagent-capture.ts:3-8) says it closes the "do you recall the Opus subagent you spawned?" crisis.
  Impact: Subagent final reports are discarded exactly as before Phase 90; the agent has no recall of delegated research or results across turns or sessions.
  Fix: In the SDK stream observer that already parses tool_result blocks (sdk-transcript-reader.extractContentBlocks is exported for this), detect `tool_use.name === "Task"` plus its matching `tool_result`, and call `turnDispatcher.handleTaskToolReturn(agent, {subagent_type, task_description: input.description ?? input.prompt, return_summary: resultText, spawned_at_iso, duration_ms})`. Add a wiring test that spies on captureSubagentReturn through a fake Task tool_result.
- [high/data-loss] **Disk flush silently drops a 15-minute window whenever the summary is empty, aborted, or the skip heuristic fires**
  Evidence: src/memory/memory-flush.ts:219-226 `if (!meaningfulTurnsSince(turns)) { ...; this.lastFlushAt = now; return null; }` and 249-256 `if (typeof summary !== "string" || summary.length === 0) { ...; this.lastFlushAt = now; return null; }`. The summarizer never throws: src/manager/summarize-with-haiku.ts:72-87 catches everything and `return ""`. The timer aborts at 10s (memory-flush.ts:239 `setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), 10_000)`), the bridge SIGTERMs the child on abort and reports failure (src/bridge/haiku-via-bridge.ts:551-568, 651 `succeeded = exitCode === 0 && !aborted`), and the same summarizer path was measured at 30-64s in production, which is why the DB summarizer was raised to 120s (src/memory/session-summarizer.ts:36-47). The bridge's own summarizer budget is 60s (src/bridge/bridge-spawn-governor.ts:41).
  Impact: Any flush that takes over 10s (likely most on the default bridge route), any Haiku error, and any tick where the user has spoken but the assistant has not yet replied advances the watermark past those turns. Those turns never reach a memory/YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM.md file, so the retrievable mid-session record is missing or sparse, and the retry-on-error branch is unreachable.
  Fix: Advance `lastFlushAt` only after a successful write; on skip/empty leave it unchanged (optionally advance only when `turns.length === 0`).
