Links also feed tier management: inbound edge counts (store.ts:1395-1423) qualify warm memories for hot promotion (tier-manager.ts:477-486, tiers.ts:89, threshold 5) and shield hubs from cold archival (tier-manager.ts:591). The dashboard memory-graph IPC (src/manager/memory-graph-handler.ts:111-159) renders the newest N nodes and the links among them.

CROSS-AGENT SHARING. Per-agent memories.db stores are isolated (enforceAgentIdentity on every IPC). The one fleet-level memory is SharedFactsStore (src/memory/shared-facts-store.ts): one SQLite file shared-facts.db under the manager dir with a v1 MiniLM 384-dim vec0 table, keyed by owner. Agents write with memory_save({scope:"owner"}) (mcp/server.ts:1677-1692 -> daemon.ts:18538-18557); the owner comes from the agent's own config. Reads happen in two places: appendOwnerFacts at the end of per-turn retrieval (session-manager.ts:685-704, called at :947) and inside clawcode_memory_search (clawcode-memory-search.ts:145-155). ownerFactsAsChunks (:233-255) returns at most 3 facts with similarity >= 0.5 as labelled chunks. Save dedups at cosine >= 0.9 by overwriting the nearest same-owner fact (:139-153). CrossAgentCoordinator (src/manager/cross-agent-coordinator.ts) exists for tagged, rollback-able multi-agent batch writes but has no production caller; consolidation.ts:32-42 only re-exports it. SharedDocRefStore is not memory: it remembers the last Discord share message per (agent, filename) so a re-share can edit the old message with a superseded banner (daemon.ts:6456-6491). resolveMemoryRoots (src/memory/memory-roots.ts:57-76) is the single resolver for MEMORY.md/USER.md and the dated memory dir.

MEMORY PRESSURE MEANS RAM, NOT CONTEXT. memory-pressure.ts reads kernel PSI (/proc/pressure/memory and the cgroup's memory.pressure) plus cgroup memory.events deltas (:118-134, :184-240). memory-guard.ts takes a 60s census of every process in the daemon's cgroup (:683-760), classifies it (:182-222), evaluates class budgets (:445-633), and routes breaches through a corroboration gate (:1612-1666) and a page/digest/telemetry tier router (memory-imminence.ts:123-145). memory-imminence.ts adds trajectory predicates on anon MB (:292-382). memory-relief.ts is the human-clicked SIGKILL with pid-identity, TTL, cgroup and daemon guards (:130-178). memory-signal-bus.ts carries one boolean (anon balloon) from the guard tick (memory-guard.ts:1371-1378) to the spawn governor and workflow admission gate (daemon.ts:3853-3860). memory-census.ts is the odd one out: it counts rows, tiers and integrity in the memory store for the heartbeat and doctor, and is named for the data, not for RAM. Context-window pressure is handled elsewhere (heartbeat/context-zones.ts).

**Data flow.** ENTRY. Agent content enters MemoryStore.insert (src/memory/store.ts) which writes memories + vec_memories, extracts [[id]] wikilinks into memory_links (store.ts:402-407), then autoLinkMemory (similarity.ts:199-225) writes auto:similar pairs via insertLink. Heartbeat discoverAutoLinks (similarity.ts:241+) re-scans by the auto_linked_at cursor. Dream pass: runDreamPass (dream-pass.ts:216+) reads graph-edges.json (:241-243) into the prompt; applyDreamResult / applyDreamResultD10 (dream-auto-apply.ts:146-150, :407-411) hand newWikilinks to applyAutoLinks -> appendDreamWikilinks (dream-graph-edges.ts), which lands in <memoryRoot>/graph-edges.json only. Owner facts: memory_save scope=owner (mcp/server.ts:1687-1692) -> daemon memory-save case (daemon.ts:18538-18557) -> SharedFactsStore.save -> shared_facts + vec_shared_facts in ~/.clawcode/manager/shared-facts.db.

READ. GraphSearch.search (graph-search.ts) via invokeMemoryLookup (memory-lookup-handler.ts:177-178) for memory_lookup scope=memories page=0; results leave as {id, content, relevance_score, linked_from} to the MCP tool. Tier manager reads backlink counts via listWarmCandidatesForPromotion / listColdArchiveCandidates. Dashboard reads handleMemoryGraphIpc (memory-graph-handler.ts) through sendIpcRequest "memory-graph" (dashboard/server.ts:481). Owner facts leave through ownerFactsAsChunks into the per-turn chunk list (session-manager.ts:947) and into clawcode_memory_search hits (clawcode-memory-search.ts:150-151). graph-edges.json is read only by dream-pass.ts:241-243 (back into the next dream prompt). CrossAgentCoordinator.runBatch/rollback: no reader or caller outside tests. SharedDocRefStore.record is called from the share-file IPC (daemon.ts:6467) and lives only in process memory.

PRESSURE. makeProductionReaders (memory-guard.ts:770-860) reads /sys/fs/cgroup/<self>/memory.*, /proc/<pid>/status|stat|cmdline, /proc/meminfo, statfs("/"), and readPressure. MemoryGuard.tick (:1248-1744) publishes {at, anonBalloon, balloonPid} to MemorySignalBus.publish (daemon.ts:6274); SpawnGovernor (:249) and WorkflowAdmissionGate (:315) read it through memorySignalBus.get(signalMaxAgeMs). Pages leave via postMemoryPage (daemon.ts:6118-6158) carrying snap.fattestAnonProc as the offender; the button click returns through handleReliefButton -> executeRelief, which appends to ~/.clawcode/security/memory-relief.jsonl. memory-census: collectMemoryCensus (memory-census.ts:68-109) reads store.checkIntegrity and tier counts for the heartbeat check (heartbeat/checks/memory-census.ts:34) and report-card-cron.ts:98.

**Issues.**

- [high/never-injected] **Dream-pass wikilinks never reach memory_links or any retrieval path**
  Evidence: dream-auto-apply.ts:146-150 `const linkArgs = outcome.result.newWikilinks.map((w) => ({ from: w.from, to: w.to })); const applyResult = await deps.applyAutoLinks(agentName, linkArgs);` and dream-machinery.ts:444-447 `const applyAutoLinks = async (_agent, links) => { ... return appendDreamWikilinks({ memoryRoot, links, now }); }`. appendDreamWikilinks writes only `<memoryRoot>/graph-edges.json` (dream-graph-edges.ts:58, :97). The prompt asks for path-shaped links, dream-prompt-builder.ts:124 `"newWikilinks": [{"from": "memory/path.md", "to": "memory/other.md", ...}]`, which cannot pass store.ts:404 `this.stmts.checkMemoryExists.get(targetId)`. grep for graph-edges across src finds exactly one reader: dream-pass.ts:241-243 `.readFile(`${deps.memoryRoot}/graph-edges.json`)`. Nothing in graph-search.ts, memory-retrieval.ts, tier-manager.ts or session-manager.ts reads it.
  Impact: Every connection the reflection model proposes is written to a file that only the next reflection pass sees. The agent's GraphSearch, hot-tier promotion and dashboard graph never gain an edge from dreaming, while the run log reports `wikilinks_added` (dream-machinery.ts:403-414) as if the graph were being enriched. The one LLM-driven linking mechanism in the system is inert for recall.
  Fix: In applyAutoLinks, resolve each from/to path to a memory id (memory_chunks.path -> source memory, or the memories row whose content/origin matches) and call stmts.insertLink(fromId, toId, "dream", now) inside a transaction, keeping graph-edges.json as a secondary mirror. If resolution is not feasible, change the dream prompt to emit chunk ids (it already receives `id=` per chunk, dream-prompt-builder.ts:145) and link by id.
- [high/bug] **Owner-facts KNN over-fetches across all owners, so an owner's facts drop out as the fleet grows**
  Evidence: shared-facts-store.ts:178-191 `// Over-fetch across owners, then filter: vec0 KNN cannot join on owner. ... WHERE v.embedding MATCH ? AND k = ? ... .all(embedding, Math.max(k * 8, 32)) ... return rows.filter((r) => r.owner === owner).slice(0, k)`. Both search (:195-202, used by ownerFactsAsChunks :240-242) and the dedup check in save (:139 `this.nearest(input.owner, embedding, 1)[0]`) go through this. The test (shared-facts-store.test.ts:24-62) covers two owners and a handful of rows only.
  Impact: Once 32 facts belonging to other owners sit closer to a query than the asking owner's matching fact, that fact is silently never recalled, and restating it is not deduplicated (the nearest same-owner fact is not in the 32), so duplicates accumulate. Silent recall loss that scales with the number of owners and total fact count.
  Fix: sqlite-vec 0.1.9 (installed) supports partition keys: recreate vec_shared_facts as `vec0(fact_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, owner TEXT partition key, embedding float[384] distance_metric=cosine)` and add `AND owner = ?` to the MATCH query; or, since per-owner sets are small, brute-force with `vec_distance_cosine(v.embedding, ?)` over `WHERE f.owner = ?`.
- [medium/design-gap] **No producer writes id-style wikilinks, so the graph is effectively the auto:similar KNN graph**
  Evidence: All insertLink callers: store.ts:317 and :406 (link_text = targetId from [[...]]), tier-manager.ts:289 (same), similarity.ts:181-182 (`"auto:similar"`). extractWikilinks targets must satisfy store.ts:404 `checkMemoryExists.get(targetId)`, i.e. be a nanoid memory id. grep for `[[` across non-test src finds no prompt, protocol text or tool parameter instructing an agent to write `[[<memory-id>]]`; memory_save (mcp/server.ts:1672-1684) has no related-ids parameter. similarity.ts:164-167 links only neighbours with cosine >= 0.6 (near-duplicates by the 999.119 MiniLM calibration).
  Impact: GraphSearch's 1-hop expansion mostly surfaces memories KNN would already rank, and "centrality" (tiers.ts:89, tier-manager.ts:591) rewards memories that have many near-duplicates rather than memories many others reference. Provenance and topical linking that a persistent agent needs (decision -> evidence, person -> preference) cannot be expressed.
  Fix: Give memory_save an optional `relatedTo: string[]` (ids returned by memory_lookup) that writes `link_text = "ref"` edges, and have the dream pass write typed edges (see issue 1). Keep auto:similar but tag it so GraphSearch can weight typed edges above similarity edges.
- [medium/design-gap] **Shared facts have no forget, list, decay or contradiction history; importance is stored but never used**
  Evidence: shared-facts-store.ts:204-225 define `list`, `count`, `delete`; grep across src (non-test) shows the only production calls are `shared.save(...)` (daemon.ts:18552) and `ownerFactsAsChunks(...)` (session-manager.ts:695, clawcode-memory-search.ts:150). Refresh overwrites in place: :146 `UPDATE shared_facts SET content = ?, tags = ?, importance = MAX(importance, ?), source_agent = ?, updated_at = ?`. Ranking ignores importance: :248-249 `fusedScore: f.similarity, scoreWeight: 0`.
  Impact: An owner preference that changes ("brief answers" then "detailed answers") is either overwritten with no record of the prior value, or, below the 0.9 threshold, both versions are recalled with no way to tell which is current. No agent or operator path can delete or even enumerate owner facts short of opening shared-facts.db. Importance set by the agent has no effect on what is recalled.
  Fix: Wire `delete` and `list` to a `memory_forget({scope:"owner", id})` tool and a `clawcode memory owner-facts` CLI; on refresh, append the previous content to a `history` JSON column or a shared_fact_versions table; fold importance into fusedScore (e.g. similarity * (0.5 + 0.5*importance)) and sort by updated_at as tie-break.
- [medium/bug] **Relief button always refuses on hosts where the census falls back to nested child cgroups**
  Evidence: memory-guard.ts:686-700: when `${cgroupDir}/cgroup.procs` is unreadable, `// Threaded cgroups nest ,  fall back to scanning child dirs' procs.` `pids = readdirSync(cgroupDir).filter((d) => d.endsWith(".scope") || d.endsWith(".slice")).flatMap(...cgroup.procs...)`. The relief membership check reads only the parent file: memory-relief.ts:376-378 `return readFileSync(`${cgroupDir}/cgroup.procs`, "utf-8").split("\n").some((l) => Number(l.trim()) === pid);`, and executeRelief refuses on false (:163-168 `refusal: "not-in-cgroup"`).
  Impact: On exactly the layout where the census had to use the fallback, every page names an offender the button then refuses to kill ("not in the ClawCode cgroup"). Fail-closed, so nothing wrong is killed, but the one human remediation path is inert and the operator is told the wrong reason.
  Fix: Extract the pid-listing from listCgroupProcs into a shared `cgroupPids(cgroupDir): Set<number>` that applies the same child-dir fallback, and have makeCgroupMembership call it.
- [medium/data-loss] **Dedup-merge wipes the surviving memory's outbound edges and re-extracts links from the incoming text only**
  Evidence: store.ts:310-319 `this.db.transaction(() => { const mergedTargets = extractWikilinks(input.content); this.stmts.deleteLinksFrom.run(dedupResult.existingId); ... for (const targetId of mergedTargets) { ... this.stmts.insertLink.run(dedupResult.existingId, targetId, targetId, mergeNow); } })();` where deleteLinksFrom is `DELETE FROM memory_links WHERE source_id = ?` (store.ts:2823-2825), unconditional on link_text. autoLinkMemory then runs (:330) but only re-creates neighbours still above 0.6.
  Impact: Every near-duplicate save (common for restated facts) deletes the existing memory's auto:similar out-edges and any explicit links that were in the stored content but not in the incoming text. Backlink counts used for hot promotion (tier-manager.ts:477-486) and cold shielding (:591) flicker downward after merges, and GraphSearch loses neighbours the agent could reach before the merge.
  Fix: Extract wikilinks from the post-merge stored content (getById(existingId).content) and only delete rows `WHERE source_id = ? AND link_text != 'auto:similar'` before re-inserting.
- [medium/design-gap] **Kill button is attached to pages whose cause is not the named process**
  Evidence: memory-guard.ts:1725-1733 `const post = this.deps.postPage ? this.deps.postPage({ key: breach.key, severity: breach.severity, message: text, ...(snap.fattestAnonProc !== undefined ? { offender: snap.fattestAnonProc } : {}) })` for every tier-A breach. The only key-based exclusion is daemon.ts:6124 `const offerWouldHelp = page.key !== "daemon:heap";`. Page-tier keys include `host:disk` (memory-imminence.ts:80) and `imminent:host-exhaustion` (:371-377), whose message itself says the cause may be a neighbour service (:354-355).
  Impact: A root-disk-full page or a host-wide fall caused by a process outside the cgroup carries a red "Kill <comm> (pid N)" button for the fattest in-cgroup process, inviting an operator to SIGKILL an agent CLI that did not cause the condition.
  Fix: In postMemoryPage, attach the offer only when page.key is one of imminent:anon-ceiling, imminent:oom-kill, imminent:kernel-stall or host:mem; keep the text suffix for the rest.
- [low/data-loss] **graph-edges.json grows without bound, is overwritten on any read error, and has an unlocked read-modify-write**
  Evidence: dream-graph-edges.ts:72-80 `} catch (err) { const code = (err as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code; if (code !== "ENOENT") { // Malformed JSON or read error ,  treat as empty so we overwrite cleanly ... existing = []; } }` so EACCES/EISDIR/EIO also discard the prior set; :94-97 always writes `[...existing, ...additions]` with no pruning. dream-prompt-builder.ts:257 degrades `wikilinks → summaries → MEMORY.md` first when over the 32K budget. dream-machinery.ts:234-236 acknowledges `the theoretical race of a manual IPC pass overlapping a cron pass for the same agent`.
  Impact: The file the dream prompt depends on for "not already in graph-edges.json" is the first section truncated once it is large, so the model re-proposes known edges; a transient permission or I/O error silently replaces the whole edge history with the current pass's additions; two overlapping passes can drop one side's edges.
  Fix: Only treat SyntaxError as empty and rename the bad file to `graph-edges.json.corrupt-<ts>` first; rethrow other errno; prune edges whose from/to paths no longer exist; serialise per agent with the same lock the dream cron already uses.
- [low/dead-path] **CrossAgentCoordinator is never invoked and its rollback contract does not match its signature**
  Evidence: grep for `.runBatch(` and `.rollback(` across src (non-test) finds only doc comments; consolidation.ts:32-42 re-exports the class without calling it. cross-agent-coordinator.ts:272-277 documents `If `agents` is omitted, rolls back across the `target_agents` recorded in the most-recent run-log row` while :285-288 declares `async rollback(runId: string, agents: readonly string[])` with no fallback. applyOneAgentSlice counts tags twice per memory, :392 `const before = store.findByTag(tag).length;` and :409 `const after = store.findByTag(tag).length;` (findByTag is a json_each scan, store.ts:1642-1648), and :402-408 admits dedup-merged content cannot be rolled back.
  Impact: No fleet-wide write path exists today, so cross-session or cross-agent consolidation atomicity is a doc promise only. If it is ever wired, rollback leaves merged content mutated and each slice costs O(n*m) tag scans.
  Fix: Either delete the module and its re-export, or wire it to the fleet dream pass and fix it: return `{entry, inserted}` from MemoryStore.insert instead of counting tags, and make `agents` optional by reading consolidation-runs.jsonl as documented.
- [low/inert-config] **sampleIntervalMs is start-only despite the per-tick reload contract**
  Evidence: memory-guard.ts:1160-1169 `this.timer = setInterval(() => { ... this.tick(); ... }, this.resolved().sampleIntervalMs);` and :1176-1188 the staleness warning also runs once in start(). The header says :32-33 `All knobs live under `defaults.memoryGuard` and are read per-tick → a config save takes effect on the next sample, no restart.` and schema.ts:3859 `All knobs read per-tick → RELOADABLE.`
  Impact: An operator who raises sampleIntervalMs via reload sees no change until a daemon restart; if they raise it past signalMaxAgeMs the balloon signal expires before each publish and the admission tighten leg silently disables with no warning.
  Fix: In tick(), compare cfg.sampleIntervalMs to the armed interval and re-arm the timer (clearInterval/setInterval) when it changes; re-run the staleness comparison there too.
- [low/bug] **memory-graph IPC can exceed SQLite's bind-variable ceiling at large limits**
  Evidence: memory-graph-handler.ts:129-140 `const placeholders = nodeIds.map(() => "?").join(",") || "NULL"; ... WHERE source_id IN (${placeholders}) AND target_id IN (${placeholders}) ... .all(...nodeIds, ...nodeIds)` binds 2N parameters; MEMORY_GRAPH_MAX_LIMIT is 50000 (:35). The CAP-03 test for limit 50000 (memory-graph-ipc.test.ts:134) runs against a near-empty DB, so N stays tiny.
  Impact: On a store with more than roughly 16,000 memories, a dashboard or CLI call with limit above that throws "too many SQL variables" (SQLite default ceiling 32766 bound parameters) instead of rendering the graph. Dashboard-only, no recall effect.
  Fix: Bind the id list once as JSON and join: `WHERE source_id IN (SELECT value FROM json_each(?)) AND target_id IN (SELECT value FROM json_each(?))`, or select links whose both endpoints have rowid within the same ORDER BY created_at window.
- [low/performance] **GraphSearch loads full neighbour rows for every link of every KNN hit and does not exclude cold memories**
  Evidence: graph-search.ts:74-75 `const forwardLinks = getForwardLinks(this.store, knnResult.id); const backlinks = getBacklinks(this.store, knnResult.id);` where both statements select `m.content` and every column for all matching rows with no LIMIT (store.ts:2826-2843). Only ids are used (:77-80). No tier filter anywhere in search(); similarity.ts:164 refuses to create edges to cold rows but pre-existing edges remain and cold rows stay in the store (search.ts:90-91). store.ts:1012 notes a production DB with 7,338 edges.
  Impact: A hub memory with hundreds of backlinks turns one memory_lookup into hundreds of full-row reads and JSON.parse calls per KNN hit; cold (decayed) memories can be surfaced as neighbours with a fresh access bump (:123-126), partially undoing the tier manager's decay.
  Fix: Add id-only prepared statements (`SELECT target_id FROM memory_links WHERE source_id = ? LIMIT 50`, same for backlinks) joined with `memories.tier != 'cold'`, and use them in GraphSearch.
- [low/dead-path] **Unused graph utilities: traverseGraph and MemoryLink**
  Evidence: graph.ts:49-77 exports `traverseGraph`; graph.types.ts:8-13 exports `MemoryLink`. grep across non-test src finds no importer of either (graph-search.ts imports only getForwardLinks/getBacklinks).
  Impact: Multi-hop traversal that the module header advertises is tested (graph.test.ts:47-93, :409) but never available to retrieval; readers assume depth > 1 is supported.
  Fix: Either use traverseGraph in GraphSearch behind a `maxDepth` config (default 1) or delete it and its tests.
- [low/design-gap] **SharedDocRefStore is process-memory only**
  Evidence: shared-doc-refs.ts:58-59 `export class SharedDocRefStore { private readonly refs = new Map<string, SharedDocRef>();` constructed once at daemon.ts:6456 `const sharedDocRefs = new SharedDocRefStore();`.
  Impact: After a daemon restart the superseded-banner mechanism forgets every prior share, so the first re-share of a document after a restart leaves the stale copy unmarked, which is the Vallejos incident shape the module exists to prevent.
  Fix: Persist the map as a small JSON file under the manager dir (write on record, load on construct), bounded by the same 200-entry/7-day rules.
- [low/test-gap] **Test gaps around the integration seams this audit found**
  Evidence: No test asserts a dream newWikilink reaches memory_links or GraphSearch (dream-graph-edges.test.ts:9-157 only checks the JSON file). shared-facts-store.test.ts covers two owners and under ten rows, never the 32-row global over-fetch. memory-guard.test.ts has no case for sampleIntervalMs changing between ticks. memory-relief.test.ts:215-238 tests readReliefProc but there is no test for makeCgroupMembership against a nested cgroup layout. cross-agent-coordinator.test.ts exercises the class only through direct construction.
  Impact: The issues above can regress or persist unnoticed because the suites test each module in isolation and never the hand-off between them.
  Fix: Add one integration test per seam: dream result -> memory_links row; 3 owners x 40 facts -> owner recall; guard tick after resolveConfig change; membership check with a child .scope dir fixture.

**Strengths.**

- Edge integrity is enforced by the schema: memory_links has CASCADE FKs and a composite PK (store.ts:1810-1824), checkIntegrity/repairIntegrity (store.ts:1140-1245) clean dangling edges before every linker pass, and graph.test.ts covers cascade on delete and cold archival.
- GraphSearch bumps access_count only for graph-walked neighbours and never double-bumps KNN seeds (graph-search.ts:114-126), with five dedicated tests (GS-A1..A5), so heavily linked nodes can earn hot-tier promotion.
- Owner isolation in shared facts is structural: the owner always comes from the agent's own config, never from tool input (daemon.ts:18539-18541, session-manager.ts:691), delete requires the owner (shared-facts-store.ts:217-225), and the retrieval floor was recalibrated against measured MiniLM random-pair similarity (:27-35).
- The pressure stack is pure-DI with injected readers and clocks, and its state machine is tested against real incident shapes (memory-guard.test.ts: INC-2026-08-09 chrome flap, the 2026-08-14 OOM climb, the 2026-09-16 screenshot), including the regression that a suppressed or telemetry-tier breach must not open a cooldown.
- memory-pressure.ts enforces two sound rules explicitly: deltas never absolutes for cumulative memory.events, and fail-open when PSI is absent (:17-26, :184-240), with the first-tick-after-restart case handled.
- memory-relief.ts is fail-closed in every branch and guards pid reuse with /proc/<pid>/stat starttime parsed after the last ')' (:330-360), the one correct way to do it; the audit sink can never turn a kill into an error (:302-314).
- mergeBudgets merges per field so an operator override cannot silently drop sibling defaults (memory-guard.ts:265-282), and legacy class names keep working through aliases with a one-time advisory.
- resolveMemoryRoots collapses five divergent path derivations into one pure resolver with legacy fallback and a warning (memory-roots.ts, session-config.ts:675-700), fixing a real prod split where agents edited a MEMORY.md the prompt never loaded.
- SharedDocRefStore is bounded by age and count with insertion-order eviction (shared-doc-refs.ts:61-85), so it cannot grow.

### Document store and ingestion (src/documents, src/document-ingest, src/manager/auto-ingest-*, set-doc-priority, shared-doc-refs) plus its two retrieval surfaces: the search_documents tool and the cross-ingested memory mirror

**How it works.** There are two entry paths and two retrieval paths.

Entry A (automatic, Discord): bridge.prepareInboundMessage downloads every attachment to `<memoryPath>/inbox/attachments/<ts>-<sanitized>` (src/discord/bridge.ts:2084-2086, attachments.ts:37-39,120-121), then dispatchAutoIngestAttachments fires a fire-and-forget `auto-ingest-attachment` IPC per file (bridge.ts:2240-2251). The daemon case only validates and enqueues (daemon.ts:18197-18237) into the process-global AutoIngestQueue, which drains serially under one image-work-governor permit (auto-ingest-queue.ts:178-230) and calls handleAutoIngestAttachment with DI deps wired at boot (daemon.ts:11303-11324). A 10-minute heartbeat rescan re-enqueues inbox files under 24h that have no `documents` row (auto-ingest-rescan.ts:53, auto-ingest-queue.ts:237-280). Transcripts are enqueued the same way with origin "transcript" (daemon.ts:11718-11731).

The handler (auto-ingest-handler.ts:198-616) checks the live agent flag (:228), classifies by extension/MIME/size (classifier :223-371), and for images runs sha256 exact-dedupe (:337-352, chunk-less duplicate row via recordExactDuplicate :661-704), dHash near-dup reuse of a prior analysis (:354-368, :639-654), then a batched structured vision call through IngestVisionBatcher -> VisionService -> bridge-haiku provider with ocr-only (null) as terminal fallback (:370-386, vision/ingest-batcher.ts:95-140, vision/config.ts:23-29). It then runs the Phase 101 engine (:414): detectDocumentType (detect.ts:30-65) -> per-type handler (index.ts:94-112) -> for image pages, ocrPage's chain tesseract-cli -> tesseract-wasm -> Claude vision (ocr/index.ts:93-117). The vision summary and chart shape are appended to the OCR text (:422-428) so text-free photos are findable by what they show; the merged text is chunked by chunkText (chunker.ts:27-68, ~500 tokens, 50 overlap), embedded in the worker (:479), written to document_chunks + vec_document_chunks with overwrite-by-source (store.ts:217-276), mirrored into memory_chunks/fts/vec under path `document:<slug>` by crossIngestToMemory (cross-ingest.ts:148-242, slug from computeDocSlug index.ts:73-80), archived as WebP (:522-531, image-archive.ts:32-52), and finally a provenance row is upserted into `documents` with class, weights, Discord ids, sha256, dhash and vision_json (:532-549, store.ts:604-634).

Entry B (manual): MCP ingest_document (mcp/server.ts:2218-2300) -> daemon `ingest-document` (daemon.ts:17837-18195): workspace containment (:17889-17901), 100MB size guard (:17908-17920), engine, optional extractStructured tool-use (:18008-18037), atomic `<workspace>/documents/<slug>-<date>.md/.json` writes (:18041-18055), main-thread embedV2 (:18094-18098), docStore.ingest, crossIngestToMemory (:18142-18150), logIngest telemetry and recordIngestAlert on failure. Note this path never writes a `documents` row and never captions images.

Retrieval 1 (explicit): search_documents (server.ts:2409-2458, cached 1800s per agent via daemon.ts:7054 and tool-cache-policy.ts:92) -> daemon `search-documents` (:18335-18402) -> DocumentStore.search (store.ts:301-388): embedV2 query, KNN over-fetch 3x, LEFT JOIN documents, weightedScore = similarity x agentWeight (live ingestionPriority) x contentWeight x 1.3 if ingested within 7 days, slice, attach adjacent chunks and source_image_path. The MCP formatter prints similarity, source path, optional `Image: <path>`, and the chunk text.

Retrieval 2 (automatic): every cadence-gated turn, turn-dispatcher.ts:1279 calls the retriever from session-manager.ts:894-948, which runs hybrid RRF over memory_chunks (memory-retrieval.ts:391+), applies the same D-02 weighting to `document:` candidates via getDocumentRowBySlug (session-manager.ts:919-926, memory-retrieval.ts:121-200, 598-614), reranks, caps document chunks at 2 (:658), and renders `<memory-context>` with heading = path (memory-context-augmenter.ts:130-140). `document:` paths are exempt from the 14-day window (memory-chunks.ts:197-198), so a week-old PDF or image can still surface automatically, but only at most 2 chunks, only with a 2-day recency boost, and labelled by an opaque `document:<ts>-<name>` heading.

Priority overrides (emoji/MCP/CLI) converge on set-doc-priority-handler.ts with D-08 sandbox and a JSONL audit log (:132-290, :298-326, :334-424). shared-doc-refs.ts is an unrelated in-memory "superseded share" marker for outbound Discord shares.

Answer to the two questions: a Discord image or PDF is findable a week later both automatically (memory mirror, standing-memory exemption) and explicitly (search_documents), provided cross-ingest succeeded (it is non-fatal, handler :486-510) and the agent's query lands within the 2-document-chunk cap. Images on the auto path are captioned (kind, summary, visible text, entities, chart shape) and the caption is embedded in the chunks and stored as vision_json; images on the manual ingest_document path and all scanned PDFs are OCR-only.

**Data flow.** Enters: (1) Discord attachment -> downloadAllAttachments writes `<memoryPath>/inbox/attachments/<Date.now()>-<sanitized>` (src/discord/attachments.ts:81-143) -> sendIpcRequest "auto-ingest-attachment" (bridge.ts:2240) -> autoIngestQueue.enqueue (daemon.ts:18226) -> drainNext -> handleAutoIngestAttachment (daemon.ts:11310-11323). (2) MCP ingest_document -> daemon case "ingest-document" (daemon.ts:17837). (3) transcript watcher -> autoIngestQueue.enqueue with origin "transcript" (daemon.ts:11718-11731). (4) heartbeat auto-ingest-rescan -> runRescanForAgent -> reconstructParams (auto-ingest-queue.ts:309-327) for inbox files <24h with no documents row.

Lands in (per-agent memory.db shared with MemoryStore, session-memory.ts:163-165): document_chunks + vec_document_chunks (DocumentStore.ingest store.ts:217-276), documents provenance row (upsertDocumentRow store.ts:680-699; only on the auto path, handler :549 and :672), memory_chunks + memory_chunks_fts + vec_memory_chunks(_v2) + memory_files via crossIngestToMemory -> memoryStore.insertMemoryChunk/insertChunkEmbeddingV2 (cross-ingest.ts:215-235) keyed by path `document:<computeDocSlug(filePath)>`. Files: `<memoryPath>/attachments-archive/<name>.webp` (archiveImage), `<workspace>/documents/<slug>-<date>.md|.json` (manual path only, daemon.ts:18041-18055), `<memoryPath>/audit-priority-changes.jsonl` (set-doc-priority-handler.ts:108-118).

Read by: DocumentStore.search (store.ts:301) <- daemon "search-documents" (:18374) <- MCP search_documents (server.ts:2431); getDocumentRowBySlug (store.ts:731) <- session-manager.ts:920 <- applyDocumentPriorityWeight (memory-retrieval.ts:598) during retrieveMemoryChunks <- turn-dispatcher.ts:1279; findDocumentBySha256 / listRecentImageHashes (handler :339, :647) for dedupe; getDocumentRow (rescan dedup, queue :268; priority handlers); getDocumentRowByMessageId (emoji path :308); listSources/getChunkCount (daemon "list-documents" :18427-18428); contamination-cleanup.ts:182 scans document_chunks.

Leaves: search_documents tool text (server.ts:2438-2453: similarity, Source path, Image path, context, content); `<memory-context>` block prepended to the user message (renderMemoryContext memory-context-augmenter.ts:130-140, heading = `document:<slug>` because cross-ingest writes heading null, cross-ingest.ts:218); recall footer "documents" list (recall-footer.ts:78-79); admin-clawdy alerts (manual path only, daemon.ts:17982-18171 via recordIngestAlert); pino logs tagged phase999.43-autoingest / phase101-ingest / phase999.154-ingest-vision. Never leaves: documents.channel_id, message_id, user_id, ingested_at, source_kind, auto/override class, vision_json (except indirectly through chunk text), agent_priority_weight_at_ingest.

**Config knobs parsed but not wired at runtime.**

- `defaults.documentIngest.tesseractConfidenceThreshold`: Intended floor for Tesseract short-circuit; ocrPage reads only opts.confidenceThreshold (ocr/index.ts:66) and the engine never passes it (index.ts:164-167). No runtime reader of the config field.
- `defaults.documentIngest.pageBatchSize`: Intended per-batch page cap; DEFAULT_BATCH_SIZE is a constant (page-batch.ts:21) and batchPages is never called
- `defaults.documentIngest.dimensionMaxPx`: Intended resize ceiling; DIMENSION_MAX_PX is a constant in page-batch.ts:27 and claude-vision.ts:31
- `defaults.documentIngest.visionModelDefault / visionModelHighPrecision`: Intended model ids; constants in claude-vision.ts:34-37 and extractor.ts:38-41 are used instead

**Issues.**

- [high/bug] **Tesseract tier always 'passes' so Claude-vision OCR never fires on garbage output; tesseractConfidenceThreshold is inert**
  Evidence: src/document-ingest/ocr/tesseract-cli.ts:64-66: `const trimmed = (text ?? "").trim(); const confidence = trimmed.length === 0 ? 0 : Math.max(DEFAULT_CONFIDENCE, 0.75);` (always 0.75 for any non-empty text). src/document-ingest/ocr/index.ts:66,96-97: `const threshold = opts.confidenceThreshold ?? DEFAULT_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD;` ... `const cli = await ocrPageTesseract(imageBuffer); if (cli.confidence >= threshold) return cli;`. src/document-ingest/index.ts:164-167 calls `ocrPage(page.imageBuffer, { taskHint: opts.taskHint, backend: opts.backend })` with no confidenceThreshold. src/config/schema.ts:4767 `tesseractConfidenceThreshold: z.number().min(0).max(1).default(0.7)` has no reader outside schema.ts (grep).
  Impact: On hosts with the tesseract binary (the documented deploy prereq), any scanned PDF page or image that yields even one garbled token is embedded as-is; the paid vision tier the design relies on for dense financial pages never runs, and the operator knob that is supposed to tune this does nothing. Scanned tax returns ingested via ingest_document get low-quality chunks.
  Fix: Derive a real confidence in tesseract-cli (run with `tsv` output and average word conf, or a cheap alphanumeric-ratio heuristic), and thread `config.defaults.documentIngest.tesseractConfidenceThreshold` into `ingest()` -> `ocrPage({confidenceThreshold})` via a boot-time setter like setAllowMistralOcr.
- [high/bug] **search_documents with a `source` filter applies the filter after KNN, so it silently returns few or no hits**
  Evidence: src/documents/store.ts:579-592 (searchBySource): `WHERE v.embedding MATCH vec_int8(?) AND k = ? AND d.source = ?` against a vec0 table declared at :461-464 with only `chunk_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, embedding int8[384]` (no partition or metadata column), and :312 `const overFetchK = Math.min(clampedLimit * 3, MAX_SEARCH_LIMIT * 3);` (at most 60). sqlite-vec returns the global k nearest rows first; the `d.source = ?` predicate then drops any that belong to other documents. The only test, store.test.ts:110 'filters by source when provided', uses a 2-document corpus so k covers everything.
  Impact: Once an agent has more than ~60 relevant chunks across documents, filtering to one source returns 'No matching documents found.' even when that document contains the answer. Agents learn the filter is useless or conclude the document was never ingested.
  Fix: Declare `source text partition key` on vec_document_chunks and add `AND v.source = ?` so the KNN is scoped, or when `source` is given prefilter with `AND v.rowid IN (SELECT rowid FROM vec_document_chunks WHERE chunk_id IN (SELECT id FROM document_chunks WHERE source = ?))`.
- [high/data-loss] **delete_document leaves the provenance row and memory mirror behind; the same image can then never be re-ingested**
  Evidence: src/documents/store.ts:394-400 `deleteDocument(source)` only calls `deleteChunksForSource`, which at :434-442 touches `document_chunks` and `vec_document_chunks` only. src/manager/daemon.ts:18414 `const count = docStore.deleteDocument(source);` with no memory or documents cleanup. Rescan dedup auto-ingest-queue.ts:268 `if (docStore?.getDocumentRow(fullPath)) continue;`. Exact dedupe store.ts:657-661 `WHERE content_sha256 = ? AND duplicate_of IS NULL` and handler :339-341 `const original = docStore.findDocumentBySha256(contentSha256); if (original !== null && original.source !== source) { return recordExactDuplicate(...)`. Cross-ingest rows under `document:<slug>` (cross-ingest.ts:193) are never deleted.
  Impact: After the agent 'deletes' a document it still surfaces in <memory-context> from the mirror, the documents row still marks it as ingested so the rescan never retries, and any future re-send of the identical bytes is recorded as a duplicate of the deleted original with no chunks. A wrong-version PDF or contaminated image cannot be purged and replaced through the tool.
  Fix: In the daemon delete case (or DocumentStore.deleteDocument with a memoryStore arg): `DELETE FROM documents WHERE source = ? OR duplicate_of = ?`, call `memoryStore.deleteMemoryChunksByPath("document:" + computeDocSlug(source))`, and unlink the archive file if present.
- [high/bug] **The inbox attachments directory is never cleaned: the cleanup check sweeps workspace/attachments, downloads land in memoryPath/inbox/attachments**
  Evidence: src/heartbeat/checks/attachment-cleanup.ts:24-25 `const attachmentDir = join(agentConfig.workspace, "attachments"); const removed = await cleanupAttachments(attachmentDir);` versus src/discord/attachments.ts:37-39 `export function attachmentInboxDir(memoryPath) { return join(memoryPath ?? "/tmp", "inbox", "attachments"); }` used by bridge.ts:2084. grep shows `cleanupAttachments(` has no other caller and nothing writes to `join(..., "attachments")` under workspace. Comments that assume the 24h policy: src/documents/types.ts:56-57, src/manager/auto-ingest-queue.ts:41-42, auto-ingest-handler.ts:516-518.
  Impact: Every attachment ever received by an ingest-enabled agent (up to 25MB each) stays on disk forever; the 'subject to 24h cleanup' and 'rescan window matches retention' assumptions are false, and disk exhaustion on the daemon host eventually stops new ingests and SQLite writes.
  Fix: Replace the path in attachment-cleanup.ts with `attachmentInboxDir(agentConfig.memoryPath)` and add a test asserting the check and the bridge agree on the directory.
- [medium/data-loss] **docSlug collisions make cross-ingest delete another document's memory mirror and mis-attribute priority**
  Evidence: src/document-ingest/index.ts:73-80 `computeDocSlug` is `basename(filePath).replace(/\.[^.]+$/, "")` lowercased; src/document-ingest/cross-ingest.ts:193,199 `const path = \`document:${args.docSlug}\`; ... args.memoryStore.deleteMemoryChunksByPath(path);`; src/documents/store.ts:731-741 `getDocumentRowBySlug` scans all sources and the docblock at :727-728 admits 'slug collisions across different file paths are possible'. daemon.ts:17926 `const docSlug = computeDocSlug(filePath);` for the manual path where slugs are bare basenames.
  Impact: Ingesting `q3-report.pdf` then `q3-report.xlsx` (or the same filename from two client folders) silently wipes the first document's memory_chunks; retrieval weighting then reads the wrong documents row. Inbox paths carry a ms timestamp so the auto path is mostly safe, but the manual/homelab fan-out path is not.
  Fix: Append a short hash of the full source path to the slug (`${slug}-${sha256(source).slice(0,8)}`), persist `slug` as an indexed column on `documents`, and look up by that column instead of the linear scan.
- [medium/performance] **Files that produce no chunks or fail deterministically are retried every 10 minutes for 24 hours with paid OCR and vision calls**
  Evidence: src/manager/auto-ingest-handler.ts:439-470 returns `{ ok: true, skipped: true, reason: "no chunks produced (empty text)" }` without `upsertDocumentRow`; classifier rejects (:283-287) and engine errors (:614) likewise write no row. src/manager/auto-ingest-queue.ts:262,268 `if (now - st.mtimeMs > RESCAN_MAX_AGE_MS) continue;` ... `if (docStore?.getDocumentRow(fullPath)) continue;` and auto-ingest-rescan.ts:40 `interval: 600`. Each retry on an image runs ocr/index.ts:93-117 (tesseract -> wasm -> Claude vision) and handler :370-372 `analysis = await deps.analyzeImage(params)`.
  Impact: A photo whose OCR is empty and whose vision analysis returned null (provider down or refusal) costs up to 144 vision calls and 144 OCR chains per file per day; a corrupt PDF loops the same way. During a vision outage this multiplies cost and keeps the serial drain busy, delaying real ingests.
  Fix: Write a chunk-less provenance row with a `skipped_reason` column (the exact-duplicate path already does this), or keep a persisted attempt counter and stop re-enqueueing after N (3) failures.
- [medium/bug] **search_documents results are cached 30 minutes per agent with no invalidation on ingest or delete**
  Evidence: src/mcp/tool-cache-policy.ts:92 `search_documents: { ttlSeconds: 1800, keyStrategy: "per-agent" }`; src/manager/daemon.ts:7054-7069 intercepts `search-documents` through dispatchTool before routeMethod; grep for 'invalidat' in tool-cache-store.ts and tool-dispatch.ts returns nothing, and neither daemon.ts:18114 (`docStore.ingest`) nor the auto-ingest handler touches the cache. The MCP side adds a second per-turn cache (server.ts:2426 `invokeWithCache`).
  Impact: An agent that searches, gets nothing, ingests the PDF, and searches again with the same words receives the stale 'No matching documents found.' for up to 30 minutes. Freshly deleted documents keep appearing for the same window.
  Fix: On successful ingest-document, auto-ingest dispatch, and delete-document, purge tool-cache rows where tool='search_documents' and agent matches; or lower the TTL to ~60s.
- [medium/never-retrieved] **Provenance is stored but never reaches the agent: no sender, date, channel, or filename in any prompt or tool output**
  Evidence: documents columns channel_id, message_id, user_id, ingested_at, source_kind are written at auto-ingest-handler.ts:532-549. daemon.ts:18382-18400 returns only chunk fields plus weighted_score, recency flag and source_image_path; server.ts:2438-2452 prints `Source: ${hit.source} [chunk ${hit.chunk_index}]` where source is the inbox path `<Date.now()>-<sanitized>` (attachments.ts:120-121). The memory mirror writes `heading: null` (cross-ingest.ts:218) so renderMemoryContext (memory-context-augmenter.ts:135 `const heading = c.heading ?? c.path;`) shows `document:1729…-image_png`.
  Impact: The agent cannot answer 'who sent this statement and when', cannot distinguish a client upload from an operator ingest, and sees opaque timestamped headings in <memory-context>; recall of the right document is not actionable without provenance.
  Fix: Include ingested_at, user_id/user_name, channel_id, source_kind and the original filename in the search-documents payload and MCP formatter, and set the cross-ingest heading to e.g. `Attachment "<filename>" from <user_name>, <date>`.
- [medium/design-gap] **No forgetting or lifecycle: document rows and chunks grow forever, archive sweep leaves dangling image paths, non-image originals have no durable copy**
  Evidence: src/documents/store.ts:263-269 only `console.warn` when count exceeds 10,000; no age-based deletion exists anywhere in the subsystem. image-archive.ts:55-82 `sweepImageArchive` unlinks WebPs older than ttlDays but `documents.source_image_path` is never nulled, and daemon.ts:18399 still emits `source_image_path` for retrieval. auto-ingest-handler.ts:522-531 archives only when `isImageMime`, so PDFs/docx keep no durable original once the inbox copy is gone.
  Impact: Every Discord screenshot ever sent becomes permanent retrieval competition for the agent's real documents; after 90 days the agent is told `Image: <path>` for files that no longer exist and fails on Read; for a PDF the agent can never re-read the original for a detail outside the retrieved chunk.
  Fix: Add a periodic sweep that deletes low-class discord_attachment documents (chunks, row, mirror) older than N days, null out source_image_path when the archive sweep removes a file (or existsSync-check before emitting the Image line), and archive non-image attachments as well.
- [medium/design-gap] **Document stores are strictly per agent; nothing shares a document across the fleet**
  Evidence: src/manager/session-memory.ts:163-165 `const documentStore = new DocumentStore(store.getDatabase()); this.documentStores.set(name, documentStore);`; daemon.ts:18337,18342 `enforceAgentIdentity(params, agentName)` then `manager.getDocumentStore(agentName)`; the only workaround is src/cli/commands/homelab.ts:248 fanning out ingest-document to every agent. The `documents.agent_name` column (store.ts:474) is therefore always the owner and never used for sharing.
  Impact: A statement uploaded in agent A's channel is invisible to agent B; the same PDF must be ingested N times (N embeddings, N mirrors, N sets of chunks) and priority overrides do not propagate.
  Fix: Smallest step: a fleet-level DocumentStore (or a `sharedWith: [agents]` list on the documents row) that search-documents consults read-only after the agent's own store, with results tagged by owning agent.
- [medium/inert-config] **Five documentIngest config fields are parsed with defaults but never read at runtime**
  Evidence: src/config/schema.ts:4767-4771 `tesseractConfidenceThreshold`, `pageBatchSize: z.number().int().min(1).max(20).default(5)`, `dimensionMaxPx: ...default(2000)`, `visionModelDefault: z.string().default("claude-haiku-4-5")`, `visionModelHighPrecision: z.string().default("claude-sonnet-5")`. grep over src for those names finds only schema.ts; the runtime uses constants DEFAULT_BATCH_SIZE/DIMENSION_MAX_PX (page-batch.ts:21,27), DIMENSION_MAX_PX and CLAUDE_VISION_MODEL_* (claude-vision.ts:31-37), EXTRACTOR_MODEL_* (extractor.ts:38-41). The schema docblock at :4714 states 'All fields are reload-safe'.
  Impact: An operator who raises dimensionMaxPx for a dense chart, switches the vision model, or tightens the OCR threshold sees no change and no error, and may believe the knob is broken elsewhere.
  Fix: Either wire each field through a boot-time setter (the setAllowMistralOcr pattern) or remove the five fields from the schema and docs.
- [low/performance] **getDocumentRowBySlug is a linear scan with slug recomputation, called per document candidate on every retrieval turn**
  Evidence: src/documents/store.ts:731-741: `for (const { source } of allSources) { if (computeDocSlug(source) === slug) { return this.getDocumentRow(source); } }`; invoked from session-manager.ts:919-926 inside applyDocumentPriorityWeight (memory-retrieval.ts:168-169) for every `document:` candidate among up to 40 fused results.
  Impact: With thousands of auto-ingested images per agent (every Discord image makes a row) each turn's retrieval does tens of thousands of basename/regex operations plus a row fetch per candidate on the main thread.
  Fix: Persist `slug` on the documents row with an index (also needed for the collision fix) and query `WHERE slug = ?`.
- [low/bug] **backend='none' is accepted by the daemon but unhandled by ocrPage, so it runs the full paid chain**
  Evidence: src/manager/daemon.ts:17864-17872 accepts `backendRaw === "none"`; src/document-ingest/ocr/index.ts:69-91 branches on mistral, tesseract-cli, tesseract-wasm, claude-haiku, claude-sonnet only, then :93-117 falls into Tier 1-3 including `return ocrPageClaudeVision(...)`.
  Impact: An operator asking for no OCR on an image-only document gets Tesseract and, when empty, a paid Claude vision call, with telemetry attributing it to the chain.
  Fix: In ocrPage: `if (opts.backend === "none") return { text: "", confidence: 0, backend: "none" };`.
- [low/dead-path] **Dead code and never-populated telemetry across the engine**
  Evidence: clientNamePatterns is always `undefined` at auto-ingest-handler.ts:262 and :406 and no config field exists, so classifier rung 6a (:288-302) can never fire. `isChartLike` (auto-ingest-classifier.ts:407) and `batchPages` (page-batch.ts:52) have no non-test callers; the engine loops pages directly (index.ts:156). alerts.ts reason `ocr-low-confidence` (:31) is never emitted (grep); `ocrConfidence` and `apiCostUsd` (types.ts:65,69) are never set (index.ts:182-190 omits them). IngestOptions.extract/force (types.ts:46-47) are never read by `ingest()` (index.ts:164-167).
  Impact: Operators reading alerts.ts expect an OCR-quality alert that cannot occur; the client-name HIGH rung documented in the cascade is unreachable; maintenance cost of code that looks live.
  Fix: Delete isChartLike, batchPages, the extract/force engine options and the ocrConfidence/apiCostUsd fields, or wire them; add `defaults.documentIngest.clientNamePatterns` if the rung is wanted.
- [low/bug] **Scanned-PDF handler renders every page before enforcing MAX_PAGES**
  Evidence: src/document-ingest/handlers/scanned-pdf.ts:9-10 claims documents over MAX_PAGES 'reject before any rendering work', but :53-59 runs `pdftoppm -r 150 -png input.pdf page` on the whole file and the check `if (pngs.length > MAX_PAGES) throw new IngestError(...)` is at :71-73 after rendering.
  Impact: A 5,000-page scanned PDF rasterizes 5,000 PNGs at 150 DPI into tmp before being rejected, defeating the T-101-03 DoS mitigation.
  Fix: Pass `-f 1 -l ${MAX_PAGES + 1}` to pdftoppm (or run `pdfinfo` first) and reject when the page count exceeds the cap.
- [low/bug] **xlsx formula and date cells are flattened to '[object Object]' or verbose Date strings**
  Evidence: src/document-ingest/handlers/xlsx.ts:26-32: `if (typeof v === "object" && "text" in (v as object)) { cells.push(String((v as { text: unknown }).text ?? "")); } else { cells.push(String(v)); }`. ExcelJS formula cells are `{ formula, result }` (no `text`) and date cells are `Date` instances.
  Impact: Spreadsheets with computed totals (the HIGH-class financial case) embed '[object Object]' where the numbers should be, so searches for amounts miss.
  Fix: Handle `"result" in v` (use result, recursing for rich text) and `v instanceof Date` (ISO date) before the String fallback.
- [low/bug] **Unknown binary types are decoded as UTF-8 text and embedded as garbage**
  Evidence: src/document-ingest/detect.ts:62-64 `if (mime.startsWith("image/")) return "image"; return "text";` and handlers/text.ts:10 `buffer.toString("utf-8")`. The auto path rejects only video/audio/archive extensions (classifier :62-81); .pptx, .key, .heic variants sharp cannot decode, etc. reach the engine.
  Impact: A .pptx dropped in Discord becomes chunks of zip-garbage that pollute vector neighbourhoods; an undecodable image throws in handleImage and enters the rescan retry loop.
  Fix: Return an 'unsupported' DocumentType when file-type reports a non-text MIME that is not pdf/docx/xlsx/image, and skip with a logged reason (and a chunk-less row).
- [low/bug] **Emoji priority on a re-sent (exact-duplicate) image lands on the chunk-less duplicate row and has no retrieval effect**
  Evidence: recordExactDuplicate (auto-ingest-handler.ts:672-687) upserts a row keyed by the new file_path with the new message_id and `duplicateOf: original.source` but no chunks. handleSetDocPriorityByMessage resolves `docStore.getDocumentRowByMessageId(params.message_id)` (set-doc-priority-handler.ts:308) and setDocumentPriority (store.ts:646-651) updates only that row; search joins `docs.source = d.source` (store.ts:574) so the original's weight is unchanged.
  Impact: An operator who reacts 🔴 on the second share of a statement believes it was promoted; retrieval still treats it as before.
  Fix: In handleSetDocPriorityByMessage, follow `row.duplicate_of` to the original source before delegating.
- [low/design-gap] **Two retrieval surfaces use different recency windows (7 days hardcoded vs configurable 2 days)**
  Evidence: src/documents/store.ts:335-336 `const SEVEN_DAYS_MS = 7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000; const RECENCY_BOOST = 1.3;` inside `search`; src/config/schema.ts:4803 `recencyBoostDays: ...default(2)` is threaded only into the memory path (session-manager.ts:910-916, 928).
  Impact: The same document ranks differently in search_documents and in <memory-context>; the operator's recencyBoostDays change does not affect the explicit tool.
  Fix: Pass the resolved recency window into `docStore.search(..., { recencyWindowMs })` from the daemon search-documents case.
- [low/design-gap] **Manual ingest_document never captions images; only the Discord auto path gets the structured vision summary**
  Evidence: src/mcp/server.ts:2220 advertises 'Robustly ingest a PDF/docx/xlsx/image'; daemon.ts:17973-17976 calls only `ingestDocumentEngine(fileBuffer, filePath, { taskHint, backend })`; index.ts:107-108 `case "image": return handleImage(buf);` then OCR at :164. The vision call exists only in auto-ingest-handler.ts:370-372 (`deps.analyzeImage(params)`) and the merged '## Image summary' text at :422-428.
  Impact: An operator-ingested chart or photo is retrievable only by OCR'd text; a text-free image yields zero chunks and is not findable at all through the manual path.
  Fix: In the ingest-document case, when `ingestResult.telemetry.type === "image"`, call `getVisionService().analyzeBatch([...])` and append `renderAnalysisText` the same way the auto handler does.
- [low/performance] **Chunker is O(n^2) on large documents and flattens all whitespace, destroying the Markdown tables OCR was asked to preserve**
  Evidence: src/documents/chunker.ts:51-54 calls `findWordPosition(text, words, wordStart)` and `findWordPosition(text, words, wordEnd - 1)` per chunk, and :94-106 walks from position 0 each call; :34 `text.split(/\s+/)` with :48 `chunkWords.join(" ")`. claude-vision.ts:43 prompts 'Preserve tables as Markdown'. detect.ts:71-83 and handlers/text-pdf.ts:18-21 additionally parse the same PDF twice.
  Impact: A 500-page text PDF spends seconds in position scans on the main thread (manual path) and every table row collapses into one line, so row/column semantics are lost before embedding.
  Fix: Track the running char offset incrementally while slicing, and split on paragraph/newline boundaries first so chunk content keeps line structure; cache the pdf-parse result between detection and handling.
- [low/design-gap] **First document ingest silently flips a rolled-back embedding migration back to dual-write**
  Evidence: src/document-ingest/cross-ingest.ts:81-91 `if (facade === "v1-only") { this.migrator.transition("dual-write"); return "dual-write"; }` where facadeFor maps both `idle` and `rolled-back` to 'v1-only' (:96-98).
  Impact: An operator who deliberately rolled back the v2 embedding migration finds it re-entered dual-write the next time any attachment is ingested, with no log line from this subsystem.
  Fix: Only auto-flip from `idle`; for `rolled-back` write v1 only and log a warn.
- [low/test-gap] **The ingestion engine has effectively no unit tests**
  Evidence: grep over *.test.ts for ocr/index|ocrPage(, detectDocumentType, crossIngestToMemory, recordIngestAlert, extractStructured(, handlers/scanned-pdf, handlers/text-pdf, handlers/xlsx, batchPages, computeDocSlug, claude-vision each return 0 files; only handlers/image (2) and chunkText (1) are referenced. src/documents/__tests__/store.test.ts:110 tests the source filter with two tiny documents.
  Impact: The KNN-filter bug, the tesseract confidence constant, the xlsx formula bug and slug collisions all pass CI; regressions in OCR tiering or cross-ingest would not be caught.
  Fix: Add fixture-driven tests under src/document-ingest/__tests__ for detect (pdf text vs scanned, docx/xlsx/unknown), each handler, ocrPage tier fallthrough with stubbed backends, crossIngestToMemory slug collision, and a store search test with >60 chunks across sources.

**Strengths.**

- Clean dependency injection on both handlers (handleAutoIngestAttachment, handleSetDocPriority) with in-memory DocumentStore tests covering the four locked behaviours plus dedupe, archive and transcript cases (auto-ingest-image-vision.test.ts, auto-ingest-dispatcher.test.ts).
- Never-drop queue design: enqueue-and-ACK in microseconds, serial drain under a single governor permit with try/finally, non-blocking defer at the depth cap, and an on-disk inbox rescan that makes recovery idempotent (overwrite-by-source, delete-by-path).
- Image handling since 999.154 is thoughtful: sha256 exact dedupe before any OCR, dHash near-duplicate analysis reuse, batched labelled vision calls with per-element validation and refusal sentinels, a null-never-fiction provider contract, and a durable WebP archive so retrieval paths outlive the inbox.
- Structured vision output (summary, visible text, entities, chart shape) is merged into the same chunks as OCR text, so text-free photos and charts are retrievable by content, and the importance field drives classification instead of fragile keyword matching.
- Provenance table keeps auto_classified_class and override_class separately with an append-only JSONL audit log; the D-08 sandbox is enforced at both the MCP schema and the daemon handler.
- Agent flags and ingestionPriority are read live at event and query time (hot-reload honoured) rather than snapshotted.
- Cross-ingest validates the slug against a strict grammar before any write, validates embedding alignment before deleting, and the `document:` prefix is exempt from the 14-day memory window while a per-retrieval cap of 2 stops a document dump from crowding out conversational context.
- Schema migrations (int8 vec recreate, additive columns) are idempotent and ordered correctly in the constructor; the pre-999.43 backfill keeps legacy chunks searchable via LEFT JOIN.
- Weighted search over-fetches 3x and re-ranks in JS, correctly acknowledging that sqlite-vec's k must be fixed at SQL time.

### Agent memory tool surface and memory instructions: clawcode_memory_search / recall / edit / archive (src/memory/tools), their MCP registrations and the legacy memory_lookup / memory_save tools (src/mcp/server.ts), the daemon IPC handlers that wire them, the capability-manifest memory protocol, the research-subagent tool policy, and the iOS memory gateway (src/manager/openai-memory-gateway.ts)

**How it works.** Six memory tools are registered on the in-process "clawcode" MCP server (src/mcp/server.ts). Each handler takes a self-reported `agent` arg, wraps the payload with a spawn-injected identity token via `withIdentity` (server.ts:443-456), and sends an IPC request to the daemon. The daemon case arms (src/manager/daemon.ts:18529-18755) call `validateStringParam(params,"agent")` then `enforceAgentIdentity` (daemon.ts:2654-2669), which fails open when no token is present and otherwise requires the requested agent to equal the token's caller (agent-identity-token.ts:121-150). Per-agent isolation then rests on `manager.getMemoryStore(agentName)`.

Search: server.ts:1730-1767 forwards query/k/includeTags/excludeTags; daemon.ts:18578-18627 resolves the store, embedder, and the agent's configured `owner`, and calls `clawcodeMemorySearch` (clawcode-memory-search.ts:114-190). That calls `retrieveMemoryChunks` with `timeWindowDays: 0`, `tokenBudget: 4000`, `topK: k` (lines 120-143), which fans out over memory_chunks (file-scanned markdown) and the memories table (memory-retrieval.ts:391-420, 480-561), RRF-fuses, time-windows (disabled here), caps to topK and token budget (674-694). Owner facts from the shared store are appended afterwards with ids `shared:<id>` (shared-facts-store.ts:233-255). Hits are reduced to 500-char snippets (search tool 157-170). An includeTags filter runs post hoc and discards all chunk-sourced hits (177-187).

Recall: server.ts:1770-1796 and daemon.ts:18629-18656 call `clawcodeMemoryRecall` (recall.ts:56-98), which tries `store.getById` (bumps access_count, store.ts:472-480), then `store.getMemoryChunk` (store.ts:2740-2765), else `{ok:false, error:"memory not found in this agent's store"}`.

Edit: server.ts:1799-1834 and daemon.ts:18658-18702 resolve `resolveMemoryRoots(cfg).tier1Dir` (memory-roots.ts:57-76) and call `clawcodeMemoryEdit` (edit.ts:72-177): zod enum locks path to MEMORY.md|USER.md, a relative-path jail check and an lstat symlink refusal run first (79-116), then view/create/append/str_replace do plain readFile/writeFile (118-175) and return the whole file in `after`.

Archive: daemon.ts:18704-18755 calls `clawcodeMemoryArchive` (archive.ts:50-91), which looks up a memory_chunks row and appends its body (with optional prefix/suffix) to the target Tier 1 file through the same edit primitive, bypassing the dream veto window.

memory_save (server.ts:1667-1713, daemon.ts:18529-18568) embeds and inserts into the memories table with source "conversation" (dedup merge applies), or into the fleet SharedFactsStore when scope="owner". memory_lookup (server.ts:1564-1664, alwaysLoad-pinned) goes through `invokeMemoryLookup`, which searches the memories table via GraphSearch or conversations via searchByScope (memory-lookup-handler.ts:177, 219); it never touches memory_chunks.

Instructions: session-config.ts:613-614 injects "Your name is X. When using memory_lookup, pass 'X'". `buildCapabilityManifest` (capability-manifest.ts:64-299) adds a "Lazy memory recall" bullet (193-195) and a "Memory protocol" paragraph (280-285: curated memory "always shown above", call clawcode_memory_search for older context, record facts via clawcode_memory_edit, promote via clawcode_memory_archive), but returns "" for agents with no opt-in feature (260-269). The operator directive "memory-recall-before-uncertainty" (config/schema.ts:263-268) tells agents to call `memory_lookup` before saying "I don't know". MEMORY.md and USER.md are loaded into the stable prefix at session build only (session-config.ts:708-800; call sites session-manager.ts:1320, 3027, 3354), head/tail truncated at 16,000 / 4,000 chars.

Researchers: research-tool-policy.ts allows memory_lookup, clawcode_memory_search, clawcode_memory_recall, client_facts (53-70) and denies memory_save, clawcode_memory_edit, clawcode_memory_archive (76-110), enforced both via disallowedTools (217-225) and a PreToolUse hook (173-214).

iOS gateway: createMemoryGateway (openai-memory-gateway.ts:59-126) maps device facts to memories rows keyed by origin_id `ios:<uuid>` with tag "ios-memory"; list is scoped by origin prefix; updateFact rewrites content without re-embedding; forget cascades delete.

**Data flow.** Writes enter through (a) memory_save → daemon.ts:18529-18568 → MemoryStore.insert (store.ts:270-465; dedup may mergeMemory, dedup.ts:84-130) into memories + vec_memories (+ memories_fts by trigger, store.ts:2267-2276), or SharedFactsStore.save for scope=owner; (b) clawcode_memory_edit / clawcode_memory_archive → daemon.ts:18658-18755 → clawcodeMemoryEdit writeFile on <tier1Dir>/MEMORY.md or USER.md (edit.ts:135, 152, 173); (c) iOS gateway ingest/updateFact/forget → store.insert with origin_id, store.updateContentByOriginId (store.ts:1574-1590), store.deleteByOriginId (1619-1636). File-scanned markdown under <memoryDir> is indexed into memory_chunks by the scanner, which explicitly skips the root MEMORY.md (memory-scanner.ts:44-55).

Reads: clawcode_memory_search → retrieveMemoryChunks over memory_chunks + memories (+ owner facts) → 500-char snippets returned as JSON text to the model (server.ts:1753-1756); clawcode_memory_recall → store.getById / getMemoryChunk → full body as JSON; memory_lookup → GraphSearch over memories or searchByScope over conversations; MEMORY.md / USER.md → session-config.ts:708-800 → identityMemoryAutoload → composeCarvedIdentity (context-assembler.ts:757-772) into the static prefix at session start only. Data leaves the system to the model as tool result text, to Discord via the recall footer (recall-footer.ts, which reads createdAt the retrieval never emits), and to iOS via gateway.list.

**Config knobs parsed but not wired at runtime.**

- `agents.*.memoryRetrievalExcludeTags / defaults.memoryRetrievalExcludeTags`: Tag exclusion for the pre-turn retriever only (session-manager.ts:861-863). The clawcode_memory_search handler passes only the agent-supplied excludeTags (daemon.ts:18602-18606), so the locked default never applies to the lazy tool.
- `agents.*.memoryRetrievalTopK / memoryRetrievalTokenBudget`: Pre-turn retriever only; the lazy search hardcodes tokenBudget 4000 and k default 10 (clawcode-memory-search.ts:33, 137).
- `perf.tools.idempotent (IDEMPOTENT_TOOL_DEFAULTS)`: Intra-turn result cache for memory_lookup and search_documents (server.ts:1619-1664, 2425-2457). The entries memory_list and memory_graph name tools that are never registered (schema.ts:1045-1048; no server.tool for them), and clawcode_memory_search is not whitelisted.

**Issues.**

- [high/never-retrieved] **Tier 1 content past the injection cap is unreachable by every path (prompt truncates it, scanner never indexes it, tools never warn)**
  Evidence: session-config.ts:713-722 keeps only `headLen = floor(cap*0.7)` and `tailLen = floor(cap*0.2)` and drops the rest with a marker. memory-scanner.ts:48-51: `// Root MEMORY.md is handled by Plan 90-01 (stable prefix auto-load).` `if (/\/MEMORY\.md$/i.test(absPath) && !/\/memory\/.+MEMORY\.md$/i.test(absPath)) { return false; }` so MEMORY.md and USER.md never land in memory_chunks. clawcode-memory-edit.ts:151-153 `const next = existing + parsed.content; await fs.writeFile(candidate, next, "utf8");` and clawcode-memory-archive.ts:61-73 append with no size check and no warning in the result.
  Impact: An agent that follows the protocol ('Record significant new facts via clawcode_memory_edit', 'Promote a found chunk') grows MEMORY.md past 16,000 chars; from then on the middle of the file is neither in the prompt nor searchable, and the agent is never told. Recall silently degrades as the agent curates more.
  Fix: In the daemon edit/archive handlers, after a write compare the file length to `cfg.perf?.memoryAssemblyBudgets?.injectedMemoryMaxChars ?? INJECTED_MEMORY_MAX_CHARS` and add `warning: "MEMORY.md is N chars over the M-char prompt cap; the middle will be dropped"` to the result. Separately, have the scanner index Tier 1 files (drop the root-MEMORY.md exclusion) so overflow stays searchable.
- [high/bug] **Owner shared facts returned by search cannot be recalled or archived (id namespace mismatch)**
  Evidence: shared-facts-store.ts:245-251 `chunkId: \`shared:${f.id}\`, ... source: "chunk" as const`. clawcode-memory-search.ts:159-169 passes `memoryId = c.chunkId` through. clawcode-memory-recall.ts:65-97 only tries `deps.store.getById(id)` then `deps.store.getMemoryChunk(id)` and returns `error: "memory not found in this agent's store"`. clawcode-memory-archive.ts:56-59 `const chunk = deps.store.getMemoryChunk(parsed.chunkId); if (!chunk) return { ok: false, error: "chunk not found" }`. server.ts:1733-1734 promises `Use clawcode_memory_recall(memoryId) to fetch full body.` after listing `the owner's shared facts`.
  Impact: The agent gets a hit, follows the description, and receives a not-found error for a memory it was just shown; it may conclude the memory was deleted or the tool is broken. includeTags also drops these hits because source is "chunk".
  Fix: In clawcodeMemoryRecall accept an optional `ownerFacts` dep and resolve ids with the `shared:` prefix via SharedFactsStore lookup; in the search tool tag these hits `source: "owner-fact"` and note in the description that owner facts are returned in full.
- [high/inert-config] **Operator tag-exclusion defaults never apply to clawcode_memory_search**
  Evidence: clawcode-memory-search.ts:138 `excludeTags: parsed.excludeTags,` (agent input only). daemon.ts:18602-18606 `const excludeTags = Array.isArray(params.excludeTags) ? (params.excludeTags as string[]) : undefined;`. The locked default lives only on the pre-turn path: session-manager.ts:861-863 `config?.memoryRetrievalExcludeTags ?? (["session-summary", "mid-session", "raw-fallback"] as const)`.
  Impact: Session summaries, mid-session flushes and raw-fallback rows (the pollution-feedback memories Phase 115 sub-scope 4 excluded on purpose) rank in lazy search results and consume the 4000-token budget, crowding out curated facts.
  Fix: In the clawcode-memory-search daemon arm, read `cfg.memoryRetrievalExcludeTags` and pass `excludeTags: params.excludeTags ?? cfg.memoryRetrievalExcludeTags` (agent override replaces, matching the schema's replace semantics).
- [medium/design-gap] **Two search tools with contradictory steering; the protocol's tool is deferred while the narrower legacy tool is pinned**
  Evidence: schema.ts:266 directive: `ALWAYS invoke the \`memory_lookup\` MCP tool with the user's question terms.` session-config.ts:614 `When using memory_lookup, pass '${config.name}' as the agent parameter.` server.ts:1564 `alwaysLoad(server.tool("memory_lookup", ...` vs server.ts:1730 plain `server.tool("clawcode_memory_search", ...`. memory_lookup never searches memory_chunks: memory-lookup-handler.ts:177 `new GraphSearch(memoryStore)` and :219 `searchByScope(`. capability-manifest.ts:283 steers to `clawcode_memory_search` instead.
  Impact: The directive the agent sees most often routes recall to a tool that cannot see dated notes, digests, or owner facts, while the hybrid tool sits behind ToolSearch deferral (server.ts:411-421 comment). Agents answer 'I don't have that in memory' for facts that only live in memory_chunks.
  Fix: Wrap the clawcode_memory_search registration in `alwaysLoad(...)` and change the directive text and the agent-name line to name clawcode_memory_search (or route memory_lookup scope=memories through retrieveMemoryChunks).
- [medium/design-gap] **Memory protocol is dropped for baseline agents and is wrong for subagents and memoryAutoLoad=false agents**
  Evidence: capability-manifest.ts:193-195 pushes the Lazy memory recall bullet unconditionally, but :260-269 `const hasOptIn = config.dream?.enabled === true || ... ; if (!hasOptIn) return "";` discards it and the memoryProtocol (:280-285). The protocol says `Your curated memory is in MEMORY.md and USER.md, always shown above. Recent context is retrieved for you before each turn` while subagent-config-overrides.ts:137 sets `memoryAutoLoad: false` and :143 `memoryRetrievalTopK: 0`.
  Impact: A plain agent (no skills/MCP/dream) is never told the four tools exist or when to write memory; a thread subagent is told its memory is shown above when nothing was loaded, so it trusts an empty prefix instead of searching.
  Fix: Emit the memory protocol regardless of hasOptIn (it is the only write instruction the agent gets), and build the 'shown above' / 'retrieved before each turn' sentences conditionally on `config.memoryAutoLoad !== false` and `memoryRetrievalTopK > 0`.
- [medium/data-loss] **create mode overwrites the whole Tier 1 file with no guard or backup**
  Evidence: clawcode-memory-edit.ts:131-137 `case "create": { ... await fs.writeFile(candidate, parsed.content, "utf8"); return { ok: true, after: parsed.content }; }`. server.ts:1802 advertises `create (overwrite)`.
  Impact: One mis-chosen mode (create instead of append) erases the agent's entire curated memory and the operator-curated USER.md; nothing is kept and the prompt still shows the old text until restart, hiding the loss.
  Fix: Refuse `create` when the target exists and is non-empty unless `force: true`, or write `<memoryDir>/backups/<file>.<ts>.md` before overwriting.
- [medium/data-loss] **memory_save silently merges into an existing similar memory and replaces its content; agent is told 'Memory saved'**
  Evidence: daemon.ts:18567 `store.insert({ content, source: "conversation", importance, tags }, embedding)` (no skipDedup). store.ts:290-300 `if (!hasOriginId && this.dedupConfig.enabled && !input.skipDedup) { ... if (dedupResult.action === "merge") { mergeMemory(...)`. dedup.ts:105-115 `UPDATE memories SET content = ?, importance = ?, tags = ?, ... WHERE id = ?` with `input.content`. store.ts:163 `similarityThreshold: 0.85`. server.ts:1700 `: \`Memory saved (id: ${result.id})\``.
  Impact: Two distinct facts with similar wording (two clients' AUM, two dates) above 0.85 cosine collapse into one row and the older value is gone with no history; the agent cannot tell a merge happened, and a correction cannot be traced back (no provenance of the replaced text).
  Fix: In the memory-save arm compare `entry.createdAt` to a timestamp taken before insert (the trick store.ts:425-428 describes) and return `{ id, action: "merged" | "created" }`; render 'Merged into existing memory <id> (previous text replaced)' so the agent can re-save with skipDedup or a distinguishing tag.
- [medium/performance] **Edit tool echoes the entire file back into the model context on every write**
  Evidence: clawcode-memory-edit.ts:136, 153, 174 return `after: parsed.content` / `after: next`. server.ts:1813-1823 `as { ok: boolean; after?: string; error?: string }` then `text: JSON.stringify(result, null, 2)`. The search tool's own header warns against this: clawcode-memory-search.ts:17-18 `snippets are capped at 500 chars per hit (Pain Point #2 ... leaked giant blobs into the prompt)`.
  Impact: Each append or str_replace on a 16K+ MEMORY.md injects the full file (JSON-escaped) into the transcript; a few edits per session cost tens of thousands of tokens and push real context out.
  Fix: Return `{ ok, chars, preview }` where preview is ~200 chars around the change for str_replace/append and omit `after` except for mode=view.
- [medium/design-gap] **Search hits and recall results carry no timestamp; retrieval hydrates createdAt then drops it**
  Evidence: memory-retrieval.ts:561 `createdAt: meta.created_at,` (hydrated) but the output push at :683-690 `Object.freeze({ chunkId, path, heading, body, fusedScore, scoreWeight, source })` omits it. SearchHit (clawcode-memory-search.ts:48-61) has no date or tags field; chunk recall (recall.ts:81-91) returns no mtime although getMemoryChunk exposes `file_mtime_ms` (store.ts:2745). recall-footer.ts:71 `...(c.createdAt ? { createdAt: c.createdAt } : {})` therefore never fires.
  Impact: When two hits contradict (old budget vs new budget) the agent has no way to prefer the fresher one; the Discord recall footer's date range is always empty.
  Fix: Add `...(h.createdAt ? { createdAt: h.createdAt } : {})` to the output push in retrieveMemoryChunks; surface `createdAt` (memories) and `fileMtimeMs` (chunks) on SearchHit and RecallResult.
- [medium/dead-path] **Mid-session Tier 1 edits never reach the agent's own prompt; the referenced cache-bust does not exist**
  Evidence: buildSessionConfig runs only at session-manager.ts:1320 (startAgent), :3027 (reconcile/resume) and :3354 (voice). context-assembler.ts:403-405 `When the agent edits MEMORY.md mid-life, the static prefix legitimately changes; that's the rare event \`[diag] static-prefix-cache-bust\` (Plan 115-08) will surface.` A grep for `static-prefix-cache-bust` finds only that comment. Protocol text (capability-manifest.ts:282) says the files are `always shown above`.
  Impact: After str_replace-ing a wrong fact the agent keeps seeing the wrong fact in its prefix for the rest of the session and may 'correct' it again or trust the stale version.
  Fix: Append `note: "takes effect at the next session start"` to edit/archive results, and have the daemon arm set a per-agent 'tier1Dirty' flag that forces a session rebuild on the next idle/resume.
- [medium/missing-feature] **No agent-facing list, forget, update or link operation for memories-table rows**
  Evidence: memory_save (server.ts:1667-1713) has no inverse; delete_document (server.ts:2461-2480) deletes document chunks only. schema.ts:1045-1048 whitelists `"memory_list", "memory_graph"` and server.ts:386-388 says `memory_list, memory_graph ... are not yet registered`; no `server.tool("memory_list"` exists. Only the iOS gateway can forget/update (openai-memory-gateway.ts:114-124) and only `ios:` rows. Graph edges exist (store.ts insertLink) but no tool links memories.
  Impact: An agent that learns a saved memory is wrong can neither delete nor correct it; the stale row keeps winning vector searches, and the agent cannot browse what it has stored to audit it.
  Fix: Register `memory_forget(memoryId)` over store.delete and `memory_update(memoryId, content)` that re-embeds (reuse mergeMemory's vec swap, dedup.ts:117-128); register memory_list over listByOriginPrefix-style paging or drop the dead whitelist names.
- [low/bug] **str_replace interprets `$` replacement patterns and silently replaces only the first match**
  Evidence: clawcode-memory-edit.ts:169-172 `if (!existing.includes(parsed.oldStr)) { return { ok: false, error: "oldStr not found in file" }; } const next = existing.replace(parsed.oldStr, parsed.newStr);` String.prototype.replace with a string pattern expands `$$`, `$&`, `` $` `` and `$'` in newStr and only touches the first occurrence; no uniqueness check despite the Anthropic memory_20250818 contract the header cites.
  Impact: A financial agent writing `$$` or `$'` into USER.md gets mangled text; a non-unique oldStr edits the wrong section with ok:true.
  Fix: Count occurrences with `existing.split(oldStr).length - 1`, return an error when it is not exactly 1, and replace with `existing.replace(oldStr, () => newStr)`.
- [medium/bug] **Archive accepts only memory_chunks ids although search returns three id kinds, and appends without provenance or dedupe**
  Evidence: clawcode-memory-archive.ts:56-59 `const chunk = deps.store.getMemoryChunk(parsed.chunkId); if (!chunk) { return { ok: false, error: "chunk not found" }; }`. Search returns `source: "memory"` hits whose ids come from the memories table (memory-retrieval.ts:553) and `shared:` ids. server.ts:1842 `Chunk ID to promote (returned from clawcode_memory_search)`; capability-manifest.ts:285 `Promote a found chunk to permanent memory via clawcode_memory_archive(chunkId)`. archive.ts:61-62 appends `${prefix}${chunk.body}${suffix}` with no source path, date, or existing-content check.
  Impact: Promoting a saved memory or owner fact fails with a misleading error; promoting the same chunk twice duplicates it; promoted text loses where and when it came from, so later contradictions cannot be adjudicated.
  Fix: Resolve `getMemoryChunk(id) ?? store.getById(id)`; default the prefix to `### ${heading ?? path} (archived ${date})`; skip when the target already contains the body.
- [medium/bug] **iOS gateway updateFact leaves the vector index stale**
  Evidence: store.ts:1568-1575 `Deliberately does NOT re-embed ... the stale vector is acceptable, documented debt.` and :1577-1582 `UPDATE memories SET content = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE origin_id = ?`. openai-memory-gateway.ts:114-118 calls it directly although the gateway holds `deps.embed`. FTS stays fresh via the trigger at store.ts:2273-2276, the vec tables do not.
  Impact: After the user edits a fact on the phone, vector legs of both the pre-turn retriever and clawcode_memory_search rank the row by its old wording; the corrected fact can miss on semantic queries while the FTS leg still matches the new text, giving inconsistent recall.
