  Fix: Give MemoryStore an `updateContentByOriginId(originId, content, embedding)` overload that also deletes/inserts vec_memories and clears vec_memories_v2 (mirror dedup.ts:117-128), and have the gateway `await deps.embed(fact)` before calling it.
- [low/silent-failure] **iOS re-ingest with a changed fact keeps the old content while overwriting metadata**
  Evidence: openai-memory-gateway.ts:65-88: `const embedding = await deps.embed(mem.fact); store.insert({... origin_id: originId, skipDedup: true }, embedding); store.setMetadataByOriginId(originId, encodeMeta({...}))`. store.ts:316-330 on origin_id collision: `inserted = false; return;` (INSERT OR IGNORE) and returns the existing row.
  Impact: Device sends an updated line under the same id; the server reports success, stores new savedAt/context, but list() and recall keep returning the old fact. The embedding is computed before the skip for nothing.
  Fix: Compare the returned `entry.content` with `mem.fact`; when different call the (re-embedding) updateFact path.
- [low/race] **Tier 1 read-modify-write has no lock; dream-promote and agent edits can interleave**
  Evidence: clawcode-memory-edit.ts:143-153 and :160-174 read then write with awaits in between. daemon.ts:3961-3965 `const r = await clawcodeMemoryEdit({ path: "MEMORY.md", mode: "append", content }, { memoryRoot: roots.tier1Dir, ... })` runs from the veto tick while an agent turn may be editing the same file.
  Impact: A promotion landing between the agent's readFile and writeFile is lost (or the agent's edit is), with ok:true on both sides.
  Fix: Serialize per absolute path with a module-level `Map<string, Promise<void>>` chain inside clawcodeMemoryEdit.
- [low/bug] **includeTags silently discards every chunk hit and bumps access counters on memories it filters out**
  Evidence: clawcode-memory-search.ts:179-184 `if (h.source === "chunk") return false; const entry = deps.store.getById(h.memoryId);` while store.ts:472-480 getById runs `this.stmts.updateAccess.run(now, id)`. server.ts:1738 describes includeTags as `Only return memories with at least one of these tags`.
  Impact: An agent filtering by tag loses all file-note hits without explanation, and relevance-decay/tiering clocks are reset for memories the agent never saw.
  Fix: Use `store.getMemoryForRetrieval(id)` (side-effect free, memory-retrieval.ts:543) for the tag check and say in the description that tag filters apply to saved memories only.
- [low/design-gap] **Owner facts bypass k and carry an incomparable score**
  Evidence: clawcode-memory-search.ts:120-151: retrieveMemoryChunks caps at `topK: parsed.k` (131) and then `allChunks = [...chunks, ...facts]` (151) appends up to SHARED_FACTS_RETRIEVAL_K more without re-sorting; shared-facts-store.ts:249 `fusedScore: f.similarity` (cosine 0..1) vs RRF-scale fusedScore at memory-retrieval.ts:510.
  Impact: Result counts exceed the requested k and `score` cannot be used to rank across sources; the agent may over-weight owner facts because their scores look much higher.
  Fix: Either re-sort and slice to k or add a `scoreKind` field; document in SearchHit.
- [low/test-gap] **Tests do not cover the behaviors the headers claim or the MCP registrations**
  Evidence: clawcode-memory-search-tool.test.ts:8 header lists `includeTags filter drops hits without matching tags` but the file contains no includeTags test; :149-160 excludeTags test only asserts `resolves.toBeDefined()`. capability-manifest.test.ts has no assertion containing `Memory protocol` or `clawcode_memory` (grep empty). server.test.ts touches only `TOOL_DEFINITIONS.agent_status` and invokeWithCache. No test exercises a `shared:` id through recall/archive.
  Impact: The id-namespace bug, the includeTags chunk drop, and any drift in the protocol prose would pass CI unnoticed.
  Fix: Add tests for includeTags with mixed chunk/memory hits, for recall/archive of a `shared:` id, and a manifest test pinning the protocol sentences and the four tool names.
- [low/dead-path] **TOOL_DEFINITIONS is a second, drifting source of truth for tool descriptions**
  Evidence: server.ts:145-176 define `memory_lookup: { description: "Search your memory for relevant context, past decisions, and knowledge" ...}` while the live registration at :1566-1575 is a different, longer text; TOOL_DEFINITIONS is only imported by server.test.ts:3 and list-agent-schemas.test.ts:193.
  Impact: Anyone reading or generating docs from TOOL_DEFINITIONS sees descriptions the model never receives.
  Fix: Derive the live registrations from TOOL_DEFINITIONS (description + ipcMethod) or delete the memory entries from it.
- [low/design-gap] **Agent-authored memories are stored as source "conversation" with no turn lineage, indistinguishable from auto-captured summaries**
  Evidence: daemon.ts:18567 `store.insert({ content, source: "conversation", importance, tags }, embedding)` while openai-memory-gateway.ts:71 uses `source: "manual"`, session-summarizer.ts:326 and compaction.ts:151 also use `"conversation"`; `sourceTurnIds` (types.ts:46) is never set from the tool. Recall surfaces `source: mem.source` (recall.ts:73).
  Impact: When the agent recalls a row it cannot tell whether it deliberately saved it or a summarizer produced it, which matters when deciding which of two conflicting rows to trust or forget.
  Fix: Use `source: "manual"` plus a `agent-saved` tag in the memory-save arm, and pass the active turn id as sourceTurnIds when the IPC carries one.

**Strengths.**

- Tool bodies are pure-DI with zod input schemas and structured {ok,error} results; security failures (jail, symlink) are logged and returned, never thrown (clawcode-memory-edit.ts:72-116), and the edit primitive is reused by dream-promote so there is one jailed write path.
- Agent identity is never taken from tool input: SEARCH/RECALL/EDIT/ARCHIVE schemas have no agent field (tests pin this), the daemon resolves the store from the validated agent, and enforceAgentIdentity rejects cross-agent access when a token is present (agent-identity-token.ts:141-148).
- Phase 999.187 unified the Tier 1 directory (memory-roots.ts) so the prompt loader and the edit/archive tools read and write the same MEMORY.md/USER.md, with a logged legacy fallback (session-config.ts:681-706).
- The lazy search deliberately disables the 14-day window (clawcode-memory-search.ts:132-136), so 'search all time' in the description is true, and owner facts ride in fail-open with a warning (145-155).
- Snippets are capped at 500 chars and the full body is only fetched on explicit recall, which keeps search results small.
- Researcher policy is layered (SDK disallowedTools plus a PreToolUse hook), read-only clawcode allowlist, deny-wins over operator widening, read-only SQL detection, and it is well tested (research-tool-policy.test.ts).
- memory_save's owner scope is clearly described and the daemon derives the owner from config, never from the call (daemon.ts:18541-18546).
- The iOS gateway uses an idempotent origin_id namespace, scopes list() to ios:% rows only, round-trips device metadata, and has end-to-end endpoint tests (memory-endpoints.test.ts).

## What the audit did not cover

- **Client fact vault: src/manager/client-vault.ts plus the client_fact_set / client_facts MCP tools (src/mcp/server.ts:1434-1510) and daemon cases client-fact-set / client-facts (src/manager/daemon.ts ~15530-15590)** It is a persistent memory store that no auditor covered, and it is the only place in the codebase with real supersession (current value plus up to 20 dated prior values per key). Its facts are reachable only by an explicit tool call: they are not mentioned in the capability manifest, not injected, and not indexed (facts.json is not .md and lives in <workspace>/clients, outside the scanned memory dir). Writes are read-modify-write with no lock, and the vault is keyed on workspace rather than memoryPath, so shared-workspace agents share it. Several audits state that no supersession exists anywhere, which is only true for the memories table. How to fill: Audit it as a memory subsystem: who writes it, whether agents are told it exists, concurrency, scoping. Decide whether to surface a per-client summary in the prompt or index facts.json, and whether its supersede/history model should be reused for memories.
- **Delegated-work consolidation: src/discord/subagent-consolidation.ts, called from src/discord/subagent-thread-spawner.ts ~2308** This is the only live path that persists subagent output. It writes one source=consolidation memory (task truncated to 200 chars, findings to 1200 chars) into the DELEGATE agent's store, with dedup on at 0.85. The parent agent that asked for the work gets nothing. The capture audit called subagent capture fully dead without noticing this path. How to fill: Audit it next to subagent-capture.ts. Decide whether the parent should also receive a record (skipDedup, provenance tag), and whether 1200 chars of findings is enough to recall a research result.
- **Derived sessions open a second MemoryStore on the parent's memories.db (src/heartbeat/shared-memory-guard.ts)** Only the maintenance heartbeat checks skip derived sessions. Nobody traced whether fork and warm-pool sessions (as opposed to thread subagents, which subagent-config-overrides.ts disables) run mid-session flush timers, disk flushes, storeSoulMemory, conversation sessions and brief assembly against the parent's DB. Any rows they write carry no agent scoping, so they can appear in the parent's retrieval, brief and search. How to fill: Trace startAgent for each spawn kind returned by spawnKindOf, list which timers and writers start, and count rows tagged with fork session ids in a production DB.
- **Workflow run memory: src/manager/workflow-summary/previous-run.ts and workflow-reports.ts** Workflow findings persist only as run.json beside the artifacts and are read only by the next run of the same template. They never enter ConversationStore, memories or memory_chunks, so an agent cannot recall what a workflow found when asked in conversation. How to fill: Decide whether workflow completion should insert a memory or write a dated memory/*.md file, then add it to the capture audit.
- **Turn-time image understanding when vision.mode is inline (bridge.ts formatDiscordMessage with opts.inlineImages)** With inline vision on, images travel as native blocks and the stored user turn keeps only attachment metadata and paths (the analysis lines are skipped). When autoIngestAttachments is off, nothing records what the image showed except whatever the assistant said in its reply. How to fill: Check which agents set vision.enabled and mode inline, and decide whether to persist a short caption into the captured user turn or a memory.
- **Live production data** No auditor inspected a real memories.db, registry, memory directory or log stream (there is no live daemon on this Mac). The severity of the mid-session flood, scanner gaps, cut-off stop summaries, missing flush files, consolidation threshold, reranker fallbacks and envelope dilution all depend on real counts. How to fill: On the production host: sqlite counts of memories by tag and source; memory_chunks by path prefix compared with memory/*.md files on disk; conversation_sessions by status with turn counts and summary_memory_id; migrations.phase per agent. Grep logs for 'session flush written' vs 'flush: empty summary', 'phase115-tag-filter', 'summary-fail-loud', reranker fallbacks and 'stable-prefix-cap-fallback'.
- **End-to-end recall evaluation with production-shaped inputs** recall-eval uses a hash embedder, short fixtures and bare queries, so it cannot detect envelope-wrapped queries, 200-word embedding truncation, path-weight dominance, KNN-then-filter crowding or the 14-day post-fetch window. Every top issue below passes CI. How to fill: Extend src/memory/eval with the real MiniLM/bge models, envelope-wrapped queries, long chunks, competing vault and document chunks, and a flood of mid-session rows, then assert recall floors.
- **Embedding v2 phase per agent** Under cutover, searchMemoriesVec and searchMemoryChunksVec INNER JOIN the v2 tables. Dedup merges delete the v2 vector, updateContentByOriginId leaves it stale, and rows without a v1 vector are skipped by the backfill. Nobody established which agents read from v2, so the size of this hidden-row class is unknown. How to fill: Query the migrations table per agent, count memories and chunks missing v2 rows, and confirm whether any agent is in cutover.
- **Lifecycle tests for SessionManager did not run** session-manager.test.ts failed in its beforeAll embedding warmup, so the timer, restart, scanner-teardown and brief-gap behaviour that several top issues depend on has no passing test coverage in this run. How to fill: Rerun with a cached model or stub EmbeddingService in beforeAll, then add tests for scanner recreation after restartAgent and for the brief after an unfinished stop.

## Contradictions between auditors and how they were resolved

- memory.deduplication: the capture audit marks it wired, while the storage and tools audits say it is inert. Resolved as inert. session-memory.ts:71 calls new MemoryStore(dbPath) with no dedup config, so store.ts:200 always falls back to DEFAULT_DEDUP_CONFIG (enabled, 0.85). Dedup is always on at 0.85 and the YAML knob cannot change it.
- systemPromptDirectives, including memory-recall-before-uncertainty: the tools audit says it is wired through context-assembler.ts:1374; the injection audit and the restart trace say it is never injected. Resolved as never injected. The assembler renders sources.systemPromptDirectives only when a caller sets it, the ContextSources literal in session-config.ts has no such key, and renderSystemPromptDirectiveBlock / resolveSystemPromptDirectives have no non-test caller (grep). The tools audit's point that the directive steers recall to memory_lookup is therefore moot. What still steers to memory_lookup is session-config.ts:614 and the fingerprint default instruction.
- createdAt and footer dates: the injection audit says the date reaches the Discord recall footer but not the model; the retrieval audit, tools audit and fact-lifecycle trace say it is dropped. Resolved as dropped. memory-retrieval.ts hydrates createdAt (~561), but the frozen output object (~683-691) omits it. summarizeRecall never sees a date, so the footer range is always empty and the model never gets dates either.
- context-summary.md: the injection audit says it always holds the heartbeat placeholder and is injected at every boot; the capture audit and restart trace say it is never written in production. Resolved as never written in practice. Its only writer is the zone-snapshot callback (daemon.ts ~5590), which needs an upward zone transition. CharacterCountFillProvider.addTurn has no production caller, so fill stays at 0 and the zone stays green. The Context Summary slot is empty unless a legacy file survives from an older build. Compaction's real summary is reduced to a boolean (daemon-compact-session-ipc.ts:414).
- consolidation.weeklyThreshold: the dreaming and capture audits give a fleet default of 4; the storage audit gives 7 with a config default of 4; the fact-lifecycle trace gives 7. Resolved: it depends on the YAML shape. The config/schema.ts:3672 default (4) applies only when defaults.memory is absent. If any defaults.memory block exists, zod fills consolidation from memory/schema.ts consolidationConfigSchema, which defaults to 7. This repo's clawcode.yaml:41-43 sets only compactionThreshold and searchTopK, so the effective value here is 7 and the 999.187 '7 to 4' fix does not reach this deployment.
- Scanner boot backfill: the capture audit lists 'avoids re-embedding unchanged files on boot backfill' as a strength, and the daemon.ts comment (~5491) says backfill runs at boot; the fact-lifecycle trace says it never runs. Resolved as never running in production. MemoryScanner.start uses chokidar ignoreInitial:true (memory-scanner.ts:107), and .backfill( is called only from cli/commands/memory-backfill.ts. In addition, stopAgentCore deletes the scanner (session-manager.ts ~2485) and only daemon boot creates one (daemon.ts:5516), so a per-agent restart leaves the agent unscanned until the daemon restarts.
- Brief gap rule: the conversation audit says the gap skip never fires once sessions are summarized; the fact-lifecycle trace says the brief is skipped on every quick restart; the restart trace says a crash restart withholds it. Resolved: each is true under different conditions. listRecentTerminatedSessions requires that turns still exist (conversation-store.ts ~1198-1210), and summarizeSession prunes turns, so after a completed clean stop the gap check ignores that session and the brief is injected. When the previous session still holds turns, the gap is about 0 and the whole brief (including older summaries) is skipped. That happens when the stop-path summary is cut by the 10s stopAll step or 20s watchdog, when a crash restart (about 2s backoff) races the fire-and-forget summary, or when closeStaleActiveSessions stamps an orphan ended_at=now just before buildSessionConfig (session-manager.ts:1303 then 1320). Given the shutdown budget, the skip is the common case.
- memory.decay: the storage audit marks it wired and the retrieval audit marks it inert. Resolved as partially wired. Only TierManager.scoringConfig reads it (session-memory.ts:149-153). retrieveMemoryChunks uses DEFAULT_MEMORY_SCORING (no caller passes scoring), SemanticSearch is constructed without it, and shouldArchiveToCold hardcodes 30 days. It changes only hot-tier promotion tie-breaks.
- Mid-session rows: the capture audit says they are never read; the conversation and tools audits say they surface in explicit search. Resolved as read. They are excluded from pre-turn retrieval, the brief, the digest and summary-dedupe. They are returned by clawcode_memory_search (the daemon passes only agent-supplied excludeTags, daemon.ts:18600), by memory_lookup's searchByScope memory path (which skips only session-summary) and by GraphSearch, where they crowd out real hits.
- Subagent output: the capture audit says subagent reports are discarded exactly as before Phase 90. Partly wrong. handleTaskToolReturn has no production caller, so Task-tool returns to the parent are still lost. But the unaudited src/discord/subagent-consolidation.ts writes one memory into the DELEGATE agent's store for delegateTo thread spawns. The parent still receives nothing, and that record goes through dedup at 0.85.
- Supersession: the storage audit, retrieval audit and dream trace say none exists anywhere. src/manager/client-vault.ts keeps current values plus dated history per key through client_fact_set. Supersession exists, but only for structured client facts, which are not injected, not indexed and not in the manifest.
- Summarizer minTurns, timeoutMs and importance: the conversation audit marks them wired and the capture audit marks them not wired. Resolved as not operator-tunable. SessionManager never passes deps.config to summarizeSession or flushSessionMidway (flushSessionIfPossible passes no config), so the code constants always apply.
- Path weights: the retrieval audit lists only vault, procedures and archive weights; the documents audit and trace give document: chunks +0.2. Resolved: both apply. cross-ingest.ts:224 hardcodes scoreWeight 0.2 for every document chunk, and applyDocumentPriorityWeight then multiplies the already-inflated score. Document chunks dominate as well, bounded only by the 2-chunk cap.

# Research: memory systems for agent harnesses

The seven reports cover the 2023 to 2026 memory literature thoroughly but have three blind spots: production evidence (GitHub Copilot's citation-verified memory is the only significance-tested A/B found: merge rate 83% to 90%), 2026 benchmarks closer to tool-using agents (LongMemEval-V2, Supersede, ConvoMem, Evo-Memory), and context-management measurements (Complexity Trap observation masking, Chroma Context Rot, Factory's probe-based compaction eval). Close analogues worth benchmarking against are claude-mem (same SDK, SQLite plus FTS5) and the 2026 evidence-grounded designs CueMem and Agent Zero Memory. MemPalace is a cautionary example of recall reported as accuracy and of tuning on the test set.

I fetched primary sources to resolve 13 contradictions. Most come from paper versions or mismatched metrics: MemoryAgentBench v2 'at most 7%' vs v4 'at most 28%'; Supermemory's 97% is Recall@20, set against competitors' QA accuracy; Mem0's 91.6/93.4 (April) vs 92.5/94.4 (May); Mem0's OSS top_k 20 vs benchmark top_200; LoCoMo's 9K tokens (50-conversation release) vs 26K (the public 10). The reranking dispute depends on the regime: a strong reranker over a wide document pool helps, while a small MS MARCO cross-encoder over a narrow conversational pool hurts. Recency helps conflict resolution but hurts as a global prior. Whether retrieval or writes dominate failures depends on whether the store keeps raw dialogue or is agent-curated. Graphs help only when they keep verbatim evidence and provenance. One contradiction stays unresolved: no source measures consolidation cadence.

I verified two ClawCode facts in the code. First, the +/-0.2 path weights are added to RRF scores that top out at 0.0328 (memory-retrieval.ts line 510, memory-chunks.ts line 151), so path class dominates ranking despite the comments. Second, document cross-ingest writes chunks with heading null.

The strongest-evidence recall levers are cheap indexing choices: keep verbatim turns, use BM25 as a candidate channel, use fine-grained units with max-sim, add contextual headers and fact keys pointing to the parent record, and handle supersession explicitly with source dates. Graph databases, LLM importance scoring and default-on off-the-shelf rerankers are not supported. No files were edited.

### Techniques ranked by strength of evidence

1. **Keep raw turns and episodes indexed and retrievable; extracted facts and summaries are pointers, not replacements** (academic; mem0-zep; evaluation-and-images). Most independent support of any technique. LongMemEval (ICLR 2025, https://arxiv.org/html/2410.10813v2): replacing values with summaries or facts hurts except on multi-session. Entity graph vs verbatim (https://arxiv.org/html/2608.28978v1): single-session-assistant correctness 0.911 to 0.607, paired bootstrap CI excludes zero. Zep paper single-session-assistant 94.6 to 80.4. ConvoMem (https://arxiv.org/html/2511.10523v1): full context 70 to 82% vs Mem0 30 to 45%. LongMemEval-V2 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12493): coding agent over raw trajectory files 72.5% vs RAG 48.5%.
2. **Hybrid BM25 plus dense retrieval with BM25 as its own candidate channel** (retrieval-engineering; anthropic-guidance). Consistent gains across sources and corpora: Anthropic contextual BM25 cut top-20 failures 3.7% to 2.9% (https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/contextual-retrieval); on LoCoMo BM25 alone 0.640 Hit@1 vs 0.38 to 0.43 for mean-pooled dense, and fusion adds 8.8 to 17.2 pp over dense (https://arxiv.org/html/2606.04194); vstash +16% NDCG@10 over vector-only on 1,000 papers (https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15484). ClawCode already does this; the point is not to demote BM25 to a boost.
3. **Fine-grained retrieval units (turns, rounds or topical segments) scored by max-sim, not mean-pooled sessions** (retrieval-engineering; academic). Large and significant effects: max-sim beat mean pooling by 13.5 to 23.7 pp Hit@1 across six encoders, p<1e-4 (https://arxiv.org/html/2606.04194); LongMemEval rounds beat sessions; SeCom (ICLR 2025, https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.05589v1) finds topical segments beat both turn and session units; LightMem loses 5.4 to 6.3 points without topic segmentation (https://arxiv.org/html/2510.18866v4).
4. **Contextual headers on every chunk before embedding and FTS (document or session, page, date, subject)** (anthropic-guidance; evaluation-and-images; retrieval-engineering). A controlled ablation with large relative effect: top-20 failure 5.7% to 3.7% (contextual embeddings) to 2.9% (plus contextual BM25), 1.9% with reranking (https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/contextual-retrieval); the deterministic header variant needs no LLM. Directly fixes a verified ClawCode gap: src/document-ingest/cross-ingest.ts writes chunks with heading: null.
5. **Fact and keyword key expansion that points back to the parent raw record** (academic; retrieval-engineering). Peer-reviewed ablation: keys = value + extracted facts raised recall@k 9.4% and QA accuracy 5.4% on average (round R@10 0.692 to 0.784, session 0.783 to 0.862), while fact-only keys were worse (https://arxiv.org/html/2410.10813v2). A-MEM's link ablation and CueMem 2026 (cues linked to source turns, https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12354) point the same way.
6. **Explicit supersession with valid-time columns and writes dated from the source message** (mem0-zep; academic). Knowledge updates are where retrieval alone fails: MemoryAgentBench v4 retrieval and memory systems near zero on multi-hop updates, best 28% (https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05257); Supersede shows bounded memory 77% vs full context 92% on knowledge updates even with gpt-5.4 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27472); Zep's bi-temporal edges lifted knowledge-update 78.2 to 83.3; Mem0 #4956 went 54 to 59 (dated writes) to 68 of 76 (date ordering) (https://github.com/mem0ai/mem0/issues/4956). ClawCode's dedup overwrite at cosine 0.85 is the opposite behavior.
7. **Agentic, iterative search with plain file and grep tools plus a recall-first retry** (memgpt-letta; harnesses; academic). Three independent setups agree: LongMemEval-V2 coding agent 72.5% vs best RAG 48.5% (https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12493); Letta's grep-and-open agent 74.0% on LoCoMo vs 68.5% for Mem0g (https://www.letta.com/blog/benchmarking-ai-agent-memory); EvalMem's recall-first agentic retrieval raised evidence recall 70.2% to 95.6% on LoCoMo (https://arxiv.org/html/2609.22231). Fits Claude Agent SDK agents, which are strong at file tools.
8. **Read-time verification of memories against their cited sources, with refresh-on-use expiry** (not in any report (completeness critic: GitHub Copilot memory)). The only production A/B with significance tests found: Copilot coding agent merge rate 83% to 90% and code review recall +4% and precision +3% (p<0.00001) with citation-checked memories (https://github.blog/ai-and-ml/github-copilot/building-an-agentic-memory-system-for-github-copilot/). It addresses stale memories without LLM consolidation, which suits ClawCode memories about files and vault notes.
9. **Time-aware query handling: parse dates in the query and filter or boost by event time** (academic; retrieval-engineering; mem0-zep). LongMemEval time-aware expansion raised temporal recall 6.8% (sessions) to 11.3% (rounds) with a strong expander, but not with an 8B model (https://arxiv.org/html/2410.10813v2); Zep temporal-reasoning 45.1 to 62.4 with event-time metadata (https://arxiv.org/html/2501.13956); Mem0 #3944 shows ~0.20 on LoCoMo when the wrong date is stamped. ClawCode uses a fixed 14-day cutoff instead.
10. **Wider, token-budgeted candidate pool for a strong reader** (retrieval-engineering; anthropic-guidance). Anthropic found top-20 beat top-10 and top-5 (https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/contextual-retrieval); RMM accuracy rose to 73.8 with K=50, M=10 (https://arxiv.org/html/2503.08026); GPT-4o kept improving past 20K retrieved tokens in LongMemEval. ClawCode's pre-turn path injects 5 chunks in 1,500 tokens, below every tested optimum for a Claude-class reader. Context Rot (https://www.trychroma.com/research/context-rot) caps this at relevance-gated sizes.
11. **Score-based fusion tuned on labeled queries, with path priors sized to the fused score scale** (retrieval-engineering). Tuned convex combination beat RRF(60) on 9 of 9 datasets by 1.5 to 3.2 NDCG (https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/2210.11934); weighted z-score fusion 0.752 vs RRF 0.718 Hit@1 on LoCoMo (https://arxiv.org/html/2606.04194). The verified ClawCode bug (+0.2 path weight vs a maximum RRF score of 0.0328) means the current ranking is ordered by path class first, so fixing the scale is a zero-cost recall fix.
12. **Outcome-gated writes and utility-based archiving for experience and procedural memory** (academic). Controlled study (https://arxiv.org/html/2505.16067): add-all was the worst policy on 3 of 4 agents (EHRAgent 13.05 vs strict addition 38.50) and history-based deletion raised EHRAgent 38.67 to 42.06; ReasoningBank with failure-derived items gained 4.6 to 8.3 WebArena points (https://arxiv.org/html/2509.25140). Relevant to dream promotions, which currently have no outcome signal.
13. **Usage and citation feedback as a ranking signal** (academic; harnesses). RMM's citation-rewarded reranker raised LongMemEval accuracy 63.6 to 70.4 and Recall@5 62.4 to 69.8, with citation labels matching an LLM judge at ~87% precision (https://arxiv.org/html/2503.08026); Codex ranks consolidation inputs by cited usage. A cheap SQL counter in ClawCode instead of access counts that treat retrieved as used.
14. **Pre-compaction flush and memory-pressure warning, with old tool outputs masked rather than summarized** (memgpt-letta; anthropic-guidance; harnesses). Anthropic reports +39% on an internal agentic-search eval for memory tool plus context editing (vendor, https://claude.com/blog/context-management); MemGPT's paging lifted DMR from 32.1% to 92.5% (older, https://arxiv.org/html/2310.08560v2); observation masking matches LLM summaries at about half the cost (https://arxiv.org/html/2508.21433v3). Weaker evidence than items above, but it closes a known ClawCode gap (no warning before /compact).
15. **Chain-of-Note structured reading of retrieved memories** (academic; evaluation-and-images). Peer-reviewed and cheap: reading notes first with JSON-formatted context added up to 10 points; GPT-4o oracle 0.870 to 0.924 and full S 0.606 to 0.640 (https://arxiv.org/html/2410.10813v2). It improves how much retrieved evidence turns into a correct answer, not retrieval itself, which is why it ranks last.

### Sources the sweep missed

- GitHub Copilot agentic memory (GitHub engineering blog, Jan 9 2026; docs.github.com copilot-memory). Each memory is a fact with citations to code locations; the agent re-reads the cited locations before using a memory, stores a corrected memory if the code contradicts it, refreshes it on successful use, and unused memories expire after 28 days. Add a citations column (path plus line range or content hash) to memories and to dream promotions; on a retrieval hit, re-hash or re-read the cited file and either refresh last_validated_at or mark the memory suspect; archive memories not validated within N days. Add stale-fact probes to the recall fixture and A/B it. Sources: https://github.blog/ai-and-ml/github-copilot/building-an-agentic-memory-system-for-github-copilot/ and https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/concepts/agents/copilot-memory
- Supersede: Diagnosing and Training the Memory-Update Gap in LLM Agents (arXiv 2606.27472, Jun 25 2026, single author). Add knowledge-update probes (fact stated, later changed) to the fixture, run them with Tier 1 only, with retrieval, and with a full-history arm; implement valid_from/valid_to/superseded_by before tuning caps. Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27472
- ConvoMem benchmark (Salesforce, arXiv 2511.10523, Nov 2025): 75,336 QA pairs; 'why your first 150 conversations don't need RAG'. Add a full-history arm to the ClawCode eval and record cost and p95 latency per conversation count; for agents under the crossover, consider loading recent session summaries or transcripts wholesale into the cached prefix instead of top-5 retrieval. Source: https://arxiv.org/html/2511.10523v1
- LongMemEval-V2 (Wu et al., arXiv 2605.12493, May 2026; ICML 2026): 451 questions on web-agent experience (static state recall, dynamic state tracking, workflow knowledge, environment gotchas, premise awareness) over histories of 25M to 115M tokens. Persist per-session tool trajectories as plain files in the agent workspace (not only summaries); write fixture probes of the five LME-V2 types from real work (for example Power Platform import gotchas, Ivanti workflow steps). Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12493
- The Complexity Trap: Simple Observation Masking Is as Efficient as LLM Summarization for Agent Context Management (JetBrains Research and TU Munich, arXiv 2508.21433, NeurIPS 2025). In the bench harness compare mask-only (M=10), summarize-only and hybrid on ClawCode tasks before tuning the compaction prompt; mask old tool results before any summarization call. Source: https://arxiv.org/html/2508.21433v3
- Chroma 'Context Rot' technical report (Jul 14 2025), 18 models including Claude 4, GPT-4.1, Gemini 2.5, Qwen3. Add a relevance gate to pre-turn retrieval and test injection budgets (0, 500, 1500, 4000 tokens) plus distractor probes in the eval. Source: https://www.trychroma.com/research/context-rot
- Evidence-grounded memory designs from 2026: CueMem (arXiv 2609.12354, Sep 2026; extracted records are retrieval cues linked to their source turns, evidence rebuilt by expanding temporal and semantic links) and Agent Zero Memory (arXiv 2608.29606, Aug 30 2026; episodic timeline plus entity-event graph plus citation-locked documentary memory, answers restricted to evidence the reader opened, abstains otherwise; self-reported 95.6% LongMemEval, 93.6% LoCoMo, 3.4-point spread across eight backbones). Make memory hits return the linked source turns (plus or minus one) and add abstention probes; treat both papers' scores as self-reported until reproduced. Sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12354 and https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.29606
- claude-mem (thedotmack), an open-source Claude Code plugin: PostToolUse hooks send every tool call to a background Claude Agent SDK worker that writes structured observations (title, narrative, facts, file refs) into SQLite with FTS5 and optional Chroma vectors; search is three-step progressive disclosure (search index of ~50 to 100 tokens per result, timeline, get_observations at ~500 to 1000 tokens); SessionStart injects a recent timeline. Review its observation schema and search/timeline/get API; wire it as a baseline arm in a MemoryBench-style adapter alongside a grep-only arm. Sources: https://docs.claude-mem.ai/architecture/overview and https://www.mintlify.com/thedotmack/claude-mem/architecture/overview
- MemPalace (open source, Apr 2026) and its benchmark controversy. Add a verbatim-chunks plus default-embedder baseline arm; always report Recall@k and judged QA accuracy in separate columns; keep a held-out split that is never used for tuning. Sources: https://vectorize.io/articles/mempalace-review and https://hackernoon.com/resident-evil-star-milla-jovovich-shipped-an-ai-memory-system-devs-shredded-its-benchmarks
- PrefEval (Zhao et al., ICLR 2025 oral, arXiv 2502.09597): 3,000 preference-query pairs over 20 topics, explicit and implicit preferences, multi-session conversations up to 100k tokens. Add explicit and implicit preference probes to the fixture and test placement (stable prefix vs a reminder next to the query). Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.09597v1
- SeCom (Pan et al., ICLR 2025, arXiv 2502.05589): memory units built from topical segments with LLMLingua-2 denoising. A/B segment-level units against turn-level and H2-chunk units on the recall fixture. Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.05589v1
- Evo-Memory (Google DeepMind and UIUC, arXiv 2511.20857, Nov 2025, v2 May 2026): benchmark of test-time learning over sequential task streams with 10+ memory modules, ExpRAG baseline and ReMem method. Replay sequences of real past tasks and measure success with and without lesson or procedure retrieval, using ExpRAG (retrieve prior experience) as the simple baseline. Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.20857
- Factory 'Evaluating Context Compression for AI Agents' (Dec 16 2025): probe-based evaluation of compaction (factual retention, file tracking, task planning, reasoning chains) over 36,000+ production messages. After forced compaction in the bench harness, ask probes about files touched, decisions and next steps and score retention for each compaction prompt. Sources: https://factory.ai/news/evaluating-compression and https://docs.factory.ai/guides/power-user/evaluating-context-compression

### Per-topic principles

#### MemGPT / Letta style self-editing memory: core memory blocks, archival and recall memory, agent-edited memory via tools, memory pressure and paging, sleep-time compute agents, and Letta's 2026 move to git-backed MemFS with dreaming. Includes an audit of ClawCode's existing memory stack (src/memory, src/manager/dream-*, clawcode.yaml) against these designs.

- Split always-in-context memory into small, labeled, size-limited units with a description of how each should be used, and show the agent how full each unit is. Evidence: Memory blocks have label, description, value, limit and read_only; they render with chars_current/chars_limit metadata; Letta recommends blocks under about 50k chars and under about 20 per agent. MemGPT's working context was a single fixed-size block; Letta's sleep-time post says that incremental formation in one block gets messy over time. Source: https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/memory-blocks ; https://docs.letta.com/v1-sdk/memory/context-hierarchy/ ; https://letta.com/blog/sleep-time-compute
- Give the agent both precise edit tools and a whole-unit rewrite tool, and tell it which to use when: narrow edits for additions and corrections, rethink only for condensing or reorganising. Evidence: memory_replace docstring: 'do NOT attempt to replace long strings'; memory_rethink: 'make large sweeping changes... do NOT use this tool to make small precise edits'; sleep-time prompt: chain precise edits or use rethink, then call finish. Source: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/letta-ai/letta/0.16.8/letta/functions/function_sets/base.py ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/letta-ai/letta/0.16.8/letta/prompts/system_prompts/sleeptime_v2.py
- Warn the agent before eviction and keep every evicted message retrievable; compact by summarising the older fraction, not everything. Evidence: MemGPT inserts a memory-pressure system message at about 70 percent of the window and flushes at about 100 percent, keeping evicted messages in recall storage. Letta's constants carry MESSAGE_SUMMARY_WARNING_STR ('save that information immediately by calling core_memory_append, core_memory_replace, or archival_memory_insert'); compaction defaults summarise 30 percent and keep 70 percent with a 50,000-char clip. Source: https://arxiv.org/html/2310.08560v2 ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/letta-ai/letta/0.16.8/letta/constants.py ; https://docs.letta.com/v1-sdk/messages/compaction/
- Move memory maintenance off the hot path to a background agent that shares the memory, runs on a turn count or on compaction, and has the editing tools the primary agent does not. Evidence: Primary agent tools are send_message, conversation_search, archival_memory_search; sleep-time agent tools are memory_replace, memory_insert, memory_rethink, memory_finish_edits; it runs after every turn unless sleeptime_agent_frequency sets a modulus. Letta Code dreaming triggers are step-count or compaction-event. Paper: about 5x less test-time compute for equal accuracy and up to 13 to 18 percent accuracy gains when sleep-time compute is scaled. Source: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/letta-ai/letta/0.16.8/letta/constants.py ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/letta-ai/letta/0.16.8/letta/groups/sleeptime_multi_agent_v4.py ; https://docs.letta.com/agent-sdk/memory ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13171
- One owner for rewrites; everyone else appends. Rewrites of shared memory are last-write-wins. Evidence: Letta's shared-memory guide: memory_insert concurrent-safe, memory_replace mostly, memory_rethink not; 'Multiple agents doing memory_rethink on the same block simultaneously leads to lost updates'; designate the sleep-time agent as owner. Source: https://docs.letta.com/guides/core-concepts/memory/shared-memory/
- Write absolute dates into memory and be selective but high-recall; finishing with no edit is a valid outcome. Evidence: Sleep-time prompt: 'do not write today or recently, instead write specific dates and times, because... the memory is persisted indefinitely'; 'If there are no meaningful updates... call the finish tool directly'; 'be selective in your memory editing, but also aim to have high recall'. The leaderboard penalises extraneous memory operations. Source: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/letta-ai/letta/0.16.8/letta/prompts/system_prompts/sleeptime_v2.py ; https://www.letta.com/blog/letta-leaderboard
- Version memory with git: every edit is a commit, background workers edit in worktrees and merge, and the diff is the audit trail. Evidence: MemFS commits every edit; dreaming and memory doctor use git worktrees so they do not block the main agent; the context-repositories post says memory formation used to be single-threaded because there was no way to coordinate concurrent writes. Source: https://docs.letta.com/concepts/memfs ; https://www.letta.com/blog/context-repositories
- Progressive disclosure: always load a small root (identity, key preferences, durable facts, critical rules) plus an index; load everything else on demand and keep the file tree visible so names are navigation. Evidence: Files at the memory root load into the system prompt every turn; directories with their own MEMORY.md stay out of context until needed and the root MEMORY.md lists them; the file tree is always in the system prompt and frontmatter descriptions act like SKILL.md. Source: https://docs.letta.com/concepts/memfs ; https://www.letta.com/blog/context-repositories
- Audit and defragment memory periodically: find duplicates, misplaced content and prompt-token bloat, back up, then split large files and merge duplicates toward a small number of focused files. Evidence: /doctor audits placement, duplication and system-prompt token usage; defragmentation backs up the repo then restructures into 15 to 25 focused files; 0.32.0 added default budget enforcement. Source: https://docs.letta.com/configuration/memory ; https://www.letta.com/blog/context-repositories ; https://docs.letta.com/letta-agent/changelog
- Prefer tools the model is already trained on (grep, file read, bash) over bespoke memory APIs; the agent's ability to iterate on search matters more than the store. Evidence: A filesystem agent with grep/search_files/open/close scored 74.0 percent on LoCoMo with GPT-4o mini versus Mem0's reported 68.5 percent; Letta attributes it to post-training on filesystem operations and in March 2026 replaced its memory tools with bash-style tools over MemFS. Source: https://www.letta.com/blog/benchmarking-ai-agent-memory ; https://www.letta.com/blog/our-next-phase
- Memory is a harness responsibility: what enters the system prompt, what survives compaction, whether the agent may rewrite its own instructions, and how memory metadata is presented cannot be bolted on through a plugin. Evidence: Wooders lists harness decisions a plugin cannot control (how AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md load, whether skill metadata is in the system prompt, what survives compaction, whether interactions are stored and queryable). Source: https://www.letta.com/blog/why-memory-isnt-a-plugin
- Keep bulky data out of memory; store summaries and retrieval links, and cap the repository. Evidence: Free plan 5 MiB and Pro 50 MiB per memory repository; 'Keep bulky datasets, archives, media, and project files outside memory. Store concise summaries and retrieval links in memory instead.' Source: https://docs.letta.com/configuration/memory
- Evaluate memory with the framework and tools held constant, cover read, write and update (contradiction) separately, penalise needless memory operations, and test long-lived agents as well as fresh ones. Evidence: Letta Leaderboard tasks: core read, archival read, write then clear history, update after contradiction, GPT-4.1 grader, penalty for extraneous operations; Letta Evals: evaluate design changes against both new and existing agents. Source: https://www.letta.com/blog/letta-leaderboard ; https://www.letta.com/blog/letta-evals

Anti-patterns: Treating RAG or vector search as the memory system: it is reactive (only surfaces what is semantically near the query), single-shot, and cosine similarity is noisy; the gold document often sits past the first dozen results (Letta 'RAG Is Not Agent Memory', Feb 2025; MemGPT document QA notes). Memory also needs a write path and in-context state.; Letting several agents rewrite the same shared memory unit: memory_rethink on a shared block is last-writer-wins and loses updates (docs.letta.com shared-memory guide).; Relative time words in persisted memory ('today', 'recently') that go stale (Letta sleep-time prompt).; Bolting memory on as a plugin or MCP server with no control over the system prompt, compaction survival or how memory metadata is shown; retrieval alone is a small part of memory (Letta 'Why Memory Isn't a Plugin', Apr 2026).; Incremental memory formation with no reorganisation step: MemGPT-style append-and-replace gets messy and disorganised over time (Letta sleep-time post); defrag/doctor passes are the fix.; Evicting the whole history at compaction, or dumping everything into context; keep roughly 70 percent and summarise the rest (Letta agent-memory post, compaction defaults).; Over-eager memory operations: models that write memory on every observation are penalised on Letta's leaderboard (Haiku 3.5 example).; Trusting vendor-run memory benchmarks: the same product scores 58 to 95 on LoCoMo depending on who runs it, LoCoMo never scores knowledge updates, and Letta could not reproduce Mem0's MemGPT numbers.; ClawCode gap: memoryAutoLoadPath on all four work agents points at ~/Knowledgebase/MEMORY.md, so resolveMemoryRoots makes the shared vault index the agent-writable Tier 1 file (clawcode_memory_edit, clawcode_memory_archive and dream promotion append there, USER.md lands in the vault, four writers, no lock, and daemon-side IPC bypasses the fileAccess sandbox). Fix before start-all.; ClawCode gap: the memory scanner indexes only <workspace>/memory/**, so the 301 vault notes are not in FTS5 or sqlite-vec and clawcode_memory_search cannot find them; agents must fall back to grep.; ClawCode gap: no memory-pressure warning to the agent; contextFill thresholds (0.6 / 0.75) only drive telemetry and the heartbeat /compact, so the agent never gets MemGPT's 'save now' alert before eviction.; ClawCode gap: MEMORY.md is a single 16,000-char blob with 70/20 head-tail truncation that silently drops the middle from the prompt; there are no sections with limits, no rethink/insert tools, and dream edit/merge candidates carry no replacement text so they are downgraded to appends.; ClawCode gap: dreaming is off by default, idle-only (30 min) and cron-driven, with a history of being unwired for 5+ days in production; no step-count or compaction-event trigger, and it emits suggestions rather than tool-driven edits.; ClawCode gap: insert-time dedup at cosine >= 0.85 replaces the old memory's content with the new one, so a similar-but-different fact can be silently overwritten and no update/contradiction record exists.; ClawCode gap: mid-session flush files (YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM.md) never match the weekly consolidation pattern (YYYY-MM-DD.md) and so are never digested or archived; importance is a syntactic heuristic (length, numbers, proper nouns) rather than an agent judgement.; ClawCode gap: no version history for agent edits to MEMORY.md (str_replace is irreversible; only dream promotions get a 30-minute veto); two stale tests fail in src/memory (conversation-brief gap-skip) and the README states the full suite is not green.

Systems reviewed:

- **MemGPT (paper, Oct 2023 / v2 Feb 2024; older source)**: Treats the LLM context window like OS virtual memory: a bounded main context plus external storage, with the LLM itself paging data in and out through function calls, and a queue manager that warns and then flushes when the window fills. Memory model: Main context = read-only system instructions + a fixed-size read/write 'working context' (persona and user facts, editable only via function calls) + a FIFO message queue whose first entry is a recursive summary of evicted messages. External context = recall storage (every message ever, searched by text/date) and archival storage (arbitrary-length text, embedding search, paginated). Writes: the LLM calls functions (working_context.append/replace, archival insert, recall/archival search) and can chain calls with request_heartbeat=true; a yield ends the step. Memory pressure: when prompt tokens pass a warning threshold (about 70 percent of the window) a system message tells the model eviction is imminent so it can save facts; at the flush threshold (about 100 percent) the queue manager evicts a block of messages (about 50 percent of the window), writes a new recursive summary from the old summary plus evicted messages, and the evicted messages stay in recall storage. Retrieval is paginated so tool results cannot overflow the window. Applicable to ClawCode: Copy the memory-pressure warning: ClawCode already computes context fill (heartbeat contextFill warningThreshold 0.6 / criticalThreshold 0.75 in src/config/schema.ts) but only uses it for telemetry and to fire the native /compact (src/manager/daemon-compact-session-ipc.ts). Add one injected message in the mutable suffix at the warning threshold ('history will be trimmed soon; save durable facts with clawcode_memory_edit or memory_save now'), as Letta's MESSAGE_SUMMARY_WARNING_STR does. Cost: a few lines in the turn dispatcher plus a cooldown so it fires once per crossing; must not touch the cached stable prefix. The recall tier already exists (memory_lookup scope=conversations over session_archive FTS5), so keep it. Gap: ClawCode currently has no agent-facing warning at all; compaction happens to the agent without a chance to save. Sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.08560, https://arxiv.org/html/2310.08560v2, https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.08560
- **Letta V1 memory blocks, archival and recall memory, self-editing tools and compaction (2025)**: Letta's productised MemGPT: in-context memory is split into labeled, size-limited, described 'memory blocks' the agent edits with precise and whole-block tools; out-of-context memory is recall (message history search) and archival (vector-indexed passages); the server compacts history with a sliding-window summarizer and can hand memory management to a background sleep-time agent. Memory model: Blocks: fields label, description, value, limit (chars), read_only; rendered into the system prompt inside <memory_blocks> with chars_current/chars_limit metadata; defaults persona and human; blocks are DB rows with block_id so they can be shared across agents (last write wins). Letta's source (tag 0.16.8) sets CORE_MEMORY_PERSONA_CHAR_LIMIT and HUMAN 20,000, BLOCK 100,000; the docs recommend blocks under about 50k chars and under about 20 per agent. Write tools (letta/functions/function_sets/base.py at 0.16.8): core_memory_append / core_memory_replace (legacy), memory_insert (line insert), memory_replace (exact-string, 'do NOT replace long strings'), memory_rethink (whole-block rewrite for condensing or reorganising), memory_apply_patch (unified diff, multi-block), memory_finish_edits, plus an Anthropic-style 'memory' omni-tool (create / str_replace / insert / delete / rename on block paths, added Sep 30 2025). Archival: archival_memory_insert(content, tags) and archival_memory_search(query, tags, tag_match_mode, top_k, start_datetime, end_datetime), passages about 300 tokens, no practical size limit, RETRIEVAL_QUERY_DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE 5. Recall: conversation_search(query, roles, limit, start_date, end_date) hybrid text + semantic. Compaction (docs.letta.com/v1-sdk/messages/compaction): triggers when history no longer fits (SUMMARIZATION_TRIGGER_MULTIPLIER 0.9 of context window in source); defaults mode sliding_window, sliding_window_percentage 0.3 (summarise about 30 percent, keep 70), clip_chars 50,000, summarizer model claude-haiku-4-5 for Anthropic; modes all / sliding_window / self_compact_* (include system prompt and tools for cache hits); if the budget is not met the summarised fraction grows in roughly 10 percent steps. A pre-eviction system alert (MESSAGE_SUMMARY_WARNING_STR) tells the agent to save with core_memory_append/replace or archival_memory_insert. The Oct 2025 V1 agent loop removed heartbeats and the send_message tool (assistant text is a direct generation) so that control flow stays in-distribution for modern models. Letta deprecated this whole block-and-tool layer in March 2026 in favour of MemFS (see below). Applicable to ClawCode: 1) Split MEMORY.md into labeled blocks with per-block char limits and descriptions, and render chars_current/chars_limit so the agent can see pressure; today MEMORY.md is one 16,000-char blob (INJECTED_MEMORY_MAX_CHARS) that is head-tail truncated 70/20 with the middle silently dropped from the prompt (src/manager/session-config.ts). Cost: a block parser (H2 sections with a limit in the heading or frontmatter) in session-config plus the edit tool; moderate. 2) Add memory_rethink and memory_insert(line) semantics to clawcode_memory_edit (it has only view/create/append/str_replace) and give the dream pass replacement text: today edit/merge candidates carry no new text and are downgraded to appends (src/manager/dream-promote.ts). 3) Add read_only for the autoload file; see the vault issue below. 4) Keep archival tags: ClawCode memories already carry tags and clawcode_memory_search has includeTags/excludeTags, but includeTags silently drops all file-chunk hits because chunks carry no tags (src/memory/tools/clawcode-memory-search.ts). 5) Add an 'update/contradiction' test like the leaderboard's: ClawCode's insert dedup overwrites an existing memory's content when cosine >= 0.85 (src/memory/dedup.ts mergeMemory), which can silently clobber a distinct but similar fact and never records that a fact changed. Existing ClawCode strength to keep: the Anthropic memory_20250818 contract on files already matches Letta's omni-tool direction. Sources: https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/memory-blocks, https://docs.letta.com/v1-sdk/memory/context-hierarchy/, https://docs.letta.com/guides/agents/archival-memory, https://docs.letta.com/v1-sdk/messages/compaction/, https://docs.letta.com/guides/core-concepts/memory/shared-memory/, https://www.letta.com/blog/memory-blocks
- **Letta sleep-time agents and sleep-time compute (Apr 2025 paper and blog; server-side implementation through 2025, deprecated Mar 2026 in favour of client-side dreaming)**: Moves memory editing off the conversation's hot path: a second 'sleep-time' agent that shares the primary agent's memory blocks reviews recent conversation in the background and rewrites the blocks, turning 'raw context' into 'learned context' so the primary agent needs less reasoning at answer time. Memory model: Enabling enable_sleeptime creates a group with a primary agent and a sleep-time agent that share block ids. Tool split in source (constants.py at 0.16.8): primary gets BASE_SLEEPTIME_CHAT_TOOLS = send_message, conversation_search, archival_memory_search (no block-editing tools); sleep-time agent gets BASE_SLEEPTIME_TOOLS = memory_replace, memory_insert, memory_rethink, memory_finish_edits. Trigger (letta/groups/sleeptime_multi_agent_v4.py): after each primary step a turns counter is bumped; if sleeptime_agent_frequency is None the sleep-time agent runs after every turn, else when counter mod frequency == 0; it receives, as a single user message with a system-reminder ('you are NOT the primary agent... your primary role is memory management'), all primary-agent messages since last_processed_message_id plus the latest response, and runs as a background task. The sleep-time system prompt (sleeptime_v2.py) instructs: use precise tools for narrow edits and rethink for whole-block reorganisation, continue until blocks are organised with no redundant or outdated information then call finish, skip edits when nothing meaningful changed, be selective but aim for high recall, and write absolute dates never 'today' or 'recently'. Models can differ (blog example: fast model for chat, larger model for sleep time). The same agent can pre-read uploaded documents into the primary's blocks. Letta's docs warn concurrent memory_rethink on one block loses updates and recommend a single owner (the sleep-time agent) for rewrites with others using memory_insert. Applicable to ClawCode: ClawCode's dream pass (src/manager/dream-pass.ts, dream-prompt-builder.ts, dream-auto-apply.ts) is the analogue but differs in four ways worth closing: (a) it is off by default (defaults.dream.enabled false) and fires only after 30 idle minutes via cron, and the cron was once unwired for 5+ days in production (comment in dream-machinery.ts); Letta runs after every turn or every N turns. Add a turn-count trigger (every N dispatched turns per agent) and a compaction-event trigger. (b) It returns JSON suggestions (wikilinks, promotion candidates with scores, consolidations) rather than editing memory with tools; only additive candidates scoring >= 80 are appended after a 30-minute Discord veto, and rewrites are operator-only. Replace with a sleep-time agent that holds the same edit tools (str_replace / insert / rethink on MEMORY.md sections) and the sleep-time prompt rules (absolute dates, finish when nothing changed, selective but high recall), writing to a working copy and keeping the digest/veto for review. (c) Enforce the single-owner rule: let the sleep-time pass own rethink/rewrites and keep the primary agent to append/str_replace, which also removes the race between dream promotion and clawcode_memory_edit. (d) Feed it the same inputs Letta does: the turns since the last pass, not only chunks and summaries. Cost: one haiku/sonnet call every N turns per agent (Letta's default is every turn; N = 10 to 25 is cheaper), plus a lock or copy-on-write for MEMORY.md. Sources: https://letta.com/blog/sleep-time-compute, https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13171, https://arxiv.org/html/2504.13171, https://raw.githubusercontent.com/letta-ai/letta/0.16.8/letta/constants.py, https://raw.githubusercontent.com/letta-ai/letta/0.16.8/letta/groups/sleeptime_multi_agent_v4.py, https://raw.githubusercontent.com/letta-ai/letta/0.16.8/letta/prompts/system_prompts/sleeptime_v2.py
- **Letta Code MemFS / context repositories with dreaming, memory doctor and shared memory (Feb to Oct 2026)**: Letta's current design: each agent owns a git repository of markdown memory files projected onto the agent's machine; root files are compiled into the system prompt every turn, deeper directories load on demand, every edit is a commit, and background subagents (dreaming, memory doctor, defragmentation) edit memory in git worktrees and merge back. Specialized memory tools were dropped in favour of ordinary file and bash tools. Memory model: Storage: $MEMORY_DIR git repo; each memory is a markdown file named by label with YAML frontmatter (name, description); root MEMORY.md is an index listing subdirectories that have their own MEMORY.md (older agents use system/); skills live in $MEMORY_DIR/skills; 'All Letta agents use MemFS' and MemFS became mandatory in 0.27.21. Reads: root files (or system/) are in the system prompt every turn; the file tree is always in the prompt; everything else is opened on demand with file-search and read tools; no vector index by default (optional memfs-search mod: keyword, or semantic/hybrid via QMD); conversation search is separate (full-text, vector or hybrid on cloud, full-text only locally). Writes: the agent or a subagent edits files with normal tools and commits with informative messages; edits become memory once committed and pushed; cloud repos sync across machines; local-only agents must back up themselves. Dreaming: Agent SDK option dreaming { trigger: off | step-count | compaction-event, behavior: reminder | auto-launch, stepCount (example 25, default not stated) }; optional 'agent reviews before applying' second pass; reflection runs in a worktree and merges back; /dream --to syncs a doc into system/ before reflecting (0.27.28). Maintenance: /init bootstraps memory by inspecting the repo and prior sessions with parallel subagents; /doctor audits placement, duplication and system-prompt token usage; defragmentation backs up the repo then splits large files, merges duplicates and restructures to 15 to 25 focused files; 0.32.0 added default MemFS v2 budget enforcement and 0.31.8 a default memory constraints policy (numbers not published). Limits: 5 MiB per memory repo on Free, 50 MiB on Pro, counted on the latest snapshot not history. Sharing: shared memory repositories (cloud) attached to several agents appear in the prompt as a file tree, agents commit and push, others fast-forward; Conversations (Jan 2026) let one agent run many concurrent conversations (for example one per Slack thread) with memory shared and message history per conversation. Mods (Jun 2026) let the agent also change the harness (context assembly, compaction prompt) via hot-reloaded extensions. Applicable to ClawCode: 1) Put each agent's Tier 1 dir (~/.clawcode/agents/<name>/) under git and commit after every clawcode_memory_edit, archive, dream promotion and consolidation write; cheap (git is present, one exec per write) and gives rollback, blame and a diff to show in the weekly digest; today str_replace on MEMORY.md is irreversible and the only safety net is the 30-minute Discord veto on dream promotions. 2) Run the dream/sleep-time pass in a git worktree and merge, which also solves the concurrent-writer problem above. 3) Adopt progressive disclosure: root MEMORY.md as an index plus per-topic files loaded on demand; the user's vault already has exactly this shape (MEMORY.md index under 180 lines, hubs per project), so the fix is wiring rather than design. 4) Add a 'memory doctor' CLI command that reports duplicates (dedup already has the vectors), files over cap, and stable-prefix token usage; small. 5) Add compaction-event and step-count dream triggers (see sleep-time entry). 6) Letta's conclusion that grep-style file tools beat specialized memory tools argues for exposing the memory dir and vault to the sandboxed agent as files, not only via MCP. Gaps found in the current deployment: (a) clawcode.yaml sets memoryAutoLoadPath: ~/Knowledgebase/MEMORY.md on all four agents; resolveMemoryRoots (src/memory/memory-roots.ts) then makes ~/Knowledgebase the Tier 1 directory, so clawcode_memory_edit, clawcode_memory_archive and dream promotion would append to the shared vault index (which the vault rules say must stay under 180 lines and only hold hubs and hard rules) and USER.md would be created at ~/Knowledgebase/USER.md, written by four agents with no lock. The daemon-side IPC handlers bypass the bwrap/fileAccess sandbox, so fileAccess does not protect it. Fix: per-agent memoryAutoLoadPath copies, or a read-only autoload flag plus a separate writable Tier 1. (b) The scanner only indexes <workspace>/memory/** (src/memory/memory-scanner.ts), so the 301 vault notes are invisible to FTS5 and sqlite-vec and to clawcode_memory_search; set memoryPath or add ~/Knowledgebase as an extra scanner root (cost: one-time embedding of about 5 MB of markdown with bge-small, seconds to minutes). Sources: https://docs.letta.com/concepts/memfs, https://docs.letta.com/configuration/memory, https://docs.letta.com/agent-sdk/memory, https://docs.letta.com/concepts/shared-memory, https://docs.letta.com/letta-code/memfs, https://docs.letta.com/letta-code/memory
- **Letta filesystem-memory agent (LoCoMo experiment, Aug 2025) and Letta Evals (Oct 2025)**: An experiment showing that an agent given only file tools over the raw conversation history scores well on the LoCoMo long-conversation QA benchmark, used by Letta to argue that tool-use ability and iterative search matter more than specialized memory stores; plus Letta's evaluation framework for stateful agents. Memory model: The LoCoMo history was uploaded as one file and attached to a Letta agent (Letta parses and embeds attached files); tools were grep, search_files, open, close and answer_question; tool rules forced the agent to start with search_files and keep searching until answer_question; the agent chose its own queries and number of calls; model GPT-4o mini; minimal prompt tuning. Letta Evals: JSONL datasets, Agent File (.af) targets, graders (exact match, LLM rubric, agent-as-grader), gates as CI quality bars; recommends evaluating both fresh and long-lived agents. Applicable to ClawCode: ClawCode has an in-repo recall eval (src/memory/__tests__/recall-eval-999187.test.ts) that reports recall@5 = 1.0 and MRR 0.39 (pre-turn) / 0.35 (lazy) on 11 and 15 synthetic queries, which is a start but tests retrieval only. Copy the leaderboard task shapes (write-then-clear-history, update-after-contradiction, penalty for extraneous memory ops) as vitest cases driven by a fake LLM, and run them against both a fresh agent store and a long-lived store snapshot. Cost: test fixtures only. Also note the test suite state: npx vitest run src/memory gives 842 passed, 2 failed (conversation-brief.test.ts gap-skip cases seed zero-turn sessions while listRecentTerminatedSessions now requires a turn row; stale tests, not a runtime bug), and the README admits the full suite is not green. Sources: https://www.letta.com/blog/benchmarking-ai-agent-memory, https://signals.aktagon.com/articles/2026/02/benchmarking-ai-agent-memory-is-a-filesystem-all-you-need/, https://braindetox.kr/en/posts/memory_benchmark_scores_reliability_2026.html, https://www.letta.com/blog/letta-evals

#### Mem0 and Zep/Graphiti: extraction-then-update memory, graph memory, bi-temporal knowledge graphs, and the LoCoMo/LongMemEval/DMR results and disputes (as of 2026-10-25)

- Keep the write path cheap and non-destructive; do reconciliation (merge, supersede, summarize) in a separate, later pass. Evidence: Mem0's 2025 pipeline used two LLM calls per message pair (extract, then ADD/UPDATE/DELETE/NOOP against the top 10 similar memories). Its 2026 rewrite uses one ADD-only call with hash dedupe, reports about half the extraction latency, and moved LoCoMo from 71.4 to 91.6 and LongMemEval from 67.8 to 93.4 (vendor-reported, new judge setup, so the causal share of this one change is unknown). Graphiti keeps expensive work (communities) optional and periodic. This maps onto ClawCode's dreaming. Source: https://docs.mem0.ai/migration/oss-v2-to-v3
- Invalidate, do not delete: a superseded fact stays queryable with its validity window, and the default query filters it out. Evidence: Graphiti README: 'When information changes, old facts are invalidated, not deleted'; code sets old.invalid_at = new.valid_at and old.expired_at = now. Mem0g also marks obsolete edges invalid instead of deleting them. Commenters on Mem0 issue #4956 argue the old facts are the only record of what was believed at the time. Source: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/getzep/graphiti/main/graphiti_core/utils/maintenance/edge_operations.py
- ADD-only storage is not enough: pair it with structural supersession or a recency signal, or stale facts outrank current ones. Evidence: Mem0 issue #4956 (open, April to October 2026): after ADD-only v3, 'I work at Company A' and 'I now work at Company B' both remain and scoring has no temporal term; duplicates act as votes for the stale value. Mem0 then added temporal metadata with event_end for ongoing states and search-time decay (1.5x boost to 0.3x damping) on the platform only; Zep's paper shows knowledge-update at 83.3% (gpt-4o) with bi-temporal edges versus 78.2% full-context. Source: https://github.com/mem0ai/mem0/issues/4956
- Track two times: when a fact was true (valid time) and when the system learned or retired it (transaction time), and resolve relative dates against the source's timestamp, never the current clock. Evidence: Graphiti edges carry valid_at/invalid_at (timeline T) and created_at/expired_at (timeline T'), and an LLM resolves 'last Tuesday' against the episode reference time. Mem0 issue #3944 reproduced a roughly 0.20 LoCoMo score because the platform stamped events with January 2026 instead of the dataset's 7 May 2023; Zep's rebuttal says Mem0 broke Zep's temporal reasoning by appending timestamps to message text instead of using created_at. Source: https://github.com/mem0ai/mem0/issues/3944
- Keep raw episodes as a lossless layer and link every derived memory back to its sources. Evidence: Graphiti stores episodic nodes verbatim and every entity edge has an episodes list for provenance; the paper lists episode-to-edge traceability as future work for richer use. Mem0's v3 prompt emits linked_memory_ids to existing memories. Raw retention lets extraction be re-run and covers the async gap where searches right after ingestion fail (Mem0 paper's observation about Zep). Source: https://github.com/getzep/graphiti
- Retrieve with several cheap signals fused together: vector similarity, BM25, and entity or graph proximity, with BM25 and entity used as re-ranking boosts over semantic candidates. Evidence: Mem0 v3 docs: semantic, BM25 and entity overlap run in parallel and 'BM25 is a boost signal, not a recall expander'. Graphiti search combines cosine, Okapi BM25 and BFS with RRF, MMR, episode-mentions, node-distance or cross-encoder reranking. ClawCode already has the two storage primitives (FTS5 and sqlite-vec). Source: https://docs.mem0.ai/migration/oss-v2-to-v3
- Budget the injected context and report accuracy together with tokens and latency; the memory layer's main proven win over full-context is cost, not accuracy. Evidence: Mem0 2025: 1,764 tokens and 1.44s total p95 versus 26,031 tokens and 17.1s for full-context, but full-context scored higher (72.90 vs 66.88 J). Zep 2025: 1.6k tokens versus 115k and 3.2s versus 31.3s on LongMemEval-S, with accuracy 63.8 versus 55.4 (gpt-4o-mini), where full-context genuinely degrades. Mem0 2026 about 6.8k to 7k tokens per query; Zep 2026 4.4k to 5.8k. Source: https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413
- Memory benchmark scores mostly measure the harness (answer model, judge model, judge prompt, category handling), so run your own fixed-harness eval with baselines and repeats. Evidence: Same product, 65.99 to 94.7 on LoCoMo for Zep depending on who ran it and with which models; Penfield audit: 6.4% of the answer key is wrong and the gpt-4o-mini judge accepted 62.81% of deliberately wrong answers (interested-party source); a secondary source reports a 26-point swing from changing only the grading prompt. Mem0's own page now calls LoCoMo a baseline, 'less so' a deciding benchmark. Prefer long benchmarks with knowledge-update and temporal categories (LongMemEval, about 115k tokens in S). Source: https://mem0.ai/library/agent-memory/how-to-read-memory-benchmarks
- Restrict candidate search for deduplication to the same subject (same entity pair) and use a wider search only for contradiction detection. Evidence: In Graphiti's resolve_extracted_edges, duplicate candidates come from a hybrid search limited to edges between the same two nodes, while invalidation candidates come from an unfiltered search minus those; the paper says this reduces both false merges and compute. Mem0's equivalent is the top-s=10 similar memories per fact. Source: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/getzep/graphiti/main/graphiti_core/utils/maintenance/edge_operations.py
- A typed graph database is not what delivers the gains at single-agent scale; the temporal metadata and entity linking are. Evidence: Mem0g added 1.56 J points overall for about 2x tokens and over 3x p95 search latency, and lost on single-hop and multi-hop; Mem0 later removed graph stores (about 4,000 lines) from OSS and replaced them with untyped entity linking as a retrieval boost. Graphiti needs Neo4j, FalkorDB or Neptune; its embedded option Kuzu was archived upstream in October 2025 and is marked deprecated in the README. Source: https://docs.mem0.ai/migration/oss-v2-to-v3
- Extract from the assistant's turns as well as the user's. Evidence: Zep lost to full-context on single-session-assistant questions (94.6 to 80.4 with gpt-4o, a 17.7% drop; 81.8 to 75.0 with gpt-4o-mini). Mem0's 2026 release states agent-generated facts are now stored with equal weight to user-stated facts, and older Mem0 prompts offered a user-messages-only variant. Source: https://arxiv.org/html/2501.13956
- Forgetting should be a ranking effect (recency and access boosts and damping, expiry that hides but does not delete), not an LLM-decided deletion. Evidence: Mem0 May 2026: recent use boosts up to 1.5x, stale memories dampened toward 0.3x, search-time only, 'nothing is deleted'; expiration_date hides memories from search while get-by-id still returns them. Graphiti has no deletion path at all by design. Source: https://mem0.ai/blog/the-token-efficient-memory-algorithm-now-has-temporal-reasoning
- Periodic rebuilds beat per-write maintenance for clustering and community summaries. Evidence: Zep paper: dynamic community updates (a new entity joins its neighbors' plurality community) drift from a full label-propagation run, so periodic refreshes are needed; Graphiti docs say each build_communities() call removes and recreates communities, and a third-party performance guide advises leaving update_communities off per episode because it adds latency. No ablation of community benefit appears in the paper I read. Source: https://help.getzep.com/graphiti/core-concepts/communities

Anti-patterns: Taking vendor LoCoMo or LongMemEval numbers at face value or comparing across vendors: answerer model, judge model, judge prompt, and category handling differ (Zep 65.99 vs 75.14 vs 94.7; Mem0 66.88 vs 92.5). Counting the adversarial category in the numerator but not the denominator was alleged in the 2025 dispute.; Letting an LLM overwrite or delete stored facts on every write (Mem0's original UPDATE/DELETE on the hot path): costs a second LLM call per message and risks irreversible information loss; Mem0 itself removed it from the OSS SDK in 2026.; ADD-only storage with no supersession and no recency term: contradictory facts accumulate and stale ones can rank first (Mem0 issue #4956, open); duplicates act as votes for the old value.; Stamping memories with the current time instead of the source message or document time (Mem0 issue #3944 produced about 0.20 on LoCoMo), or pasting timestamps into message text instead of a dedicated timestamp field (Zep's complaint about Mem0's run).; Extracting facts only from user turns for an agent that acts: Zep's single-session-assistant accuracy fell below full-context (down to 80.4 from 94.6 on gpt-4o).; Adding a graph database server for a single-agent store: Mem0g gained about 1.6 J points for roughly 2x tokens and 3x tail search latency, lost on single-hop and multi-hop, and Mem0 later dropped OSS graph support; Graphiti's embedded backend (Kuzu) is archived upstream.; Running full community detection or summarization on every write: adds ingestion latency and the incremental result still drifts from a full recompute.; Assuming memory is searchable immediately after asynchronous ingestion: Mem0's paper saw Zep searches right after ingest fail and succeed hours later; keep raw recent turns searchable until extraction catches up.; Evaluating on corpora that fit in the context window (DMR about 60 messages; LoCoMo about 16k to 26k tokens): full-context scores 94.4 to 98.0 on DMR and 72.90 on LoCoMo, so such tests cannot show a memory benefit beyond cost; and they under-test knowledge updates.; Trusting an LLM judge without calibration: the LoCoMo judge reportedly accepts 62.81% of intentionally wrong topically adjacent answers (Penfield, interested party), and grading prompt changes alone are reported to move scores by tens of points.; Publishing platform-only scores as if the open-source code reproduces them: Mem0 says its managed platform has proprietary optimizations and OSS users should expect 'directionally similar' but not identical results; temporal reasoning and memory decay are platform-only features.; Relying on third-party aggregator figures without tracing them: I could not trace a '49.0% independent LongMemEval score for Mem0' to a primary source (it appears to be a ConvoMem number misattributed), so it is not used here; Mem0's LoCoMo question count is also stated inconsistently across its own docs.

Systems reviewed:

- **Mem0 (original 2025 pipeline: extract, then ADD/UPDATE/DELETE/NOOP)**: Memory layer that turns chat turns into short natural-language facts stored in a vector DB and keeps them reconciled against what is already stored. Paper: arXiv 2504.19413, v1 submitted 28 April 2025 (Mem0's own pages later cite it as an ECAI 2025 paper). Headline claims: 26% relative gain in LLM-as-judge score over OpenAI memory, 91% lower p95 latency and over 90% token savings versus full-context. This is the pipeline that the 2026 'v3' algorithm replaced in the open-source SDK (see the Mem0 v3 entry). Memory model: WRITE, extraction: triggered per new message pair. An LLM (GPT-4o-mini in the paper) receives (a) a conversation summary S that is refreshed asynchronously so it adds no latency, (b) the last m=10 messages, (c) the new pair, and emits candidate facts. WRITE, update: for each candidate, retrieve the top s=10 semantically similar existing memories by embedding, then give the fact plus those memories to the LLM through a function/tool call that picks one of four operations: ADD (nothing equivalent exists), UPDATE (augment an existing memory; Algorithm 1 only replaces when the new fact carries more information), DELETE (existing memory contradicted by new info), NOOP. No separate classifier. READ: embed the query and return top-k memories from the vector store. FORGETTING: only via DELETE; no decay or tiers. In the repo prompts (mem0/configs/prompts.py) the update prompt tells the model to keep the same ID on UPDATE and record old_memory; the default fact-extraction prompt variants can be restricted to user messages only. Footprint reported in the paper: about 7k tokens of stored memory per conversation versus about 26k raw. Applicable to ClawCode: The extract-then-reconcile loop maps directly onto ClawCode's periodic 'dreaming'. Concrete copy: in dreaming, for each newly extracted memory run sqlite-vec KNN (top 10, same as Mem0's s=10) plus an FTS5 query, hand the candidates to one LLM call that returns ADD / UPDATE / SUPERSEDE / NOOP, and implement DELETE as soft supersession (set superseded_at and superseded_by) rather than removing the row, so a wrong LLM call is recoverable. Cost: one LLM call per new fact (batchable per cluster), extra columns, and a prompt to maintain. Do not run this on the hot path: Mem0's own authors moved off it (next entries). Also copy the async rolling conversation summary as extraction context: it adds no write latency and ClawCode already has Discord threads and sessions to summarize. Also note that the default Mem0 extraction prompts focus on user messages; ClawCode agents produce decisions and results in their own turns, so extract from agent turns too. Sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.19413, https://arxiv.org/html/2504.19413, https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mem0ai/mem0/main/mem0/configs/prompts.py
